# Checklist Choices: Cryptographic Architecture and phyco-v3 Design

> This feature makes no security claim. No PhycoWeb-PRNG model, `phyco-v3` included, may be used
> to generate secrets; use an established cryptographic random-number generator for keys,
> nonces, tokens and salts. Passing statistical tests does not show cryptographic security.

## How to answer

- The first three rounds were answered "all recommended" on 2026-11-01, and the third re-run's 70
  rows on 2026-11-02. The fourth re-run's 36 rows were answered in advance on 2026-11-02: "you have
  my approval of all suggestions to continue without human interaction", which takes the recommended
  option of every row. The fifth re-run's 11 rows were answered in advance by the same approval,
  which takes the recommended option of every row of every round of the loop. The sixth re-run's 22
  rows were answered in advance by the same approval. The seventh re-run's 37 rows were answered in
  advance by the same approval. The eighth re-run's 25 rows were answered in advance by the same
  approval. The ninth re-run's 15 rows were answered in advance by the same approval. The tenth
  re-run's 28 rows were answered in advance by the same approval. An answer could also have given
  letters by ID, for example "SEC-096 B, REP-100 B, the rest recommended", or an option in the
  owner's own words.
- After each answer, the changes each chosen option names are applied with their reasons. The six
  checklists are then re-read against the changed text, and the reviewer ticks an item once the text
  it asks for is in place.
- IDs: `SEC`, `RAT`, `ANA`, `PRO`, `HW` and `REP` stand for the checklists
  [security.md](./checklists/security.md),
  [design-rationale.md](./checklists/design-rationale.md),
  [analysis.md](./checklists/analysis.md), [protocol.md](./checklists/protocol.md),
  [hardware.md](./checklists/hardware.md) and
  [reproducibility.md](./checklists/reproducibility.md). `SEC-010` is CHK010 of the security
  checklist.
- The first word of each recommendation says what kind of change it is:

  | Word | Meaning |
  |------|---------|
  | Clarify | States what the documents already mean; no rule changes |
  | Align | Removes a disagreement between two texts, keeping the stricter or more careful one |
  | Add | A new requirement, record, test or check outside the protocol's decision rules |
  | Stricter | Tightens a rule of the pre-registered protocol in the direction FR-044 allows; the row names the direction |
  | Settle | Fixes a case the protocol leaves open, by its most conservative reading, before any result it affects (PRO-027) |

- From plan approval the protocol may only become stricter (FR-044), and no result exists yet.
  Fifteen recommended options set aside one reading each of the old text, under which it fails

  change a check only at the research sizes, where as written it fails every candidate; ANA-062 and

## Conflicts

The 74 items marked `[Conflict]`. Each one names two texts that cannot both stand as written.

From the tenth re-run of 2026-11-03, answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the recommended options,
and applied, in checklist order:

Which bound does a research row state, and is s a field of it? | Settle: a research row's bound is its `bound_bits`, as FR-009, FR-010 and data-model §10 give it, at the variant's own k, nonce bits and c, the multi-target term at the u that `limits.json` states, as at every row, and a term below zero counting as zero, so that it is never above c/2, one of its terms; s = c/2 is the level at which research R14 and data-model §10 make FR-010's checks for that variant, not a field of the row and not its bound. | PRO-079 | FR-044 allows a change only in a rule's stricter direction or as a settling "never looser than any reading of the existing text", with no qualifier of size, while "How to answer" says that RAT-036 and RAT-052 change a check at the research sizes, "where as written it fails every candidate", and ANA-062 and ANA-068 the round counts and bits of the avalanche test's validation, under which, read with every round count from 1 and no bit exempt, "it fails every correct test", the Settle rows PRO-065, PRO-068 and PRO-071 admit writes that the guards "Protocol log" and "Environment" refused as written, the Review & Approval rows of the third to ninth re-runs qualify the claim by size, and the re-run's row, which answered RAT-036, calls every change stricter or a conservative settling. How does a first entry record a rule changed against such a reading, so that FR-044 and the rows' claim agree? | Stricter (an entry's fields and the guard's refusals only added, FR-044): data-model §18's `readings` give each reading of the old text that the new text sets aside, admitting what that reading refuses, the mark `set_aside`, with the case under which the old text fails, every candidate, every correct tool or a write the protocol needs; the first entries of RAT-036, RAT-052, ANA-062, ANA-068, PRO-065, PRO-068 and PRO-071 carry one, as do those of SEC-093, RAT-086, ANA-087, PRO-076, REP-100 and REP-101, and every other entry the agent finds of that kind. `protocol_change.py create` exits 2 while a first entry holds such a reading that the owner has not decided, entry by entry, in the Review & Approval row "Readings set aside", set at the tasks approval: the change kept, with the owner's reason, as a change FR-044's words do not allow, or the old text restored; a later entry holding one shows it at `propose`'s approval step and records the owner's reason. FR-044 keeps its words; the notes of the rows "Checklist choices" of the re-run and of the third to ninth re-runs and "How to answer" name those rows as setting aside one reading each instead of qualifying the claim by size or calling every change stricter, and this round's Clarification records the correction, the earlier Clarifications keeping each answer as given. | B: FR-044 reads "never looser than any reading under which a correct candidate, tool or needed write can meet the rule", so that those readings are not readings, and the rows keep their claims (looser). C: unchanged: FR-044's words forbid what the seven rows did, while the rows' claims are qualified by a size FR-044 does not name or call those changes stricter. | spec FR-044, Clarifications, Review & Approval (the rows "Checklist choices" of the re-run and of the third to ninth re-runs, a row "Readings set aside" and the notes under the table); data-model §18 `readings`; cli contract `protocol_change.py`, "Protocol log"; checklist-choices.md "How to answer" |
| REP-100 | A reproduction runs "in the environment that made the original, as its environment record shows, and in no other" (research R1; data-model §1; spec FR-053; cli contract introduction), a difference in a field that enters a verdict being a tool defect, while every write through `protocol/evidence.py`, a reproduction record's included, exits 2 unless the running environment equals the platform's record in force as a whole, so that after any update, a Windows update included, the record that made an earlier original is no longer in force, FR-061's test reproduces per kind its latest light run and, where one exists, one made before the last change to its code, and `validate_tool.py` has a later draw reproduce every hash "in an environment with that numpy version". Is the environment that made the original the platform or the record it cites, and what does a reproduction of a record made before the latest change do? | Settle (no reproduction runs in another environment): the environment that made the original is the environment record it cites; `--reproduce` exits 2, writing nothing, with the reason "environment record <id> is not in force", while that record is not the platform's record in force, so that no difference is ever charged to a tool that a changed environment could explain; a record made under an earlier record stays the evidence of its environment, as `validate_tool.py`'s old validation does, and FR-061's test takes per kind the latest light run made under the record in force and, where one exists under it, one made before the last change to its code, and lists each kind whose run before that change was made under an earlier record, with that record. The protocol change log's entry marks as `set_aside` the reading under which the test reproduces such a run and fails after every environment change until the kind's code next changes (PRO-079). | B: a reproduction runs under the record in force, the fields that depend on a changed version reported and not compared, a difference there being no tool defect (looser). C: unchanged: R1 and data-model §1 send a reproduction to the original's environment, which after an update the guard no longer admits. | spec FR-053, FR-061; research R1, R16; data-model §1; cli contract introduction, "Environment", `validate_tool.py` |

From the ninth re-run of 2026-11-02, answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the recommended options,
and applied, in checklist order:

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| RAT-085 | The cli contract has `mode_limits_v3.py` evaluate the theorem's bound "at the limits stated in the design document" and write each row of `parameters.json` "from the variant table and the design document's layout", which the chapters "Mode" and "Permutation" hold as written text, while data-model §1's table reports `design_sha256` only for the size profile and limits, a field "compared only for a kind whose code reads the design document", and the chapter "Security target" holds per size a parameter table and the output limits (User Story 2, scenario 2) that one security-target review reads beside `parameters.json` and `limits.json`. In what form does the script read the limits, the variant table and the layout, is its `design_sha256` compared, and is the chapter's table taken from the files or checked against them? | Align: `mode_limits_v3.py` reads three tables of the design document, each a Markdown table with fixed columns in a section of its own, which the chapters' test checks: the chapter "Permutation"'s section "Variant table", one row per variant with its ID, n, w, b, c, r, R and status, and the chapter "Mode"'s sections "Layout", which lanes form the rate part and which the capacity part at each size, and "Limits", each limit in calls of Π and in bits at every claimable size, with u and the two ways of choosing nonces; it exits 2 if a table is missing, appears twice or holds a row it cannot read; data-model §1's table gives the size profile and limits a row of their own that compares `design_sha256`, since their code reads the design document, as the constant tables' row does; and the chapter "Security target"'s parameter table and output limits restate `parameters.json` and `limits.json`, a test comparing each with its file row by row and value by value, the key entropy, the reachable secret state, c, r, R and the bound among them, and failing on any difference, so that the security-target review reads one set of values; FR-010, FR-034 "Chapters", User Story 2, data-model §1 and §10 and the cli contract's `mode_limits_v3.py` say so | B: `mode_limits_v3.py` reads the limits, the layout and the variant table from arguments of its command, each a JSON file written by hand under `research/004/` and held by path and SHA-256 in the run's `config`, the design document restating them and a test comparing the two, `design_sha256` staying reported only. C: unchanged: the table reports `design_sha256` for a kind whose code reads the design document, the form of what the script reads is unstated, and the chapter's table and the files can differ | spec FR-010, FR-034 "Chapters", User Story 2; data-model §1, §10; cli contract `mode_limits_v3.py` |
| ANA-082 | Research R6 fixes the D4 width as the widest of 16 and 8 "at which at least one entered candidate of each family has both a differential and a linear trail bound for a common number of rounds of at least 2", r* as "the largest such common number", and scores "Only candidates with both bounds at r* rounds", so that at a width that qualifies each family has a scored candidate, while R6 also has, under "One family scored", "That family is chosen by default", and data-model §8 lists `chosen by default` among the outcomes. Can that outcome arise, which outcome does the record take when at 16 and at 8 only one family has a candidate with both bounds at a common round count of at least 2, at which width and r* are that family's candidates then scored, and does "common" mean common to one candidate's two bounds or to the candidates of both families? | Settle: "common" means common to the two bounds of each scored candidate and to the scored candidates of both families: r* is the largest number of rounds, at least 2, at which at least one entered candidate of each family has both bounds, so that at a width where both families qualify each has a scored candidate and r* never exceeds every round count the other family reaches; where at 16 and at 8 only one family has a candidate with both bounds at some number of rounds of at least 2, that family is chosen by default, as research R6's "One family scored" and data-model §8's `chosen by default` state, the width being the widest at which it has one, r* the largest such number there and its candidates with both bounds at r* scored, the highest being its representative, with the other family's outcomes listed; this reading is the conservative one, since the other family has no reduced-width evidence the choice could weigh; where neither family has one, D4 is not computable, as now; the protocol's "Nonlinearity choice rule" states the two cases, research R6, data-model §8 and the cli contract's `d4_choice.py` say so, and its tests cover the outcomes `chosen`, `chosen by default` and `not computable` | B: where only one family has such a candidate, D4 is not computable, as the width rule read alone gives, both families going on to the production-width analysis and selection taking the eligible candidate with the smallest R at 4×4, and R6's "One family scored" and data-model §8's `chosen by default` are removed (looser). C: unchanged: the width rule leaves `chosen by default` unreachable while R6 and data-model §8 list it, and "common" has two readings | spec "Nonlinearity choice rule"; research R6; data-model §8; cli contract `d4_choice.py` |
| REP-097 | FR-059 has k "stated per claimable size in the design document" as the least number of whole blocks for which the model's run meets each structure's taking part, "unless the design document names a structure that needs more, with its reason", and the self-test's vector file "holds k with the structure that set it, which a test computes again from the model's run", while `gen_vectors_v3.py`'s synopsis takes no k, data-model §1's table reports `design_sha256` for it only, comparing it for `gen_constants_v3.py` "since its restatement check reads the design document", and each candidate's `cost` record takes FR-020's self-test formula and FR-026's storage before selection, when neither the design document's k nor the vector file exists. Where does the generator take each claimable size's k from, can the test's k, computed from the model's run, equal a k that a named structure raised, and which k does a `cost` record take? | Align: `gen_vectors_v3.py` reads each claimable size's k from a table of the design document's chapter "Hardware structure", in its section "Self-test", which the chapters' test checks: one row per claimable size with k, the structure that set it and, where k exceeds the least that the model's run gives, the structure the design document names with its reason; it computes that least k from a `ResearchDuplex` run through the self-test's call sequence, as FR-059 defines taking part, exits 1 if the table's k is below it, or above it with no named structure and reason, and writes k, the structure that set it and the run's least k into `selftest_<variant>.json`; the test computes the least k again from the model's run and checks the file's k against it and against the table, equal unless a named structure with its reason raised it; data-model §1's table compares `design_sha256` for `gen_vectors_v3.py`'s runs and checks, since its code reads the design document; and before selection each candidate's `cost` record computes the least k from a run of the candidate through the self-test's call sequence, its operation list interpreted by `research/004/ir.py` in the shared mode, and records it with the structure that set it, for FR-020's self-test formula and FR-026's storage, the design document stating the selected candidate's k equal to it or raised by a named structure with its reason; FR-020, FR-026, FR-034 "Chapters", FR-059, research R8, data-model §1 and §11 and the cli contract's generators say so | B: `gen_vectors_v3.py` computes k from the model's run alone and reads no design document, and the clause "unless the design document names a structure that needs more" is removed from FR-059, research R8 and data-model §11, so that k is always the least the run gives, the design document restating it and a test comparing the two, and a `cost` record takes that least k from the candidate's run (looser). C: unchanged: the generator has no source for a k that a named structure raised, the test's k cannot equal it, and a `cost` record has no k | spec FR-020, FR-026, FR-034 "Chapters", FR-059; research R8; data-model §1, §11; cli contract "Generators of constants and vectors" |

Who commits the register's change in that window, is "file of this feature" defined, so that `protocol/claims.json` and security.md are known to be among the files left to the owner, and where does the window end, given that the Assumptions' "from the owner design review's approval on" names no end? | Align: a file of this feature is every file under the paths the plan's project structure lists and every other file that this feature's requirements change, security.md, README, `.gitignore`, `.gitattributes` and `requirements.txt` among them, so that `protocol/claims.json` and security.md are both files of this feature; the window in which the agent commits none of them runs from the first commit that records an owner design review's approval to the feature's end, plan phase 9 included, as "from the owner design review's approval on" reads, each later commit of such a file being the owner's (PRO-072); inside it the agent writes each change of `phyco-v3`'s entry and of its §15 line that SEC-076 and SEC-079 name in the working tree, once the owner has set the row or made the change there and before the owner's commit, which holds it, so that at every commit of the window the register gives the rows' level, and `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 while `check_claims.py` reports a finding at HEAD; FR-012, FR-043, FR-069, the Assumptions, the notes under the Review & Approval table, plan phase 9, data-model §13 and §15 and the cli contract's `check_claims.py` and `design_freeze.py` say so | B: the window ends at the owner's commit of the approved freeze record, step (4), after which the agent commits again for plan phase 9, and the agent's commits of `protocol/claims.json` and of security.md §15's line are exempt from it, made right after the owner's commit as SEC-076 states (looser). C: unchanged: no commit of the register's change can be made in the window, and the window has no stated end | spec FR-012, FR-043, FR-069, Review & Approval notes under the table, Assumptions; plan "Phases and gates"; data-model §13, §15; cli contract `check_claims.py`, `design_freeze.py` |
| RAT-080 | FR-034 has the design document's header hold "Version: 0.<k>", "1.0 once frozen", with `rationale.md` carrying the same version, while "Each committed change before the freeze makes the next 0.<k>", the agent commits no file of this feature from the owner design review's approval on, and `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 unless the owner design review's row with the highest k reviews what the record freezes "by path and SHA-256", so that a version line changed in step (1) gives files the review did not read, and one changed after the freeze changes frozen files, which `validate` reports. When, by whom and in which commit does the line come to read 1.0, or is 1.0 the version the freeze record names, the files keeping the 0.<k> reviewed? | Align: the design document's and `rationale.md`'s version lines keep the 0.<k> that the owner design review read, and 1.0 is the version the freeze record gives them: the record names the design document's version as 1.0, with `reviewed_version`, the header's 0.<k> that it hashes, so that no file changes at or after the freeze and the reviewed text is the frozen text; `protocol/evidence.py` gives a later record `design_version` 1.0 while the design document has the SHA-256 that the approved freeze record names, and the header's 0.<k> otherwise; FR-034's "Version" reads "1.0 once frozen" as that name, and FR-043, data-model §1 `design_version`, §13 and §17 and the cli contract's `design_freeze.py` say so | B: the line reads 1.0 before the owner design review, in the last committed change before it, so that the review reads 1.0, and a change after the review makes the next 1.<k>, reviewed in a row of its own. | PRO-071 | The guard "Protocol log" lets `protocol/evidence.py` write only a `lock-cleared` record while the protocol change log does not exist, every other kind refused, while plan phase 1 step (2a) writes each platform's first environment record, a record of kind `environment` and so written through that module (data-model introduction, §1 `kind`), before step (4) creates the log, and the log's writer, `protocol/changelog.py`, checks the running environment against the platform's record in force, so that neither can come first. Is the environment record admitted while the log does not exist, with `protocol_seq` and `protocol_line_sha256` `none`, and do data-model §1's `protocol_seq` row, which calls `lock-cleared` "the one kind `protocol/evidence.py` writes then", and the guard's test name that exception? | Settle: while the protocol change log does not exist, the guard "Protocol log" admits two kinds through `protocol/evidence.py`, each with `protocol_seq` and `protocol_line_sha256` `none`: a `lock-cleared` record, as now, and an environment record written by `owner_record.py environment`, which records the platform and holds no result, so that each platform's first record comes before the log, in plan phase 1's order, and the log's writer runs under it; once the log exists an environment record, after a Windows or WSL update among others, cites its last entry as every record does, and every other kind stays refused; FR-044, plan phase 1, research R16, data-model §1 `protocol_seq` and §18 and the cli contract's "Protocol log", "Environment" and `owner_record.py` say so, and `tests/test_protocol_004.py` covers both kinds and the refusal of every other | B: the protocol change log comes first, at step (2a), its writer checking the running environment against research R1 and the pins of `requirements.txt` while no record is in force, its first entries naming `environment` `none`, and the first environment records follow it (looser). Which `status` does the freeze record hold, and which common fields does each of the four kinds hold as `none`? | Align: the freeze record's approval state moves to a field of its own, `freeze_status`, `draft` until the owner approves and then `approved`, which `approve` sets, `draft_sha256` leaves out with the other approval fields and `validate` reads, and its common `status` is computed by code as every record's is, `pass` once each of `create`'s checks holds, as it must for `create` to write the record; in each of the four kinds, `command`, `config`, `code_sha256`, `versions`, `platform`, `parallelism`, `exit_status`, `assertions`, each of the script's checks with its outcome, and `heavy_run`, false, hold the run of the script that wrote the record or its latest change, `owner_record.py environment`, `downloads.py` or `owner_record.py owner-provided`, `design_freeze.py create` and `review_package.py`, so that each is traced to the command and the code that wrote it, while `inputs` and `outputs` are `none`, the files each names being held in its own fields, `model_id` is `phyco-v3` in the freeze record and the review package's manifest and null in the other two, and `variant_id` is null in all four, the freeze record's variants standing in its `variant_map`; FR-043, FR-061, FR-065, data-model §1 `status` and "Kinds kept under a name of their own", §13 and §16 and the cli contract's `design_freeze.py` say so | B: the freeze record holds no common `status`, its §13 `status` being the only one, and each of the four kinds holds as `none` every common field but `record_id`, `kind`, `created_utc` and `disclaimer` (looser). C: unchanged: one key holds two meanings, and which fields are `none` is left to each writer | spec FR-043, FR-061, FR-065; data-model §1, §13, §16; cli contract `design_freeze.py` |

From the seventh re-run of 2026-11-02, answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the recommended
options, and applied, in checklist order:

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| ANA-073 | A `replacement` entry is logged with every decision that makes a candidate `rejected` or `ineligible` "while its family has fewer than three evaluated candidates and the budget allows", stays `pending` until a replacement's `proposed` entry or a stated reason, "the family full" or "the budget spent", closes it, and `round_rule.py --close` writes `set-closed` only once "no replacement is pending", while some decisions have a cause that no replacement can escape: `ineligible`, "not analysed at the production width: D4 chose the other family", which a replacement in that family would get too, a fired uniform control, which makes its size's bias evidence inconclusive "for every candidate", no validated trail route, and a match in the shared polynomial, which "touches every candidate and ends in a stop". Which decisions call for no replacement, and what closes their entries? | Settle: a `replacement` entry is logged open only with a decision whose cause lies in the candidate itself, its own evidence or properties; a decision whose cause its family or the whole set shares logs its `replacement` entry closed at once, with the reason "cause shared": `ineligible` for D4's choice of the other family (research R7, "The other family"), whose family takes a replacement only if it is analysed after all, and the decisions that a fired uniform control, the lack of a validated trail route or a match in the shared polynomial bring, which end in the stop rule's recommendation for every candidate, as written; `round_rule.py --close` reads those entries as closed; the protocol's "Eligibility and selection" and "Stop rule", Edge Cases, data-model §3 "Decisions", research R7 and the cli contract's `round_rule.py` say so | B: every such decision keeps its `pending` entry, and replacements are entered and decided in turn until "the family full" or "the budget spent" closes it, so that hours go to candidates that cannot become eligible. C: unchanged: the set cannot close while those entries wait | spec "Eligibility and selection", "Stop rule", Edge Cases; data-model §3; research R7; cli contract `round_rule.py` |
| PRO-068 | `protocol/evidence.py` writes every record, the environment records among its fixed-name files, and exits 2 at start unless the running environment matches "the platform's environment record in force" and that record's row "Environment record <n>" reads Approved at HEAD, `scripts/recheck_004.py` alone being exempt, while plan phase 1 writes the first record when none is in force, after "a Windows or WSL update" the guard refuses the very record that "the owner then writes" with `owner_record.py environment` to resolve the difference, and a `lock-cleared` record, which PRO-065 lets that module write before the log exists, is refused until a new record is approved, so that a stale `data/log-append.lock` blocks every log writer. Which writes does the guard admit, on what condition, what does their `environment` field hold, and does the guard apply to the log writers? | Settle: the guard "Environment" checks every write through `protocol/evidence.py` and through the log writers, `protocol/changelog.py` and `protocol/provenance.py`, and admits one write while the running environment differs from the platform's record in force, or none is in force: the environment record itself, written by `owner_record.py environment` and checked instead against research R1 and the pins of `requirements.txt` for that platform, exit 2 on any difference, its `environment` field `none` with the interpreter's and each pinned package's version that it checked; every other write, a `lock-cleared` record included, waits until that record's row "Environment record <n>" reads Approved at HEAD with its SHA-256, which needs no lock, so that a stale lock found after an update is cleared once the new record is approved, and none can be held before the first record, since no log writer runs before it (REP-082); FR-058, data-model §1 `environment`, research R1 and R16 "Stale locks" and the cli contract's "Environment", "Protocol log", `owner_record.py` and `clear_lock.py` say so | B: the guard exempts `owner_record.py` and `clear_lock.py` as wholes, with no check against research R1 and the pins, and the log writers take no environment guard (looser). C: unchanged: the first record and the record after an update are refused, and so is every write that waits for them | spec FR-058; data-model §1; research R1, R16; cli contract "Environment", "Protocol log", `owner_record.py`, `clear_lock.py` |
| HW-083 | An instantiate's input is "the nonce's words, in the model's word order, then the personalization string's length word, then the string's words", and FR-025's first figure runs "from the nonce's last word accepted to the copy done", so that the copy sets every lane from the key and the nonce before the length word, "refused above the design's maximum", is checked, while the model raises `ValueError` for an over-long string, after which "nothing changes, and the object stays usable", and FR-025 makes a refusal that changes nothing only of "a bad request that valid and ready refuse before anything changes". Is that refusal an error path that zeroizes, or one that changes nothing, and what does a refused instantiate in `instantiated` leave of the state it would have replaced? | Align: the length check precedes every change, as the model's rule has it: the nonce's words fill the nonce chain and the rate lanes' nonce registers as now, and the copy that sets the lanes from the key and the nonce waits until the personalization string's length word is accepted and found within the design's maximum, so that a length over it is a bad request refused before the lanes, the key or a counter changes, the refusal clearing the nonce chain and the nonce registers, which hold only that call's words; the core stays in the state it was in, `key-held` or `instantiated`, with the state a refused instantiate would have replaced, and a new instantiate may follow, as the model's may; FR-025's first figure runs from the length word accepted to the copy done, and its tests cover a refused instantiate in `key-held` and in `instantiated` and the instantiate after it; FR-025 "Flow control", "Key loading and reset" and "Zeroization", FR-058 and the model contract's "Lengths" and "Failure paths" say so | B: the refusal is an error path in the RTL and the model alike: it zeroizes and needs a new key, the model's `instantiate` then ending the object as a limit does. C: unchanged: the copy precedes the check, so that a refusal that changes nothing cannot occur | spec FR-025, FR-058; model contract "Lengths", "Failure paths" |
| REP-082 | `protocol/evidence.py` "checks at start the running environment against the platform's environment record in force" and exits 2 unless that record's row reads Approved at HEAD, `scripts/recheck_004.py` "alone" being exempt, while plan phase 1 writes the downloads manifest's `approved` entries at step (5) with `scripts/downloads.py init` and each wheel's `fetched` entry at step (6), both through that module, before step (7) writes "the environment records of both platforms", the first of them while no record is in force, as FR-058 writes the first records only "once the wheels are installed and the pins take effect". Which writes does the guard admit before the first record is in force and approved, and what do the early entries and the first records name as their environment? | Align: each platform's first environment record comes before any write through `protocol/evidence.py` or a log writer: plan phase 1 runs (1) and (2) as now, then the first environment records, of research R1's interpreter and the packages that `requirements.txt` pins before the wheels, written by the owner with `owner_record.py environment`, the one write the guard admits while no record is in force (PRO-068), each with its row "Environment record <n>" Approved before the next step; then (3) to (6) under them, every entry and record naming the record in force as its `environment`; then (7), each platform's new record once the wheels are installed and R18's pins written into `requirements.txt`, approved before any later write, which is the new environment record that FR-058 asks for once the wheels are installed; then (8) and (9); FR-058, research R1 and R19, data-model §1 `environment` and the cli contract's "Environment" and `owner_record.py` say so | B: before the first record is in force the guard admits plan phase 1's steps (3) to (7), each checked against research R1 and the pins installed so far, with `environment` `setup` (looser). C: unchanged: step (5)'s and step (6)'s writes and the first record are refused | plan "Phases and gates"; spec FR-058; research R1, R19; data-model §1; cli contract "Environment", `owner_record.py` |

From the sixth re-run of 2026-11-02, answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the recommended options,
and applied, in checklist order:

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|

Where does the chapter's status stand? | Align: the chapter carries no mark in `design.md`, as FR-034 asks of the whole document: its status is the `review_status` of its provenance entries, `pending` from its drafting, and its Review & Approval row, `_pending_` until the review; the review adds a review entry for each chapter it covers (data-model §14), and nothing in the file changes, so that the text the review approved is the text `create` compares (SEC-075); FR-012, plan phase 4 and the row's notes read "recorded as under review, in the provenance log and in its Review & Approval row, until the security-target review of phase 8" in place of the mark | B: keep the mark in the chapter and remove it after the approval in a commit of its own, `create` accepting a chapter that differs from the reviewed one only by the mark's line (looser). C: unchanged: the mark's place and its removal are unstated, and its removal changes the text the review approved | spec FR-012, Review & Approval "Security-target review"; plan phase 4; data-model §14 |

Where do the replacement rows stand? | Align: the replacement rows stand in a chapter of their own, "Replaced mechanisms" (RAT-069), outside the mapping chapter: one row for each of S2, S5 to S7 and S11 to S15, naming the rule of Π that replaces it and the evidence record, or the section and test where the answer is by specification, as FR-030 lists them; FR-051's lessons table, in security.md and in the final report, cites that chapter's rows with their records; the mapping chapter keeps each component's place in the metaphor and its one fixed sentence, so that its word rule, its one allow-list entry and "contains no security statement, and none cites it" stand as they are, and the claim test's other rules apply to the new chapter as to the rest of the design document; FR-030 names the chapter, and FR-034's list of chapters holds it | B: the rows stand in the rationale, each beside the attack class of FR-034's "Attack classes" it concerns, the mapping chapter unchanged. From the fifth re-run of 2026-11-02, answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the recommended options,
and applied, in checklist order:

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| ANA-068 | FR-039, research R4 and R13 and data-model §7 re-find each of the avalanche test's planted results at every round count from 1 to its variant's R "only if exactly that set fires", the expected set being the planted bits and every bit that the exact dependency matrices show independent of the flipped bit at that round count, while below full diffusion a bit that depends on the flipped bit can still fire, one that receives it only through an XOR flipping on every input, so that a correct test reads `not re-found`, which keeps analysis CHK062 open. Which comparison does a correct test meet, and at which round counts? | Settle (the reading a correct test meets, beside ANA-062): at every round count from 1 to the variant's R every bit of the expected set must fire, since each of them flips always or never; a firing outside it is reported with its round count, position and count and decides nothing below the variant's R; at the variant's R, fixed with the known result before the tool is drafted as a round count at which the exact dependency matrices show every bit outside the expected set dependent on the flipped bit, no other bit may fire, and the chance that a correct test fires there on a uniform bit, at most b times the threshold 0.005/(128·S_A) per flipped bit, is stated beside it; the related-key, related-nonce and rotational planted results are compared in the same way, each from its own dependency matrices; "How to answer", the spec's clarification of 2026-11-02, research's introduction and the plan name ANA-068 beside ANA-062 | B: check at every round count only that every bit of the expected set fires, with no exact set at any round count (looser). C: unchanged: below full diffusion a correct test fires outside the expected set and reads `not re-found` | spec FR-039, Clarifications of 2026-11-02; research introduction, R4, R13; plan (the paragraph naming ANA-062); data-model §7; checklist-choices.md "How to answer" |
| ANA-069 | FR-039 says "Each known result is of the kind of its use, or a cross-check covers the difference", while FR-039 and research R12 also say that the cross-checks at w = 4 and 8 "never widen a kind", that a search's results count only at a width its known results cover and that "A tool used outside its validated kind writes `not evaluated`", and the cross-checks themselves run the ARX search at w = 4 and 8, outside its kind of w = 16, and the active-S-box counter at 4×4 with w = 4, so that, read literally, each is a use outside the kind that calls for a validation again, after which the cli contract's "until then its results are `not evaluated`" reads as if a validation on the same known results widened the kind. What difference does a cross-check cover, and is it a use of the tool it checks? | Settle: a cross-check checks a tool on a candidate's own boxes and layers and is not a use: its values are compared, by exact equality and against the floor, never counted, and it writes no `not evaluated` and calls for no validation again; it covers the candidate's operation set, which no known result shares, so that a use on that operation set counts once the candidate's cross-checks pass, in place of a validation again, which on the same known results could not cover it, and it never covers a word width or a kind of model; FR-039 reads "Each known result is of the kind of its use in word width and kind of model, and the cross-checks cover each candidate's operation set"; a use at a width or kind of model that no known result covers counts only once a further known result covering it, fixed with its source before the tool's first validation, is re-found, and a validation again on the known results the tool had never widens its kind, so the cli contract's "until then" means until such a known result is re-found; FR-039, research R12, data-model §7 and the cli contract's "Tool validation" say so | B: count the cross-checks at w = 4 and 8 as validations at those widths, widening the kind for the candidate they check (looser). C: unchanged: read literally, the cross-checks run outside the kind and write `not evaluated`, and a validation again on the same known results widens the kind | spec FR-039; research R12; data-model §7; cli contract "Tool validation" |
| REP-074 | FR-065, research R16 and data-model §18 say only that "a disagreement in either is reported", the cli contract has `recheck_004.py` exit 1 on any disagreement, data-model §1 classifies the `recheck` record as no finding, and `round_rule.py` reads the candidate's `reproduction` records, not the `recheck` records, so that a verdict the re-check cannot recompute leaves the candidate's eligibility as it was; the tool-defect lists of the spec's Edge Cases and research R7 differ, the Edge Cases leaving out the failed cross-check between kinds; and the rule that makes a differing reproduction a tool defect "since it ran in the environment that made the original" does not fit a `recheck` record's reproduction, which runs anywhere. What does a disagreement of the re-check block? | Settle: a disagreement under the rules in force at a record's entry blocks the candidate as a differing reproduction does: the record's result becomes an unverified hypothesis, `blocked` (FR-060), the `recheck` record is a finding, and the disagreement is a tool defect of research R7, whose one re-run follows the fix; a disagreement under a later stricter change is that change's recomputed verdict and is applied as R7's stricter change, as `results_known` records it (data-model §18), not a defect; a `recheck` record's reproduction that differs is a tool defect of `recheck_004.py` wherever it ran, since its verdicts and counts depend on no environment; `round_rule.py --select` reads the `recheck` records beside the `reproduction` records and exits 2 while one holds an unresolved disagreement for a record it cites; and the spec's Edge Cases list of tool defects gains the failed cross-check between kinds and the re-check's disagreement, research R7's list the re-check's disagreement, so that the two lists agree | B: a disagreement is reported and blocks nothing, every record's classification unchanged (looser). C: unchanged: a verdict the re-check cannot recompute leaves the candidate's eligibility as it was | spec FR-060, FR-065, Edge Cases "A run is suspected of a tool defect"; research R7, R16; data-model §1, §9, §18; cli contract `recheck_004.py`, `round_rule.py` |

From the fourth re-run of 2026-11-02, answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the recommended
options, and applied, in checklist order:

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-066 | FR-050 asks every document of this feature, and FR-068 every hand-written one, for the design-form disclaimer, and FR-069's claim test checks it over a scope that names the checklists, while none of the eight checklist files carries it and checklist-choices.md does. Do the checklists carry it, and does tasks.md, which `/speckit-tasks` writes into the same directory? | Align: each of the eight checklist files (analysis, design-rationale, hardware, protocol, redesign-inputs, reproducibility, requirements and security) gains the disclaimer under its title, as checklist-choices.md has it, a change to its header alone, its items and ticks unchanged; tasks.md carries it from its first version; and the claim test's scope keeps the checklists, so that it fails while one lacks it | B: exempt the checklists from FR-050 and FR-068 as review artifacts, stated in FR-069's scope. C: unchanged: the claim test fails on all eight files | spec FR-050, FR-068, FR-069; cli contract `check_claims.py`; the eight checklist files; tasks.md |
| RAT-062 | FR-064 and data-model §14 keep each AI-drafted section's review status in the provenance log, whose hash-chained lines are only appended, while the owner design review "updates" that status and §14 says "one line per file" though each section has its own entry; and an evidence record's `reviewer` "stays `pending` until a review is recorded" in a record that is never overwritten. How is a review recorded without changing an entry or a record? | Align: a review is a new provenance entry for the same `path` and `section`, holding the `seq` of the entry it follows and the SHA-256 of the section text reviewed, and the latest entry for that path and section gives the status; `review_status` takes `pending`, `accepted` or `changes-requested`. Data-model §14 opens "one line per file, and per section where a file is drafted in parts". An evidence record's `reviewer` stays as written; its review is a provenance entry that names the record's ID and SHA-256, and data-model §1 says so | B: a fixed-name review-status file beside the rationale, its versions kept, outside the frozen text. C: unchanged: an update has no stated form in an append-only log | spec FR-034, FR-064, FR-067; data-model §1, §14 |
| RAT-063 | An exact D4 tie goes first to the smaller R at 4×4, then to fewer serial carry chains per round, and only then to the owner's choice, while the "D4 tie" row applies "only if the nonlinearity choice rule ends in an exact tie" and `round_rule.py --select` waits after any D4 tie for that row to read Approved with the SHA-256 of `d4/tie-choice.json`, a file that exists only when the tie-breaks reach the owner. What do the row and `--select` do when `d4/tie.json` decides the tie by R or by the carry chains? | Align: `d4/tie.json` gains `decided_by`: `R at 4×4`, `carry chains`, `ineligible` or `owner`. When a tie-break decides, no choice is made: the "D4 tie" row reads "Not needed", set by the owner as the first list says, naming the tie record and its tie-break, and `--select` opens on that value at HEAD and takes the family from `d4/tie.json`; only when the tie-breaks reach the owner's choice does `--select` wait for the row to read Approved with `d4/tie-choice.json`'s SHA-256. The row's condition reads "only if the tie-breaks reach the owner's choice"; selection ties go to the owner at once and are unchanged | B: always write `d4/tie-choice.json`, the owner confirming a tie-break's outcome as a choice. C: unchanged: selection waits for a choice that a decided tie never makes | spec Review & Approval "D4 tie" and its first list; data-model §8; cli contract `d4_choice.py`, `round_rule.py` |
| ANA-059 | Data-model §7 records a validated kind's word width, and research R12 makes a use outside the validated kind a trigger for validating again, with `not evaluated` until then, while the ARX search's one named known result, SPECK32's optimal trails, has 16-bit words, its counted searches run at w = 16 and 32, and its cross-checks at w = 4 and 8. Which word widths does each tool's validated kind cover, and how do the cross-checks bear on it? | Settle: `validated_kind` states the word widths its known results cover, and a search's results count only at a width so covered. The ARX search's kind is w = 16, from SPECK32; its searches at w = 32 count only after it re-finds a known result at 32-bit words, fixed with its source before its first validation as a further known result, for example SPECK64's optimal trails where one of research R19's approved papers gives them, and otherwise its results at w = 32 are `not evaluated`. The cross-checks at w = 4 and 8 check each candidate's boxes and never widen a kind. Each other tool's kind states its widths in the same way | B: treat the exact models of addition as generic in w, so that a kind validated at w = 16 covers every width: looser, since a fault that shows only at w = 32 would count. C: unchanged: the validated kind's width is not stated, so a use at w = 32 is either always or never outside it | spec FR-039; research R12, R19; data-model §7 |
| ANA-060 | Research R12 compares, by exact equality, the ARX search's optimal single-trail weight over r rounds of a box with the best product of one-round entries of the box's exhaustive tables, "one round equalling the table's best entry", while a table entry sums every trail through the box's internal additions, so that a correct search can lie above it for a box with more than one addition; and its rule that a trail weight above a differential's weight is expected and not compared also passes a search that reports too high a weight. Can the cross-check pass a correct search and fail one that misses optimal trails? | Settle: two checks replace the comparison. At w = 4 the ARX search's optimal single-trail weight over r rounds equals that of an exhaustive search over the box's internal trails, run by the exhaustive-tables tool, for every r that search reaches within its 8 h cap; and at w = 4 and 8 no search result lies below the best product of one-round entries of the box's exhaustive tables, which is a floor. Either failure is a tool defect (R7). They replace "one round equalling the table's best entry" and "not compared", and the record lists both values per r | B: equality only for boxes of one addition, the floor alone elsewhere, so that a search that misses optimal trails through a box of two or more additions could still pass. C: unchanged: read as written, the comparison passes a search that overstates weights, which is the floor alone | spec FR-039; research R7, R12; data-model §6 `cross_check`, §7 |
| ANA-062 | Data-model §7 re-finds the avalanche test's planted gap only if, "at every round count tested, every difference bit of the unmixed lane fires and no other bit does", while below the variant's full diffusion the bits that the exact dependency matrices show independent of the flipped bit never flip and so fire as well. Are the tested round counts and each one's expected set stated, and is "unmixed" defined, for the flip set and for the related-key, related-nonce and rotational paths? | Align: each planted result states the round counts tested, 1 to its variant's R, and at each round count its expected set: the planted bits and every bit that the exact dependency matrices show independent of the flipped bit at that round count; it is re-found only if exactly that set fires. "Unmixed" means a lane that no update writes, which others may read. The related-key, related-nonce and rotational planted results state their sets in the same way, from their own dependency matrices | B: test only round counts at or above the variant's full diffusion, where only the planted bits fire. C: unchanged: as written, a correct test fails below full diffusion | spec FR-039; research R4, R13; data-model §7 |
| PRO-058 | PRO-056's Changes cell names research R7, R12 and R16, spec FR-045 and User Story 4 and the cli contract, but its applied change (commit 810cbe2) also altered the plan table "Values this plan fixes before first use", row "Analysis budget per candidate (stop rule)", while the fourth list, approved as written, has no heading "Values this plan fixes", and "A row is listed under a rule when its Changes cell names that rule's text". Is a row listed by its Changes cell or by the text its applied change altered, and is a check stated that no altered place stays outside the log? | Settle: a row is listed under every rule whose text its Changes cell names or its applied change altered. PRO-056 is added under "Values this plan fixes" beside the fourth list, with the reason, that commit 810cbe2 altered that table's row "Analysis budget per candidate (stop rule)", and the date; the owner's answer to this round approves the addition. Before the log is created, its writer (PRO-060) compares each rule's text, in each place its heading names, at the two commits of each round, and exits 2, writing nothing, while a place whose text changed has no row of that round listed under its rule; the comparison's result is recorded with the first entries | B: list by the Changes cell only and leave PRO-056's change to the table outside the log: looser, since a change to a value the plan fixes would enter no entry. C: unchanged: the table's old and new text enter no first entry | spec FR-044; data-model §18; checklist-choices.md "Rows that change the protocol" |
| PRO-059 | The demonstration's Budget row allows "a repeat only after a tool defect is fixed (Edge Cases)", and `fut_sec8.py --repeat-after-defect` admits one for each recorded defect "that no earlier record names", with no limit, while research R7's FUT-SEC-8 row "holds one pass per target" and "each row refuses a run once it is spent", FR-039 charges the vector check and sweep to the demonstration's own budget, and FR-045 charges every heavy run that charges no candidate to the feature-wide row. Which row holds a repeat, how many may follow, and does one run only on the owner's request? | Settle: the FUT-SEC-8 row holds the one pass per target with its vector check, and nothing else. A repeat after a fixed tool defect, its vector check included, is a heavy run charged to research R7's feature-wide row: it projects first, is refused, exit 2, once it does not fit, and runs only on the owner's request, quoted with its date in its record as `owner_request`, as a heavy reproduction does, so that the agent never starts one. Each repeat follows a defect that no earlier record names, and the feature-wide row's hours bound their number. FR-045's "every heavy run that charges no candidate" excepts the FUT-SEC-8 row's pass, and FR-039, R7's cap and the cli contract say so | B: charge each repeat to the FUT-SEC-8 row, enlarged by one pass per repeat: looser, since the cap of R7 would no longer bound the repeats. C: unchanged: a repeat has no row, so the cap does not bound it | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "FUT-SEC-8 demonstration", Edge Cases, FR-039, FR-045; research R7; data-model §4; cli contract `fut_sec8.py`, "Budget" |
| HW-071 | FR-025 asks the design document to specify "serial or parallel key loading", while the same requirement loads the key "once per key along a registered shift chain", FR-013 loads it "once, along a registered shift chain", FR-059 feeds the self-test's key "word by word as the host would" with "no parallel load path", and the plan's Complexity Tracking rejects a central key register with a tree to the key lanes. Is a parallel load excluded from FR-025's list? | Align: FR-025's list reads "serial key loading along the registered shift chain below, the three latencies stated below", dropping "or parallel", so that a parallel load path, which FR-013, FR-059 and the Complexity Tracking exclude, cannot be read as allowed | B: keep the choice open for feature 008, a parallel path then justified under Principle III as the trees are. C: unchanged: FR-025 offers a load path that the rest of the text excludes | spec FR-025 "Key loading and reset" |
| HW-073 | FR-018, research R14 and R15 and the model contract have `gen_constants_v3.py` write an exploration table "for that (n, w) on demand" under `research/004/exploration/` "with its check record", and FUT-GRID-1's "Partly met" rests on such tables, while the cli contract gives `gen_constants_v3.py` no option that names an (n, w), that path or that record, and after the freeze a generator runs only with `--check` or `--reproduce`. Is the command stated, with when it may run, the check record's kind and place, and what a failed check means? | Align: `gen_constants_v3.py --exploration N W` writes, before or after the freeze, the table of an (n, w) outside the supported sizes, with every check of research R15, as `research/004/exploration/constants_n<n>_w<w>.json`, and beside it its check record, a `generator-run` record with `exploration` true, written through `protocol/evidence.py` to that directory; it writes no frozen file, no sidecar entry and no evidence, and refuses a supported (n, w), exit 2. The cli contract's rule reads "after the freeze a generator runs only with `--check`, `--reproduce` or, for `gen_constants_v3.py`, `--exploration`". A failed check, a refused-list match included, leaves that (n, w) without a table, its record stating why, and is a finding recorded at once in security.md and the report (FR-051), as a match found after the freeze is; `phyco-v3` stays as frozen | B: a separate script outside the frozen set writes the exploration tables, importing the frozen generator's rule. C: unchanged: the tables FUT-GRID-1 rests on have no command | spec FR-018, FR-023; research R14, R15 "Tables per size"; data-model §1, §11; cli contract "Generators of constants and vectors"; model contract "Variant IDs" |
| REP-070 | FR-053 pins `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/**`, `research/004/**` and `protocol/*.json` to LF and then says "Files committed there are limited to `.json`, `.jsonl`, `.md`, `.csv`, `.txt` and `.gz`, and `protocol/evidence.py` refuses any other suffix there", while the plan's tree puts code under `research/004/` (`ir.py`, the candidate modules, the superseded operation lists and the tools) and research R16 and the data-model introduction state the limit for the reports directory only. Which paths does the limit govern? | Align: FR-053 states the limit for `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/` only, as research R16 and the data-model introduction do; `research/004/**` holds the plan's Python code beside files of those suffixes, and `protocol/*.json` JSON only, each pinned to LF as now | B: move the code out of `research/004/` into a code directory, so that the limit covers `research/004/**` too. C: unchanged: the limit refuses the code the plan places there | spec FR-053 "Hashes and line endings"; research R16, R18 |

From the third re-run of 2026-11-01, answered on 2026-11-02, "all recommended", and applied, in
checklist order:

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-061 | Research R10 says the security-target chapter "cannot pass the review that records L1" while a theorem it uses is "unverified", but the spec lets that review pass and the "L1 recorded" row read "blocked"; and `design_freeze.py create` checks neither the theorem status of `limits.json` nor the preconditions, though a mode without a matching theorem is never adopted (FR-013, Edge Cases). May the review pass, and the owner design review and the freeze go ahead, while a theorem is "unverified"? | Align: the security-target review may read Approved while a theorem is "unverified", as the spec says, and the "L1 recorded" row then reads "blocked", naming the missing paper; research R10's sentence reads "and L1 is not recorded while it is (FR-012)". The owner design review and the freeze need every theorem the mode chapter uses `verified` in `limits.json`, with each precondition marked met in the mode chapter: `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 otherwise, naming the theorem, and the freeze record holds `limits.json`'s SHA-256 and each theorem's status. If a paper's form differs, the design takes it in a new design-document version, or the feature stops (research R10) | B: let the freeze go ahead with a theorem "unverified", recorded as an open item: looser, since a mode with no matching theorem could be frozen. C: unchanged: R10 and the spec disagree on whether the review can pass, and nothing stops the freeze | research R10; spec FR-012, FR-043; data-model §13; cli contract `design_freeze.py` |
| RAT-050 | Research R13 and data-model §5 give "w − 1 for a w-bit addition", while the top bit of the sum of two variable w-bit words has degree w (bit i has degree i + 1), and w − 1 holds only with a constant operand; a round of P phases has no rule; and the known result validates the truth-table route at w = 4, while at w = 32 the records come from the stated degrees and data-model §7 asks for "the degree bound's exact degree", which a correct bound looser than the exact degree fails. Are the stated degrees upper bounds, and is the route that the claimable sizes use the one validated? | Settle: by the conservative reading, R_dist keeps rising at every round count where the stated rule's count, w − 1 per addition, is at most 126, so no round count that the pre-registered rule raises is dropped, and the record says that this count is the rule's, not an upper bound. Beside it the record holds an upper bound, w for an addition of two variable words, with the round counts it reaches. A round of P phases is bounded by the product of its phases' degrees. The derivation's validation reproduces the rule's count exactly on the known result at w = 4, with the exact degree from the truth table reported beside it and the upper bound checked to be at least that degree; data-model §7 reads so | B: correct the rule to w for two variable operands and drop the w − 1 counts: looser, since round counts that the pre-registered rule raises would no longer be raised. C: unchanged: a record's cost of 2^(d + 1) would rest on a count that is not an upper bound, and §7's check would fail a correct bound | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule", FR-034; research R13 "Algebraic degree"; data-model §5 `degree`, §7 |
| RAT-051 | Research R13's symmetry check covers "every per-node constant, such as the rotation amounts", and amounts equal at every node are mapped to themselves by every translation, so they fail it, as they fail FR-024's "no non-trivial grid symmetry"; yet FR-022 gives the ARX-box one set of trail bounds, research R15 ranks "the possible amounts" as for one box, and no rule derives a size's assignment from n. May every node use the same amounts, and if not, how are the search, the bounds and the active-box count stated? | Align: R13's check of each pattern on its own stays, so the amounts differ between nodes. Each candidate states K ≥ 2 sets of amounts and a placement rule from n, in its log entry before its search, whose placement has no symmetry of the torus that moves a node at every claimable size; one set at the three nodes (0,0), (0,1) and (1,2) and another elsewhere has none at 4×4, 8×8 and 16×16 (computed on 2026-11-01). FR-022's box bounds become one pair per distinct box, each box searched within research R7's 8 h per instance and the 48 h row; the bound for Π counts active boxes at the smallest of the K boxes' bounds; and R15's search ranks an assignment by its weakest box (RAT-059). At 2×2 and 3×3, RAT-052's rule for the research sizes applies | B: let every node use the same amounts and check them together with each round's constants, which the constants break: one box and one search, but looser than R13's check of each pattern on its own. C: unchanged: R13's check fails the one-box design that FR-022 and R15 assume | spec FR-022, FR-024, FR-037; research R11, R12, R13, R15; data-model §3 |
| RAT-052 | Research R13 checks "the placement of the food nodes" as a pattern of its own, but one food node is fixed by a rotation about it, two by the half-turn about their midpoint, all nodes by every symmetry, and every placement at 2×2 and 3×3 by some symmetry that moves a node (computed on 2026-11-01), so such a placement fails although distinct constants at the food nodes break the symmetry, and FR-037 makes the check an eligibility test at every supported size. Are the placement and its constants checked as one pattern, or must the placement have no symmetry, and what is the verdict at 2×2 and 3×3? | Align: at every claimable size the placement itself has no symmetry that moves a node, so the food-node rule of FR-018 and research R13 takes at least three food nodes in an asymmetric arrangement, such as (0,0), (0,1) and (1,2), which has none at 4×4, 8×8 and 16×16 (computed on 2026-11-01), and R13's check stays as written there. At 2×2 and 3×3, where no placement without such a symmetry exists (computed the same day), the placement is checked with the constants it carries as one pattern, the `symmetry` record says so, the size profile marks the variant, and no claim covers it (D3), as RAT-036 does for the constants at 2×2. The check changes only at these two research sizes, where as written it fails every candidate, and loosens nothing at a claimable size | B: check the placement with its constants as one pattern at every size: looser, since a symmetric placement could stand at a claimable size. C: unchanged: every candidate fails the check at 2×2 and 3×3, an eligibility item, and the feature stops | research R13 "Symmetry"; spec FR-018, FR-037; data-model §5 `symmetry` |
| RAT-054 | FR-030 has the mapping chapter restate, for the selected candidate, each component's place in the metaphor "and the attack class, bound or need it serves", every component naming "at least one attack class it resists", while the chapter "contains no security statement" and the claim-register rule flags resist, resists, resistance and security inside it. Is the attack-class part kept outside the chapter, or is a stated form of the restatement exempt? | Align: the chapter stays free of security statements and restates each component's place in the metaphor only, while the attack class, bound or need each component serves moves to the rationale's component list (FR-034), which cites the candidate's `mapping` log entry; that entry keeps both parts, since it is not in the chapter. The chapter's one fixed negative statement stays its only allow-list entry, the owner design review checks that the chapter and the rationale's list agree with the entry, and FR-030's "at least one attack class it resists" is required of the rationale's list | B: exempt a stated form of the restatement ("serves: <attack class>") through an allow-list entry: the chapter keeps both parts, but it would hold a security statement per component, which FR-030 forbids. C: unchanged: the restatement FR-030 requires fails the rule FR-069 applies to the same chapter | spec FR-030, FR-034, FR-069; data-model §3 `mapping`, §15 `allow_list` |
| ANA-048 | The round rule lists "a reproduction's finding" among the rises that extend the bias tests, while a reproduction "can neither replace evidence nor add any", charges no row, is not a re-run, and has its verdict recomputed under the versions the original cites. When a reproduction differs, which result stands, is the difference a tool defect (research R7 names a disagreement "between two evaluators of one operation list", not between two runs or two environments), and who writes the evidence that raises R, in which row and with which class? | Align: a reproduction stays outside the evidence. The original stands, and a reproduction that differs on a field that enters a verdict is a finding that blocks the candidate's eligibility, `blocked`, as an unverified hypothesis does (FR-060), until it is resolved. Research R7's tool defects gain such a difference in the environment that made the original; that defect gives R7's one re-run, a new measurement in the original's row, whose own result raises R by the round rule if it fires, with its own class. The round rule's "a reproduction's finding" reads "a re-run after a reproduction's finding". A difference in a field that decides no verdict is reported and blocks nothing (REP-059) | B: let a reproduction's own result raise R directly: one run fewer, but a run that charges no row and is no evidence would change R. C: unchanged: the round rule counts a result that the reproduction rules say adds no evidence | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule"; research R4, R7, R16; data-model §1 "Reproduction"; cli contract "Reproduction" |
| ANA-054 | Research R7 fixes the 48 h row as "the reduced-width and rotation-amount searches, then box tables, box-trail searches, active-box or active-S-box counting and the solver cross-checks", the cross-checks last, while research R12 lets the ARX search count at w = 16 and 32 only after its cross-check at w = 4 and 8, and the box the cross-check uses carries the amounts that the rotation search at 16 chooses. In which order do the box tables, the cross-check, the rotation search and the D4 searches run, so that neither the D4 choice nor the amounts wait for a check that needs their own result? | Align: the cross-check becomes independent of the searches it gates. Each candidate's cross-checks run first in its 48 h row, at w = 4 and 8 on its box with the first amounts of its recorded space in tie order, fixed before the run, so they need no search result; then the rotation-amount search at 16 and the D4 searches, whose bound records then count; and after the choice the chosen amounts, scaled to w = 4 and 8, get a cross-check of their own before the production-width searches count, followed by the box tables, the box-trail searches and the counting. Research R7's fixed order reads so, and `d4_choice.py` uses only bound records of a tool that has passed its cross-check on that candidate | B: let the D4 searches and the rotation search use bound records that do not yet count, and compute the D4 choice again if a later cross-check fails: the recorded choice could change. C: unchanged: the searches at 16 would count only after a check that needs the amounts they choose | research R7, R12, R15; spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Nonlinearity choice rule", FR-039; data-model §6, §8 |
| REP-056 | The cli contract's introduction makes `fut_sec8.py --reproduce` the general mode, writing a `reproduction` record under `$REP/reproductions/<record_id>/<n>/`, while its own section runs `--reproduce RECORD --binary fast/build/seed_search --out-dir $REP/fut-sec-8` and writes a `fut-sec-8-reproduction` record, which data-model §4 places at `fut-sec-8/reproduction-<record_id>.json`, holding "the same fields". Which kind, path and fields does it write, whose `<record_id>` names the file, and does a mode whose output directory is replaced take `--out-dir`? | Align: the general mode holds. `fut_sec8.py --reproduce RECORD --binary fast/build/seed_search` writes a `reproduction` record and its outputs under `$REP/reproductions/<record_id>/<n>/`, `<record_id>` being the demonstration's and n = 1, 2, … for successive reproductions, compared under data-model §1's rule, with the timings and the binary's hash reported only and `design_sha256` not compared; it takes no `--out-dir`. The kind `fut-sec-8-reproduction` and the path `fut-sec-8/reproduction-<record_id>.json` go, data-model §4 and the plan's tree place any reproduction under `reproductions/`, and the conditions of `fut_sec8.py`'s section stay (after the FUT-SEC-8 result review, a heavy run) | B: keep `fut-sec-8-reproduction` as a kind of its own at `fut-sec-8/reproduction-<record_id>-<n>.json`: the demonstration's files stay together, but one mode would have two record forms. C: unchanged: a second reproduction would find an existing record and exit 2 | cli contract "Reproduction", `fut_sec8.py`; data-model §1 "Reproduction", §4; plan "Project Structure" |
| REP-058 | A reproduction "writes its outputs and a `reproduction` record into `reproductions/<record_id>/<n>/`", under the reports directory, while local-only files are written under `data/local/004/<record_id>/` and "never under this directory", `protocol/evidence.py` refuses a committed output above 10 MB or with another suffix, and the originals include outputs far above 10 MB (the 512 MiB bitmap, dependency matrices of about 32 MB, about 60 MB of synthetic sets) and generated files with other suffixes (`phycoweb/constants_v3.py`, `phycoweb/selftest_v3.py`, the `.hex` vectors). Where does each output of a reproduction go, and is the directory committed? | Align: the storage rules hold for reproductions too. The reproduction record, and each output that the original commits, go to `reproductions/<record_id>/<n>/`, which is committed under the same suffix and 10 MB rules; an output the original keeps local-only goes to `data/local/004/<reproduction's record_id>/`, never committed, and the record holds its SHA-256, compared with the original's; and a generator's reproduction compares each generated file in memory with the SHA-256 of the run it reproduces (REP-060) and writes no `.py` or `.hex` file, its record holding each file's path, SHA-256 and outcome. Data-model §1, research R16 and the cli contract's "Reproduction" and "Generators of constants and vectors" paragraphs say so | B: keep every reproduction output local-only and commit only the record: smaller commits, but a reviewer could not inspect a differing output without running it again. C: unchanged: the storage rules refuse the outputs a reproduction is told to write | data-model introduction, §1 "Reproduction", `outputs`; research R16; cli contract "Reproduction", "Generators of constants and vectors" |

From the second re-run of 2026-11-01, answered the same day, "all recommended", and applied, in
checklist order:

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-048 | security.md §15 gates L1 at "spec approval", while FR-012 records L1 in a row of its own once FUT-SEC-8 and the design document's security-target chapter have been reviewed, and the ladder's evidence column asks for "FUT-SEC-8 done", which could not hold when the spec was approved. Which gate holds? | Align: the Gate cell of §15's L1 row reads "FUT-SEC-8 result review and security-target review (FR-012)" in place of "spec approval", and its evidence column is unchanged. The spec approval of 2026-11-01 stays what it was, the start of the design work with L1 unrecorded, and the claim register keeps `phyco-v3` at L0 until the spec's "L1 recorded" row reads Approved (SEC-049 places the review). The change goes into security.md 2.13 with a history entry | B: keep "spec approval" and drop "FUT-SEC-8 done" from L1's evidence: looser, since L1 would need no demonstration. C: unchanged: the gate passed on 2026-11-01 while its evidence did not exist | security.md §15, §25 (version 2.13); spec FR-012, Review & Approval "L1 recorded"; data-model §15 |
| SEC-052 | security.md names constitution 1.2.0 as the version that governs it (the header, §4, §18's tree and §22's stage 1), yet 1.3.0 amended Principle IX, which the header names. Must security.md name 1.3.0, and must FR-051's security.md version carry the change? | Align: security.md 2.13 names 1.3.0 in the header ("v1.3.0, specifically the Security Policy and Principles IV, V, VIII and IX"), in §4 ("(version 1.3.0) applies"), in §18's tree and in §22's stage 1 ("Done (constitution 1.3.0; this document 2.13)"), with a history entry saying that 1.3.0 amended only Principle IX, so the Security Policy that §4 quotes is unchanged. Statements of what happened under 1.2.0 stay (§1's "makes requirements FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-9 binding", §20's "Since constitution 1.2.0"), as do §16.1, which describes features 001–003, and the review prompt's "never commit, push or publish". The later version FR-051 writes inherits the change | B: keep 1.2.0 and add a note that 1.3.0 changed only Principle IX: the header would still name a superseded version. C: unchanged | security.md header, §4, §18, §22, §25 (version 2.13); spec FR-051 |
| RAT-040 | FR-024 and research R15 search a candidate's rotation amounts at "the D4 width", recorded before the search, but R6 fixes that width only once bounds exist for candidates of both families, and the bounds need amounts. At which width does the search run, and what happens if R6 then fixes the width at 8 after amounts were searched at 16? | Settle: each candidate's amounts are searched first at 16, the width R6 tries first, with the width recorded in its log entry before the search. If R6's rule then fixes the D4 width at 8, every entered candidate's amounts are searched again at 8, with the same recorded space of amounts, ranking measure, rounds and tie order, as a new log entry, and the scores at 8 use the amounts found at 8, so R6's "Rotation amounts are chosen at this width" holds. The production amounts are the D4-width amounts scaled (FR-024). No choice is involved, since R6's rule fixes the width, and R6 itself does not change. Both searches count against the candidate's trail row (RAT-041) | B: search at both 16 and 8 for every candidate from the start: a simpler order, at twice the search hours whatever the width. C: unchanged: amounts searched at 16 could be scored at 8 | spec FR-024; research R6 "Rotation amounts", R15 "Rotation amounts"; data-model §3 |
| RAT-044 | The algebraic-degree bounds that can raise R_dist sit in the rationale, which phase 8 writes after selection, while R_dist enters R, and R at 4×4 decides the selection in phase 5. Are the bounds per-candidate evidence computed before the round rule? | Align: the degree bounds become a record kind of data-model §5, `degree`, computed per candidate and claimable size in phase 5, after the other structural checks and before the frequency and avalanche tests (ANA-037), for Π and Π⁻¹. Each round's degree comes from the operation list (from each box's or S-box's truth table where its width allows, otherwise from the stated degree of each operation, for example w − 1 for a w-bit addition), and r rounds are bounded by the product of the per-round degrees, capped at the state size minus one, which needs no search tool. A zero-sum over a cube of dimension d + 1 then costs 2^(d + 1), so every round count whose bound d is at most 126 raises R_dist. The derivation is validated under FR-039 on a known result written and entered in the provenance log beforehand: the exact degree of a stated small operation list (for example one box at w = 4) over a few rounds, from its truth table. The rationale cites the records | B: keep the bounds in the rationale and recompute R in phase 8: a raise after selection could change which candidate the rules select. C: unchanged: a source of R_dist would arrive after the selection it feeds | spec FR-034, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule"; research R13 "Algebraic degree"; data-model §5, §9; plan phase 5; cli contract `evidence_v3.py` (kind `degree`) |
| RAT-045 | Eligibility asks for FR-018 to FR-031, applied by code before selection, but FR-026's throughput and size estimate, FR-027's operation semantics and FR-030's mapping chapter are duties of the design document, which exists only for the selected candidate. How is each item decided for every candidate? | Align: every item keeps its place, since eligibility items are only added, and each gets a form that a candidate's operation list and records show before selection: FR-026's figures computed by the cost model from the candidate's operation list, P and R (a `cost` record per size); FR-027's semantics read from the operation list's own declarations, which the framework requires for every operation; FR-030's mapping as an entry in the candidate's log, written before its first score, naming each component's place in the metaphor and the attack class, bound or need it serves.
