Data-model §9 lists each item with its evidence for every candidate; the design document restates them for the selected one, and the owner design review checks that the two agree | B: decide only the operation-list items before selection and check the document duties at the freeze: a selected candidate could then fail an item and block the freeze. C: unchanged: `round_rule.py --select` would need evidence that does not exist | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Eligibility and selection", FR-026, FR-027, FR-030; data-model §3, §9; cli contract `round_rule.py` |
| RAT-048 | FR-030 requires the mapping chapter to state what the mapping does not contribute ("any security property"), while the claim-register rule flags security wording inside that chapter. Is that statement exempt, and does the rule match whole words or stems? | Align: the rule matches whole words, ignoring case, from a stated list with its inflections: security, secure, securely, insecure, strength, strengths, strong, resist, resists, resisted, resisting, resistance, resistant, unpredictable, unpredictability, chaos, chaotic, Lyapunov, ergodic, ergodicity. "Security" joins the list, which catches "provides security". The one negative statement FR-030 requires is fixed text, quoted in FR-030 and held by one allow-list entry with that reason, and any other match in the chapter is flagged. A test runs the rule on sample sentences of both kinds | B: match stems ("secur", "resist", "unpredict"): catches more, flags the required statement too, which needs the same entry, and leaves more matches to review. C: unchanged | spec FR-030, FR-069; data-model §15 `allow_list`; research R18 (`tests/test_claims.py`) |
| PRO-041 | The spec's Assumptions still read "Only the owner commits, pushes or publishes (Principle IX)", "Constitution: 1.2.0 applies" and "constitution 1.2.0 does not require an external design review", and describe AI assistance as in features 001–003, while plan.md says the agent commits locally under the owner's standing authorization of constitution 1.3.0. Which holds? | Align: the Assumptions read: "Constitution: 1.3.0 applies"; "The agent commits locally under the owner's standing authorization (Principle IX, 1.3.0), at consistent checkpoints and with no AI co-author line; only the owner pushes, tags, releases or publishes"; "constitution 1.3.0 does not require an external design review"; and "AI assistance: as in features 001–003 (security.md §16.1), except that the agent commits locally under that authorization". The verbatim Input quotation, which cites 1.2.0, stays as the owner wrote it, and security.md §16.1 stays, since it describes features 001–003 | B: keep 1.2.0 and add a sentence on the amendment: two versions would be cited for one rule. C: unchanged | spec Assumptions ("No git branch", "External review", "Constitution", "AI assistance"); plan "Technical Context" and "Constitution Check" (cited, unchanged) |
| PRO-044 | The owner's tie choice is "recorded in that record" (`d4/tie.json`, or `selection.json` for a selection tie), while its Review & Approval row cites "the SHA-256 of the record the owner saw", which writing the choice into the record changes; `d4_choice.py --tie` writes `tie.json` once, and no command writes the choice. Which file holds the choice? | Align: the tie record stays as the script wrote it, and the choice goes into a record of its own, written by a command against the hash the owner saw, as `design_freeze.py approve` works against `draft_sha256`: `d4_choice.py --choose ID --seen SHA256 --by NAME` writes `d4/tie-choice.json`, and `round_rule.py --choose ID --seen SHA256 --by NAME` writes `freeze/selection-tie-choice.json`, each holding the choice, who, when and `seen_sha256`. Each exits 2 if that hash differs from the tie record's, if no tie exists or if a choice exists, so the choice never changes. The row cites both hashes, and "recorded in that record" reads "recorded beside it" | B: write the choice into the tie record and cite its hash before and after: the record the owner saw would no longer exist as seen. C: unchanged | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Nonlinearity choice rule", "Eligibility and selection", Review & Approval "D4 tie" and "Selection tie"; data-model §8, §9; cli contract `d4_choice.py`, `round_rule.py` |
| PRO-046 | The edge case "A distinguisher on more rounds turns up before the freeze" allows "or the candidate is dropped", while the edge case on a reason the rules do not name says a candidate cannot be dropped, and FR-041's log writer refuses any decision but `rejected` or `ineligible`. Which of these does a raised R lead to? | Align: the edge case reads: "R rises by the round rule, and every measurement the raised R needs runs as a new measurement of its kind in the same budget row (research R7). If a row cannot hold it, that evidence is inconclusive and the candidate `ineligible` at that size for a spent budget, and selection proceeds among the others within the stop rule. A raised R is never by itself a reason to drop a candidate." The log writer's list of decisions stays as it is | B: allow a recorded `withdrawn` after a raised R: a decision the rules do not name, so a result could steer which candidate stays. C: unchanged: "dropped" names a decision the log writer refuses | spec Edge Cases "A distinguisher on more rounds turns up before the freeze", FR-041; data-model §3 |
| HW-043 | FR-026's implied throughput, the lesser of r/(P·R) bits per cycle and the interface's bits per cycle, assumes that draining a block overlaps the next call of Π, while FR-025's flow control starts the next call only after the consumer has taken the block. Which holds, and what is the rate? | Align: FR-025's one-block buffer stays, and FR-026's rate counts the drain: r/(P·R + ⌈r/i⌉) bits per cycle with an i-bit output interface, plus each request's forget call and the trees' latency as now. At 4×4 with R = 32, P = 2 and i = 32 that is 768 bits in 88 cycles, about 8.7 bits per cycle, or about 0.55 × 10⁸ words per second at 200 MHz instead of 0.75 × 10⁸; FR-026's example, research R5 and `throughput_implied` (data-model §10) use the corrected formula. A design that overlaps the drain with the next call needs a second buffer, which the design document may describe as a variant with its own formula, size and clearing rule (HW-046) | B: start the next call once the block is copied into the buffer, so that the drain overlaps it: the old rate holds, with a block of output held while Π runs. C: unchanged: the reported rate overstates what the stated design gives | spec FR-026 (and its example), FR-025 "Flow control"; research R5; data-model §10 |
| HW-044 | FUT-HW-2's clock, "≥ 200 MHz, from the 100 MHz oscillator through an MMCM", needs a vendor clocking primitive, while FUT-HW-1, the hardware note's R2 and R8 and Principle III ask for vendor-neutral RTL with no instantiated vendor primitives. Where does the MMCM sit? | Align: the core is vendor-neutral and takes its clock as an input. A board wrapper of feature 008, outside the core and the only file with vendor primitives, derives the clock from the 100 MHz oscillator through an MMCM, and the reports give the wrapper's and the core's timing apart. FR-025 states the split ("the core's clock is an input; clock generation belongs to the board wrapper"), and the traceability rows of FUT-HW-1 and FUT-HW-2 carry it to features 006 and 008 | B: let the core instantiate the MMCM: breaks FUT-HW-1 and Principle III. C: unchanged: two requirements contradict each other | spec FR-025 "Key loading and reset", Security Policy traceability rows FUT-HW-1 and FUT-HW-2 |
| REP-035 | Only `fut_sec8.py` has a `--reproduce` mode, `evidence_v3.py` writes a record and appends an `evidence` entry to the candidate log on every run, and research R7 allows a run to be repeated only after a recorded tool defect, while User Story 4, research R16 and FR-061's test re-run recorded commands as written. How is any other record reproduced, and what does a reproduction write? | Align: every script that writes evidence or generated files gains `--reproduce RECORD`, which runs the recorded command's configuration with its output directory replaced by `$REP/reproductions/<record_id>/<n>/` and writes a `reproduction` record: the original's ID and SHA-256, each compared field with its outcome, the reported fields, and `match` or `differs`. It appends nothing to the candidate log, charges no budget row and is not a re-run under R7, so it can neither replace evidence nor add any; it is a heavy run where R7 says so (REP-036), and a difference is a finding, exit 1. The tests of User Story 4, research R16 and FR-061 call `--reproduce`, and data-model §1 adds the kind `reproduction` | B: re-run the recorded command as written in a scratch clone: each run would append to that clone's log and charge its budget, so results could be compared only by editing logs. C: unchanged | cli contract `evidence_v3.py`, `validate_tool.py`, the generators; data-model §1; research R7, R16; spec FR-053, FR-061, User Story 4 Independent Test |
| REP-036 | Research R7 makes every run of a budgeted kind heavy, down to the inverse test on 16 inputs, and every run with more than one worker, while User Story 4, research R16 and FR-061 re-run "the light kinds" and FR-053's test "runs one light kind with two worker counts". Which kinds are light, and is that test light? | Align: R7 lists the light kinds: those it does not budget that run with one worker in under 10 minutes (constants, vectors and constructed states, `mode_limits_v3.py`, the round rule, the D4 choice, the claim register and the re-check), and User Story 4, research R16 and FR-061 re-run those; a budgeted kind is reproduced on request, as a heavy run (REP-035). FR-053's two-worker test is a heavy run, as R7 says: it runs a stated test operation list, takes the lock, projects first, charges no candidate and is not evidence. The test suite marks it heavy, so quickstart P0 passes only while no heavy run holds the lock, and otherwise fails naming the lock | B: exempt test-suite runs on fixed test lists under 60 s from the heavy rule: looser, since R7's rule would no longer cover every budgeted kind. C: unchanged: the tests as written cannot be light | research R7 "Heavy runs", R16; spec FR-045, FR-053, FR-061, User Story 4 Independent Test; plan "Project Structure" (`test_records_004.py`); quickstart P0 |
| REP-037 | A `p_values` file may be gzip-compressed JSON, and the reproduction rule compares a `.gz` file by its `uncompressed_sha256`, which needs identical p-values, but compares p-values within a relative tolerance of 10⁻⁶. Which rule applies to a compressed `p_values` file, and how is the tolerance defined at 0 or at a subnormal value? | Align: a `p_values` file is compared by its values in either format, decompressed first, and its `uncompressed_sha256` is reported only. Two values agree when their difference is at most 10⁻⁶ times the larger of their magnitudes, or when both are below 2⁻¹⁰²², the smallest normal double, so a 0 beside a subnormal agrees. Values outside are listed, and none decides a verdict (data-model §5 "Exact verdicts"). Every other `.gz` file is still compared by its `uncompressed_sha256` | B: compare compressed p-values by hash: research R4 expects them to differ between the two environments, so every Windows–WSL reproduction would fail. C: unchanged | data-model §1 "Reproduction"; research R4, R16; spec FR-053 |
| REP-049 | The environment guard of every script exits 2 unless the interpreter and packages match one of research R1's two environments, while SC-017 and User Story 4 ask for the verdicts on "a fresh clone", which an external reviewer or a researcher would make on a machine of their own. Where may the re-check run? | Align: `recheck_004.py` is exempt from the environment guard, since it recomputes verdicts from committed counts by exact integer arithmetic (data-model §5) and imports only the standard library and `protocol/`, which a test checks. It writes a `recheck` record holding the interpreter, platform and full `pip freeze` it ran with, and says when that environment is not one of R1's. A reproduction that re-runs evidence stays limited to R1's two environments; SC-017's "fresh clone" refers to the re-check, as quickstart F3 runs it | B: keep the guard and ask a reviewer to rebuild an environment of R1 (Python 3.10.11 with numpy 2.2.6 and scipy 1.15.3, or WSL's Python 3.14.4): exact, but a high bar for an outside check. C: unchanged: a reviewer's clone could not run the re-check | cli contract "Guards shared by every script", `recheck_004.py`; research R1, R16; spec SC-017, FR-065; quickstart F3 |

From the re-run of 2026-11-01, answered the same day, "all recommended", and applied, in checklist
order:

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| RAT-036 | FR-023 asks that no two constants of one call of Π be equal, rotations or complements of one another, yet w-bit words fall into only 4 such classes at w = 4 and 20 at w = 8. Which rule holds where the classes run out? | Align: the rule reads the LFSR's words in order and skips any word that is periodic (equal to its own rotation by some k = 1…w − 1) or related to a constant already taken in the same call (equal, a rotation, a complement or a rotated complement). "Differ" and R13's word-rotation check then hold by construction wherever one call needs no more constants than there are aperiodic classes: 2 at w = 4, 16 at w = 8 and 2048 at w = 16 (counted exhaustively on 2026-11-01), and far more at w = 32, so at every claimable size. Where a call needs more, as 2×2 at w = 4 does once one call needs three, no table can meet "differ": the rule then starts its record of taken classes afresh each time every class has been used once, the script records every repeat, the size profile marks that variant, every check of R13 still runs, and no claim covers it (D3). The skip belongs to the reading order, fixed before the first score, and loosens nothing at a claimable size. Without it even w = 16 would fail by chance: 128 constants read plainly hold about 4 related pairs (4.86 × 10⁻⁴ per pair). As written, FR-023 cannot be met at w = 4 or w = 8, eligibility needs FR-018 to FR-031, and FR-019's exhaustive bijectivity needs the 2×2 table at w = 4 | B: drop w = 4 and w = 8 from the supported widths: FR-019's exhaustive bijectivity at 2×2, w = 4 and D4's fallback width of 8 would go. C: keep FR-023 as written: the script fails at those widths, no table exists there, and no candidate can be eligible; or apply "differ" at the claimable sizes only, with no skip at the reduced widths (looser at w = 16) | spec FR-023, FR-018; research R13 "Constants within a call", R15; data-model §11; the design document's size profile |
| RAT-038 | The refused list must hold the constants of every design the rationale cites, a set known only after selection, while the polynomial is fixed before the first score. How can a later citation not refuse the polynomial already chosen? | Align: the list is frozen with the rule, before the first score, its SHA-256 in the change-log entry that fixes the rule (RAT-037). It holds, each with its source, the polynomials and constant sets of R12's reference designs (SPECK32, PRESENT, SPARKLE) and of every design the strategy note, the spec, the plan, research.md and security.md name, and the standard polynomials of degree 64 and 128: CMAC's R₆₄ and R₁₂₈ (SP 800-38B), GCM's (SP 800-38D), and the CRC-64 polynomials of ISO 3309 and ECMA-182. The rule and its polynomial then never change. A design the rationale cites later is added when cited, as R15's "may only grow" allows, and the check runs again: a match fails it, as R15 says, and blocks the design freeze (FR-043); the rule is never changed to escape it. The standard polynomials matter: a plain search in ascending order finds x⁶⁴ + x⁴ + x³ + x + 1 first (computed on 2026-11-01), which is CMAC's R₆₄, a MAC's constant that re-decided feature-002 FR-007 forbids, and also the CRC-64 polynomial of ISO 3309 | B: disclose a later match in the rationale and the security target instead of failing the check (looser: R15's check would no longer fail). C: hold the polynomial open until the rationale is written (looser: the rule would be fixed after results are known) | research R15 "Refused constants", "Stricter means"; spec FR-023 and the re-decided feature-002 FR-007 row; data-model §18 |
| ANA-036 | Does the order-of-sizes guard refuse the timing probes at 8×8 and 16×16 that a 4×4 heavy run's budget check needs, so that no 4×4 heavy run could start? | Clarify: the guard counts evidence records only, and a `projection` record is not evidence: data-model §1 already says that its outputs other than timing are discarded unread and that its time counts against the row of the run it projects. A probe at 8×8 or 16×16 writes nothing but its projection record, so the 4×4 run's budget check runs as the Budget guard requires, while evidence of that kind at 8×8 or 16×16 still exits 2 until the 4×4 run has finished (quickstart E7) | B: drop the larger sizes' probes from the budget check (looser: a 4×4 run could start without the larger sizes fitting its row). C: project each larger size only when its turn comes (looser: the row could be spent by then) | cli contract "Order of sizes", "Budget"; data-model §1; research R7; quickstart E3, E7 |
| REP-029 | R3, frozen at plan approval, makes every use, the tool validation included, take its inputs from its derivation, which has no kind for random draws. Where do the bias test's 100 synthetic sets come from? | Settle: R3's derivation stays exactly as frozen, and its closed class of constructed inputs gains class 5, synthetic counts, for the bias test's validation only, as PRO-001 set up the class. Set j = 0…99 at each size draws from numpy's `Generator(PCG64(s))`, where s is the integer of the first 16 bytes of SHA-256 of `phycoweb-004/dev/synthetic/n<n>/<j>`: one `binomial(N, 1/2)` count per bit, in the test's bit order, except the planted bit at position j mod S, drawn as `binomial(N, 1/2 + δ)` at that size's δ (0.0030, 0.0031 or 0.0033). `protocol/devinputs_v3.py` stays standard-library only and gains `synthetic_seed(n, j)`; `validate_tool.py` draws the counts. The validation record holds the numpy version and each set's SHA-256, and a reproduction in the same environment draws the sets again and compares. The rule fixes every set before first use, so none can be redrawn after a result is seen | B: count bits of SHA-256 output in counter mode instead of drawing binomials: the same on every platform and version, but N bits per position and set to hash and count, a heavy run where draws cost nothing. C: numpy's legacy `RandomState(s)`, whose stream numpy keeps across versions: its binomial uses floating point, so the counts could still differ between platforms | research R3, R4; data-model §2, §7; spec FR-039, FR-040; cli contract `validate_tool.py` |

From the first run, answered on 2026-11-01, "all recommended", and applied, in checklist order:

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-010 | FUT-GRID-2 says FUT-SEC-1 and FUT-SEC-2 "MUST hold at every supported size, including the smallest"; the table says only "Met at every claimable size". Is the conflict recorded as decided, with the reason? | Align: the FUT-GRID-2 row reads "Met at every claimable size; not met at the research sizes 2×2 and 3×3, a conflict decided by D3 (FR-017; security.md §20.1, option 1)". The §20.1 decision gives the reason it weakens neither requirement: FUT-SEC-1 and FUT-SEC-2 bound a claimed strength, no claim covers a research size, each research row of the size profile states its key entropy c, its s = c/2 and status `research`, and the production interface refuses every research variant (FR-033) | B: amend the note's FUT-GRID-2 to "every claimable size": the same outcome, but it narrows a rule of the note, which needs the owner and a history entry there. C: a wider node or a larger minimum state, so that even 2×2 reaches 2s (§20.1, option 2): reopens D3, which the owner approved | spec Security Policy traceability (FUT-GRID-2), FR-017; security.md §20.1 and its 2.11 history entry |
| SEC-020 | SC-002 counts FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-6 as met by specification, while the table marks FUT-SEC-4 "Not applicable" and FR-049 keeps FUT-SEC-5's analysis open. Which reading holds? | Align: SC-002 takes the table's more careful wording. FUT-SEC-4 is met by statement: it applies "if prediction resistance is claimed", and D7 states it is not sought. FUT-SEC-5, which asks that key loading and the warm-up "MUST be analysed", is met by specification for its specified part (key loading, diffusion through instantiation, R4's first-order differences) and stays open for feature 005's analysis (FR-049). The claim register records the same split, so L1's "FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-6 met by specification" (security.md §15) is decided on one reading | B: change only SC-002's count to "FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-3, FUT-SEC-6 and FUT-SEC-9": the texts agree, but L1's wording then needs its own argument at its gate. C: treat FUT-SEC-5 as unmet until feature 005: the strictest reading of "MUST be analysed", and L1 cannot be recorded in this feature | spec SC-002, Security Policy traceability (FUT-SEC-4, FUT-SEC-5), FR-012; data-model §15 |
| RAT-033 | FR-034 puts the rationale in the versioned design document, which FR-043 freezes; the plan keeps it in a separate `rationale.md`. Is it frozen either way? | Align: keep `rationale.md` as its own file, versioned with the design document and frozen with it. FR-034 says so, FR-043's list and data-model §13 add its SHA-256, and its approval status stays outside the frozen file, as FR-034 already requires | B: merge the rationale into the design document as a chapter: one file and one hash, but a longer document for feature 006's translators to read. C: leave it unfrozen: the rationale could change after the freeze while citing frozen evidence | spec FR-034, FR-043; data-model §13; plan "Project Structure"; cli contract `design_freeze.py` |
| ANA-001 | FR-039 requires validation "before any negative result ... counts"; the protocol and SC-007 require it for every counted result. Does a found trail that raises R_dist need a prior validation record? | Stricter: one rule for every counted result, positive or negative, as SC-007 and the protocol's "Tool validation" already say. FR-039 changes "any negative result" to "any result". A positive result is never dropped for want of validation: a trail or distinguisher from a tool not yet validated is recorded as an unverified hypothesis (FR-060) and blocks the candidate's eligibility until a validated tool, or direct evaluation of the trail with the validated exact models, settles it. If confirmed, it raises R_dist. A possible weakness can therefore only raise R or block, never vanish | B: keep FR-039's "negative" and narrow SC-007 and the protocol to match (looser: it narrows a protocol rule). C: count a positive result at once and validate afterwards: a raise of R could later be withdrawn, so a result could fall away after it was seen | spec FR-039, Edge Cases "A tool fails its validation"; cli contract "Guards" (tool validation); data-model §5, §6 |
| PRO-001 | FR-040 allows only inputs "derived from public strings", yet FR-058's all-zero and single-bit keys, FR-054's chosen states, the avalanche flips and rotations and feature 002's `dev` seeds are not. Is a class of constructed inputs defined? | Clarify: R3's derivation stays exactly as frozen. Beside it, a closed class of constructed inputs is listed in R3 and data-model §2, each fixed by a public rule before first use: (1) the fixed patterns of FR-058 and R8 (all-zero, all-one, alternating, counting; the single-bit keys and nonces e_i for every i); (2) the edge states of FR-054, from the design document's list; (3) the avalanche flips x ⊕ e_j, the key and nonce bit flips and the word rotations of development inputs, for every j, bit and k the rule names; (4) feature 002's `dev` seeds for FUT-SEC-8, the one external development set. FR-040 reads "derived from public strings, or from fixed constants and development inputs, by a rule fixed in the plan". No value or index is picked after any output is seen | B: give each constructed input a name in the `dev` namespace whose value the rule fixes (for example `dev/key/zero`): the same inputs under a second naming scheme. C: exempt tests and constructed states from FR-040 without listing them (looser: a class of inputs with no fixed rule) | spec FR-040, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Development inputs"; research R3; data-model §2 |
| PRO-026 | Measuring again under a stricter rule meets the limit of one re-run "only after a recorded tool defect", and budgets may only fall. How do they fit, and what is a candidate's status when a lowered budget is below the hours used? | Settle: a stricter change is applied first by recomputing verdicts from saved outputs. New measurements it needs, such as more inputs, are a new measurement of the same kind under the changed rule, not a re-run. The old records stay, and the new hours count against the same budget row. Every firing already recorded stands, so a stricter change can only raise R or make a candidate ineligible, never undo a raise. If the row cannot hold the new run, that evidence is inconclusive and the candidate ineligible at that size, as for any spent budget. A budget lowered below the hours already used leaves zero hours: results so far stand, and no further run of that row starts | B: apply a stricter change only to candidates not yet evaluated: two candidates would be judged by different rules (PRO-025). C: no stricter change after the first result: simple, but a weakness spotted late could then not be measured before the freeze | spec FR-044, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Stop rule"; research R7; data-model §3 `budget_used_h` |
| HW-003 | R5 assumes one round per clock cycle; R11 makes each colour phase a pipeline stage. Which unit do throughput and cost use? | Clarify: one stage is one colour phase, taking one clock cycle, so a round of P phases takes P cycles (P = 2 for a red-black schedule). R5's implied rate becomes r/(P·R) bits per cycle, and its cost per output bit counts P·R cycles per call; carry chains are counted per phase, as R11 already says. The D4 score and its tie-breaks do not change, since L and the carry chains per round count per round. At 4×4 and R = 32 the implied rate falls from 1.5 × 10⁸ to 0.75 × 10⁸ words per second, below the 10⁸ goal, which is reported and never an eligibility rule | B: let a round's phases share one stage, so one round takes one cycle and its chains count over the whole round. R5's figures stand, but FR-025's limit of two chains per stage then fails any candidate with more than two serial additions per round (stricter for eligibility). C: report both figures: two numbers for one quantity, with the choice between them made after the results | research R5, R11; spec FR-025 (a stage defined), FR-026; data-model §10 `throughput_implied` |
| HW-009 | R15 makes the clock, reset and the round and phase counters global, but the plan names only the absorption and output trees as Principle III's exception. Is a timing justification required? | Add: the plan's Complexity Tracking gains a row with the justification Principle III asks for. The clock uses the device's clock network. Reset is synchronous and reaches the nodes through a registered fan-out tree whose latency is part of the reset sequence (FR-025). The round and phase counters are replicated, one copy at each food node or node, all started by that reset and running in lockstep, so no counter value crosses the grid. R15's "global" sentence changes to match | B: keep one counter set and distribute its values through a registered fan-out tree, latency compensated and justified like the output tree. C: treat clock, reset and counters as exempt without a justification: Principle III names no exemption | plan "Complexity Tracking"; research R15; spec FR-025 |
| HW-010 | Re-decided FR-001 forbids comparison on secret data, but `KeyMismatch` compares each instantiate's key with the bound key. Do they agree? | Add: take the key at construction, `Generator(variant_id, key)`, with `instantiate(nonce, pers=b"")` using it. The constructor runs the self-test and zeroizes, then holds the key in the object until `zeroize`, which clears it. No key is ever compared, `KeyMismatch` goes, and the per-key counters stay with the object. In hardware the key register is loaded once and zeroized with the state (HW-014). FR-013's instantiate keeps its inputs: the key enters the state at every instantiate, from the stored copy | B: keep `instantiate(key, nonce)` and the check, made constant-time (for example `hmac.compare_digest`) and stated as an interface check outside Π and the mode's datapath; the bound key is still retained and zeroized. C: one instantiate per object, with per-key limits as usage rules only (looser than the answered choice "One key per `Generator`: kept and tightened") | model contract "Generator" and "Exceptions"; spec FR-013, FR-058; plan "Other choices" (a note on the answered choice) |
| HW-018 | Constructed-state vectors start from chosen states, but production may have no state-load or read-back port. How do FR-025's "no read-back path" and FUT-HW-4's equivalence both hold? | Add: constructed states run on the RTL in simulation only, loaded by the testbench (VHDL-2008 external names or `force`), so the synthesizable RTL has no load or read-back port. On the board, FUT-HW-4's equivalence uses the vectors a key reaches, through the normal interface. The record of an edge state no key reaches says it is covered in simulation only, as FR-054's edge case already marks such states | B: a load port behind a generic that defaults off, with a separate test bitstream: a second build to keep apart from production, and a port that one wrong generic would expose. C: no RTL use of constructed states (looser: FR-054's vectors would serve no implementation) | spec FR-025, FR-054, FR-056 (the mapping to features 006 and 008); design document interface chapter |
| HW-033 | FUT-GRID-1's expected status is "Met by specification and model", yet R14 leaves n = 5, 6, 7 and 9…15 without vectors, R_f, R_b or bounds, as open items against FUT-GRID-1. Which holds? | Align: the row reads "Partly met: the generics cover n = 2…16 in the model, the supported sizes are listed, and n = 5, 6, 7 and 9…15 are open items with their reason (FR-018)". FR-018 already foresees such open items, so the table follows it | B: commit Π vectors, R_f and R_b and the symmetry checks at every n from 2 to 16, with bounds still only at claimable sizes, so the status holds as written. The cost is eleven more sizes inside the 6 h structural row, which may only fall. C: leave both as they are: the table and R14 keep disagreeing | spec Security Policy traceability (FUT-GRID-1); final report open items |
| REP-003 | `fut_sec8.py` exits 2 on a second run unless `--repeat-after-defect` names a defect, yet FR-053 wants every record reproducible. How does a reviewer reproduce the sweep without a repeat? | Add: two modes, neither a demonstration attempt. `--verify RECORD` re-checks every listed three-word match and every recovered seed with `model_v2` in seconds, with no sweep. `--reproduce RECORD`, allowed only after the FUT-SEC-8 result review, re-runs the sweep as a heavy run and writes a `fut-sec-8-reproduction` record compared field by field with the original; it can neither replace the demonstration record nor change the review, and a difference is a finding. A plain second run still exits 2 (quickstart U4) | B: `--verify` only: the counts after one and two words could then be re-checked only by a new attempt. C: allow a plain second run (looser: it gives up "one pass per target") | cli contract `scripts/fut_sec8.py`; data-model §4 (a reproduction kind); spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "FUT-SEC-8 demonstration" (Budget row, a sentence that a reproduction after the review is not a repeat) |
| REP-015 | Generated files record their command, measured speed and generation time, yet `--check` fails on any byte difference, and those fields differ between runs and between Windows and Linux. Where do they go? | Add: generated files hold only deterministic content, as data-model §11's header example already does. The command (script and arguments, relative to the repository root), the interpreter, the model's SHA-256, the platform, the measured speed and the generation time go to a sidecar record per generator run, `vectors/phyco-v3/MANIFEST.json` or an evidence record under `$REP/vectors/`, which `--check` does not compare. FR-036's "measured speed stated" is met by the sidecar | B: keep the fields in the files and mask them in `--check`: the frozen hashes then cover bytes that change on every run. C: keep the script, its arguments and the model's SHA-256 in each header, all deterministic, and put speed, time and platform in the sidecar: self-describing files, but every model edit, even to a comment, rewrites every vector file | cli contract "Generators of constants and vectors"; data-model §11; research R8; spec FR-036 |

## Tenth re-run of 2026-11-03

The 22 items other than conflicts that the tenth re-run of 2026-11-03 found over the complete
documents and the repository, in checklist order: 3 security, 4 design-rationale, 6 analysis, 4
protocol, 3 hardware and 2 reproducibility. They were answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the
recommended options, and applied, as were the 6 conflicts above.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-093 | FR-069 compares "a bound or a claim stated in a disclaimer", line by line, with the register entries of the models the line names, a bound being beyond the register's when an entry holds `none` or a bound below the line's, while README's line on the seed holds three numbers of bits, "64-bit seed", "at 64 bits" and "the 112-bit minimum", of which only the second bounds the models whose seed it describes, so that a test reading each number of bits as a bound fails README's own line. Which text does the test read as a bound and which as a claim, may a figure that bounds no model stand in a block, and how is a negation read? | Add: FR-069 states the forms. Every number of bits in a block, written "<d> bits", "<d>-bit" or as a power of 2, is read as a bound of the models the line names, so that a figure that bounds no model, such as a seed's size or a standard's minimum, may not stand in a block; README's line on the seed is reworded in plan phase 1, with SEC-091's change, to state only the cap at 64 bits of the models it names, the seed's size and the 112-bit minimum standing in README's section 5, which states both, outside the block (SEC-095). A claim is any other use, in any inflection, of "secure", "security level", "cryptographic security", "strength", "resist" or "claim", and it is read as negated only inside the phrases FR-069 lists word for word, "no claim", "do not show cryptographic security" and "does not show cryptographic security"; any other such use in a block is a claim of the models the line names, a finding while an entry it names holds "no claim". `tests/test_claims.py` covers a figure that bounds no model, each phrase and a negation outside them. The protocol change log's entry marks as `set_aside` the reading under which every use of a claim word is a claim, its negations included, under which the disclaimers' own sentences would be findings and the test could never pass (PRO-079). | B: the test reads as a bound only "at <d> bits" and "at most <d> bits", a seed's size and a standard's minimum standing as figures, and reads as negated any claim word in a sentence that holds "no", "not" or "never" (looser). C: unchanged: the rule names no form, and read with every number of bits as a bound it fails README's own line. | spec FR-069; plan phase 1; cli contract `check_claims.py`; README's status block and section 5 in plan phase 1, with SEC-091 |
| SEC-094 | FR-069 has a flagged line pass "only through an allow-list entry in the register", an entry the agent drafts and the owner reviews at the next ⛔ gate, while each disclaimer is decided "by the rule on a level, claim or bound beyond the register's", whose part on a level is the level rule, a rule that flags lines as the others do, so that a line entry for a disclaimer line naming a level above `claim_level` would pass it, and the comparison of a bound or a claim, the fixed sentence and the checks of the register against the rows and the files are stated as findings with no word on entries. Which of the test's findings can an entry pass? | Add: inside the three disclaimer blocks no allow-list entry passes any finding, of a wording rule, of the level rule, of the comparison of a bound or a claim or of the fixed sentence, and nowhere does an entry pass a finding of the checks of the register's level, `bound`, `evidence`, `in_progress` and `history` against the rows and the files, so that the blocks and the register stand only as the rules give them; outside the blocks entries pass, as now, the flags of the wording rules and of the level rule; the test exits 1 on an entry that names a line of a block or a finding of the register's checks, and `tests/test_claims.py` covers each case. | B: an entry may pass any finding, each with its reason, the owner reviewing it at the next ⛔ gate (looser). C: unchanged: the rules name no finding an entry cannot pass, and a line entry can pass the level rule's flag on a block's line. | spec FR-069; data-model §15; cli contract `check_claims.py` |
| SEC-095 | FR-069 has the test check that "README, security.md §1.1 and §15, and the disclaimers agree with the register", while a comparison is stated only for §15's line and for the disclaimers; §1.1's column "Claim level (section 15)" reads "L0; no claim" for each model and holds no row for `phyco-v3` until FR-051's security.md version in plan phase 8, while the register holds `phyco-v3`'s entry from plan phase 1; README, outside its status block, states a bound in its passage on the seed's size; and FR-012 and data-model §15 have the agent write `claim_level`, `updated`, `history` and §15's line, naming no change to §1.1. How does the test compare §1.1 and README with the register, and who keeps §1.1 in step? | Add: the test reads §1.1's table row by row, its first column's model ID against the register's entries and the leading level and claim of the cell under "Claim level (section 15)", "L<k>; no claim" or "L<k>; <claim>", against `claim_level` and `claim`, a register entry with no row, a row with no entry or a cell that differs being a finding; `phyco-v3`'s row joins §1.1 in plan phase 1, in the security.md version that adds its line to §15, and FR-012 and data-model §15 have the agent change §1.1's row in the same commit as §15's line, which FR-051's version keeps. Outside the status block the test reads every README line by the level rule, and every line that holds "security level", "strength", "claim" or a level by the bound and claim rules of a disclaimer's line (SEC-093); the lines they flag, the passage on the seed's size among them, are reworded in plan phase 1 with SEC-093's change, so that the test passes from that phase. | B: the test compares §1.1's level only and reads README outside the block by the level rule alone (looser). C: unchanged: FR-069 names §1.1 and README with no comparison for either, and §1.1 lags the register from plan phase 1 to plan phase 8. | spec FR-012, FR-051, FR-069; data-model §15; plan phases 1 and 8; cli contract `check_claims.py`; security.md §1.1 and README in plan phase 1, with SEC-093 |
| RAT-087 | FR-034 "Version" fixes the design document's header, its title, the disclaimer and "Version: 0.<k>", and nothing else, `create` exiting 2 on any other header line, and says of `rationale.md` only that it "carries the same version", while FR-068 has `rationale.md` carry the design-form disclaimer, no rule or test reads a version line there, and "Each committed change before the freeze makes the next 0.<k>", which for a change to `rationale.md` alone would change the design document's header or leave the two files' versions apart; and the freeze record's "External review" and the final report list the design-document versions since the version that review read. recommended options, and applied, as were the 3 conflicts above.

## Eighth re-run of 2026-11-02

The 21 items other than conflicts that the eighth re-run of 2026-11-02 found over the complete
documents and the repository, in checklist order: 4 security, 4 design-rationale, 3 analysis, 1
protocol, 5 hardware and 4 reproducibility. They were answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the
recommended options, and applied, as were the 4 conflicts above.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |

## Seventh re-run of 2026-11-02

The 33 items other than conflicts that the seventh re-run of 2026-11-02 found over the complete
documents and the repository, in checklist order: 6 security, 6 design-rationale, 3 analysis, 3
protocol, 6 hardware and 9 reproducibility. They were answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the
recommended options, and applied, as were the 4 conflicts above.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-078 | FR-043 holds "the security-target review and every later review" again when a later selection record selects another candidate, and the notes under the Review & Approval table have `design_freeze.py create` read the external review's family "in the rows held for the candidate that the latest selection record selects", while the row's notes say only that the owner "may have the changes reviewed in a row of its own", a row reading "not arranged" names nothing, and `create` exits 2 only while a done row names no manifest, or one that neither `review-package/MANIFEST.json` nor its `versions/` holds. Is the external review tied to the candidate of the latest selection record, and to which candidate does a "not arranged" row apply? | Align: each row of the external review's family names the selection record it applies to, a done row through the manifest it names, which carries that record, and a "not arranged" row by naming the latest selection record when it is written; `create` reads the highest-k row that names the latest selection record and exits 2 while none does, so that a new selection record needs a row of its own, "External design review <k>", done or "not arranged", for the candidate it selects; a review done for another candidate stays done in its row and is cited, in the freeze record's "External review" and in the final report, for the candidate and the version it read only (SC-013); the row's notes, the notes under the table, data-model §13 and §17 and the cli contract's `design_freeze.py` say so | B: a review done for an earlier candidate stands for the freeze, the freeze record naming the candidate and the version it read (looser). C: unchanged: a done row for another candidate passes `create`, and a "not arranged" row names no candidate | spec FR-043, Review & Approval "External design review" and the notes under the table; data-model §13, §17; cli contract `design_freeze.py` |

What becomes of a recorded L1 when what it rests on changes before a later row is added? | Align: L1 holds at HEAD only while what its rows name holds there: `check_claims.py` compares each hash that the review rows named by "L1 recorded" or "L1 recorded <k>" name, the chapter's version item by item, the FUT-SEC-8 report and the demonstration record, with the files at HEAD, and gives L0 while any differs, so that a change to the reviewed set makes L1 lapse from the commit that makes it, as a later row does; the agent writes the register's return to L0 in that commit, or, where an owner's commit makes the change, in a commit of its own right after it, `history` keeping the earlier L1 with its dates, its rows and the version reviewed, until "L1 recorded <k>" records L1 again; FR-012, FR-069, the notes under the table, data-model §15 and the cli contract's `check_claims.py`, with its test, say so | B: L1 stands for the version reviewed until a later row is added, the register naming that version and security.md §15's line saying that L1 holds for it (looser). Who writes `in_progress`, in which commit, and can it be checked? | Add: the agent writes `in_progress` in the commit that writes the first counted trail-bound record, naming L2 with that record's date and "not reached", and `in_progress` is `none` before it; `check_claims.py` checks it against the records at HEAD and exits 1 while `in_progress` names L2 with no counted trail-bound record, names another start date, or is `none` once one exists; `tests/test_claims.py` covers each case; FR-012, FR-069, data-model §15 and the cli contract's `check_claims.py` say so | B: `in_progress` is dropped from the register, L2's start being read from the first counted trail-bound record's date where it is needed. | SEC-081 | The plan's project structure lists `protocol/claims.json`, `scripts/check_claims.py` and `tests/test_claims.py`, and no phase names them, while plan phase 8 records L1 "when its gate passes", after which the agent writes `phyco-v3`'s entry and its §15 line "right after the owner's commit", before "the security.md version of FR-051", which gives `phyco-v3` the register entry that security.md §15, listing `float-v0`, `lut-v1` and `phyco-v2` alone, lacks, and phase 9 lists the "claim-register test". | SEC-083 | FR-006 says "The behaviour on a repeated nonce MUST be stated (T7)", and the edge case "Nonce reuse" and the model contract state one case, "the same key, nonce and personalization string give the same output", while security.md's T7 is a nonce repeated under one key "by error or by force", against the goals "distinguish, recover the key or the state". | RAT-072 | Research R6 sends both representatives of an exact D4 tie on "to the production-width analysis within their own budgets" until `d4_choice.py --tie` applies the tie-breaks once both values of R at 4×4 exist, while the rule that ends a candidate of the family D4 did not choose, `ineligible` for each one "not analysed at the production width", applies from the moment "Once D4 has chosen a family" and fits no candidate analysed at 4×4, and `round_rule.py --close` writes `set-closed` only once every candidate has a final decision. | ANA-074 | The cli contract runs a uniform control once per test and size, "before that test's first use at that size by any candidate", and "only if every entered candidate's 12 h row can hold its hours", since every candidate's row is charged them "whether the control ran for it or before it" (research R7), while a budget lowered below the hours already used "leaves zero hours", so that the row of a candidate already `rejected` or `ineligible`, which will never use that size, can keep the control from running, and with it every other candidate's test at that size. documents and the repository, in checklist order: 3 security, 2 design-rationale, 1 analysis, 4
protocol, 4 hardware and 6 reproducibility. They were answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the
recommended options, and applied, as were the 2 conflicts above.

| HW-078 | FR-025 gives "a registered error-code output beside the error signal and valid with it", so that "the host can tell from the ports which refusals zeroized", while no text says how long the signal and its code stay valid after a refusal, what a second refusal does to a code the host has not read, or whether the error-code register is kept through the zeroization that a zeroizing refusal starts: "Every trigger drives the reset's registered fan-out tree, so a zeroization reaches every register within the reset's latency", the zeroization list neither names nor excepts the register, and the error output, unlike the four interfaces, each of which "uses valid and ready", has no ready from the host. Is the error output's lifetime stated? | Add: the error signal and its code are registered and stay valid from the refusal until the next command word is accepted on the command interface, or until a reset, which returns both to zero, their value after reset in the port list; a refusal follows only a command word or an input word of the call it ends, so a code is replaced only once the host has sent another command, after as many cycles as it likes to read it; the zeroization list names the error signal and the error-code register as the registers that a zeroizing refusal's zeroization keeps, so that its code stays readable, while every other trigger clears them with the rest; the host reads the code from the ports with no handshake, as it reads `selftest_fail`; FUT-HW-5's tests cover a code kept through its zeroization and a code replaced by the next command; FR-025's "Zeroization", "Flow control" and "Registers and read-back" say so | B: give the error output its own valid and ready, the code held until the host takes it and the command interface's ready low while a code waits. C: unchanged: the code's lifetime is unstated, and a zeroizing refusal's own zeroization may clear the code it raised | spec FR-025 "Zeroization", "Flow control", "Registers and read-back" |
| REP-076 | Records are "written by one module, `protocol/evidence.py`", which keeps a fixed-name file's content under `versions/` before it changes, while the cli contract gives a command for the downloads manifest's `owner-provided` entries only (`owner_record.py`), and no text names who writes its `approved` entries, held "from setup on", or changes an entry to `fetched`, "with its SHA-256, download date and local path", or to `not obtained`, "with the reason", although `limits.json` cites each theorem paper "by its `fetched` entry's SHA-256" and `validate_tool.py`'s source guard reads a `fetched` entry. Who writes these entries, from what, and what does the writer refuse? | Add: a script of its own, `scripts/downloads.py`, writing through `protocol/evidence.py` and run by the agent: `init` writes one `approved` entry per file of research R19's two tables, as research.md holds them at HEAD, with its name, source, size and licence, and exits 2 once the manifest exists; `fetched NAME PATH --pypi-sha256 HEX` computes the SHA-256 and the size from the downloaded file itself, read under `-I` from its own directory and never run, refuses a size that differs from a size R19 gives in bytes and, for a wheel, a SHA-256 that differs from PyPI's for that file, and writes the entry `fetched` with its SHA-256, the download date and the local path; `not-obtained NAME --reason TEXT` writes `not obtained` with the reason; each refuses, exit 2, writing nothing, a name that no `approved` entry holds, and keeps the manifest's earlier content under `versions/` first; `owner_record.py owner-provided` stays the owner's own act; research R19, the data-model introduction, the cli contract and the plan's Project Structure say so | B: `scripts/owner_record.py` gains modes for these entries, run by the owner. C: unchanged: no writer is named for the manifest's `approved`, `fetched` and `not obtained` entries | research R19; data-model introduction; cli contract `scripts/downloads.py`; plan Project Structure |
| REP-077 | FR-036 says "The generators run in a fixed order, each `--check` passing before the next": the constant tables, then the vectors with `phycoweb/selftest_v3.py`, and only then `Generator`, as the cli contract and data-model §11 do in three steps, while `gen_constructed_v3.py`, which writes the constructed-state vectors "from chosen states as well as from development keys" (FR-054) and has its own check (quickstart M3), is in none of them, though it reads the constant tables and runs the mode; and the freeze hashes "the scripts that generate the constants and the vectors" without naming it. Where does the third generator run, and does the freeze hash it? | Align: the order gains a fourth step, `gen_constructed_v3.py`, after step (3)'s check, running Π, Π⁻¹ and the mode through `pi`, `pi_inv` and `ResearchDuplex` only, its research path to chosen states included (REP-078), so that every generator and module it reads has passed its check before it runs; "the scripts that generate the constants and the vectors" are named, `gen_constants_v3.py`, `gen_vectors_v3.py` and `gen_constructed_v3.py`, all three hashed by the freeze; FR-036, FR-043, data-model §11 and §13 and the cli contract's "Generators of constants and vectors" say so | B: run it as step (3), after the vectors' check and before `Generator`'s, since it runs no self-test. C: unchanged: the order omits it, and the freeze's list leaves it unnamed | spec FR-036, FR-043; data-model §11, §13; cli contract "Generators of constants and vectors" |
| REP-078 | FR-054 asks for constructed-state vectors of "the first and last value of every counter, every output limit reached and one request beyond it", FR-058 tests "a call refused at a per-key limit", and quickstart M5 expects "refusals beyond every limit", while the model contract gives the mode only `Generator` and `ResearchDuplex`, which has "The same calls and rules as `Generator`", so that a counter reaches its last value only through the calls it counts: up to 2³² instantiations per key, at about 1 ms per call of Π at 4×4 (research R8), months of calls. Through which interface do the vectors and the tests reach the mode's edge states? | Add: the research interface gains `ResearchDuplex.from_state(variant_id, key, lanes, counters)`, research-only, which starts the mode from a chosen state and chosen counter values, each within its range, and refuses any other with `ValueError`; `Generator` has no such path, and a test checks that it has none and that `Generator` and `ResearchDuplex` enforce every limit through one shared function, so that a refusal reached through `from_state` tests the rule `Generator` applies; the design document's list of edge states marks each as reached by the normal calls from development keys within a stated number of calls of Π, or covered only from a chosen state, and `gen_constructed_v3.py` writes each vector the way the list names, the vector saying which; FR-058's refusals at a per-key or per-instantiation limit and quickstart M5's refusals beyond every limit are tested through `from_state`, every other path through the normal calls; FR-054, FR-058, the model contract's "Research duplex" and "Per-key limit", data-model §11 and quickstart M5 say so | B: give the research sizes a variant with reduced limits, for example 2⁴ instantiations per key at 2×2, so that the normal calls reach every limit there, the claimable sizes' limits checked only by their formulas (looser). C: unchanged: the edge states at the limits are reachable only through months of calls | spec FR-054, FR-058; model contract "Research duplex", "Per-key limit"; data-model §11; quickstart M5 |
| REP-079 | `provenance.py` refuses "a `sha256` that is not that of the named file, the named section's text or the named record", and a review entry holds "the SHA-256 of the section text reviewed", while no passage says where a section's text starts and ends, or whether its heading line and final LF are in it, and an entry holds no hash kind beside its `sha256`, as a record's hashes do ("Every SHA-256 a record holds is recorded beside its kind"), although FR-051 changes security.md and README.md, existing `text=auto` files that research R16 hashes over their LF-normalized content. Which bytes does a provenance entry's `sha256` cover? | Add: an entry's `sha256` is over the named file's bytes, LF-normalized for an existing `text=auto` file such as security.md or README.md and raw otherwise, as research R16 hashes, with `sha256_kind` (`raw` or `lf-normalized`) beside it; a section's text runs from its heading line, included, to the line before the next heading of the same or a higher level, or to the file's end, each line with its final LF, taken from those bytes, and `section` names its heading as it stands; a review entry's `sha256` is computed the same way, and `provenance.py` computes both and refuses, exit 2, an entry whose `sha256` or kind differs; data-model §14 and the cli contract's `provenance.py` say so | B: each entry names a commit and hashes the file's blob there, a section by its heading's line range at that commit, with no kind. | REP-080 | FR-067 records "Every file of this feature drafted or repaired with an AI tool" with "the tool and model, the date, the task ID and its review status", and plan phase 1 creates the provenance log, while the spec, the plan, research, the data model, the contracts, the quickstart, the checklists and checklist-choices.md were drafted and repaired with an AI tool over several sessions before it and before any task existed, as are the phase's files written before the log; `provenance.py add` takes every field but `seq`, `prev_sha256` and `date_utc`, "which the writer adds", and the commits carry no AI co-author line, so that "the history cannot show who wrote" them. Are these files in FR-067's scope, and what does each entry hold? | Add: they are in FR-067's scope: right after the log's creation, before any other entry, `provenance.py add` writes one entry per such file, with `sha256` and its kind (REP-079) of the file as committed then, `date_utc` as the writer adds it, `drafted_from` and `drafted_to`, the dates of the first and last commits that changed it, `session` "several, not recorded" unless one is known, `tool` and `model` as known and otherwise "not recorded", `task_id` `none` for a file drafted before tasks.md existed and the task's ID for a file of plan phase 1, and `review_status` `pending` until the owner reviews it, a review entry following then; the entries, not the history, state that an AI tool drafted them; a later change to such a file gets an entry as any file does; FR-067, data-model §14, plan phase 1 and the cli contract's `provenance.py` say so | B: leave the planning documents outside FR-067's scope, each stating in its own text that it was drafted with an AI tool. C: unchanged: the scope and the fields of these entries are unstated | spec FR-067; data-model §14; plan "Phases and gates"; cli contract `provenance.py` |
| REP-081 | Plan phase 1 lists the Hypothesis wheels "downloaded, checked against PyPI's SHA-256 and installed" before the downloads manifest that holds each file's entry "from setup on", and the environment records before "corrected `requirements.txt`" and `protocol/evidence.py`, while `owner_record.py environment` writes a record only when its Python and pinned packages match R1 and "the pins of `requirements.txt` for that platform" (research R1), R18's pins "enter `requirements.txt` only then", after the wheels' install (research R19), FR-058 has "a new environment record" approved once the wheels are installed, which presumes one before them, and `protocol/evidence.py`, which writes the fixed-name files, writes nothing under the guard "Protocol log" while the protocol change log does not exist. Is the order of the phase's steps stated where one needs another? | Align: plan phase 1 states its order: (1) directories, the `.gitattributes` pins, the `.gitignore` entries and R18's line-ending test; (2) the writers before what they write: `DESIGN_DISCLAIMER`, `protocol/evidence.py`, `protocol/provenance.py` with `scripts/provenance.py`, `protocol/changelog.py` with `scripts/protocol_change.py`, `scripts/clear_lock.py`, `scripts/owner_record.py` and `scripts/downloads.py` (REP-076); (3) the provenance log, with REP-080's entries; (4) the protocol change log, after the owner's confirmation of every list at the tasks approval, before every file written through `protocol/evidence.py` but a `lock-cleared` record (PRO-065); (5) the downloads manifest's `approved` entries, before any download; (6) the wheels, each downloaded into its own new directory, checked against PyPI's SHA-256, recorded `fetched` and installed under `-I`, then R18's pins written into the corrected `requirements.txt`; (7) the environment records of both platforms, matching those pins, each with its row; (8) `protocol/devinputs_v3.py`; (9) the `phyco-v2` archive; FR-058 reads that the first environment records are written once the wheels are installed and the pins take effect, and that a wheel installed after a record needs a new record, approved before any later record; research R1 and R19 point to the plan's order | B: keep the order as written, the guard "Protocol log" exempting the environment records and the manifest before the log exists (looser). documents and the repository, in checklist order: 1 security, 1 design-rationale, 2 analysis, 2
protocol, 1 hardware and 1 reproducibility. They were answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the
recommended options, and applied, as were the 3 conflicts above.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |

| SEC-074 | A review held again, after a decline, for the changed parts after an approval or for another candidate, is a numbered row of its own, "FUT-SEC-8 result review <k>" or "Security-target review <k>", naming what it reviews, while "L1 recorded" is one unnumbered row that the owner sets "once both reviews read Approved", naming neither those rows nor what they reviewed, and `check_claims.py` reads only that row. What becomes of a recorded L1 when either review is held again? | Add: "L1 recorded" names the two review rows it rests on, by family and k, with what each reviewed by path and SHA-256 (the demonstration record; the chapter's version); once a later row of either family is added, L1 is recorded again in a row of its own, "L1 recorded <k>" (k = 2, 3, … in order), when the later rows read Approved, naming them; `check_claims.py` reads that family's row with the highest k at HEAD and fails, exit 1, while the register gives `phyco-v3` a level above L0 unless that row reads Approved and names the highest-k row of each review family, both Approved; `claim_level` holds the level whose gate holds at HEAD, so that the register returns to L0 while a review held again is pending, with the earlier L1 and its dates kept in the entry's `history`; the first list of rows a guard reads, its test and data-model §15 and §17 name the family | B: L1, once recorded, stays the highest level reached, and the register notes beside it each review held again. C: unchanged: the register can keep L1 on a review that a later row has superseded | spec FR-012, FR-069, Review & Approval "L1 recorded" and the notes under the table; data-model §15, §17; cli contract "Approvals", `check_claims.py` |

| ANA-070 | Research R12 states the ARX search's validated kind and says only "Each other tool's kind states its widths in the same way", while the active-S-box counter's known result is PRESENT's (4-bit S-boxes and a bit permutation), the long-trail counter's SPARKLE's (ARX-boxes on 32-bit words) and the exhaustive tables' the 4-bit PRESENT S-box, and their uses lie elsewhere: boxes of 8 and 16 bits at w = 4 and 8, the cross-checks' internal-trail search, the D4 width, 16 or 8, and the Boolean candidates' layers at 4×4 with w = 4 and at the D4 and production widths. What are these tools' validated kinds, and which known result covers each use? | Settle: research R12's table and data-model §7 state each tool's validated kind as they state the ARX search's, its word widths, operation set and kind of model, and list beside it each use this plan makes of it with the known result that covers it: the active-S-box counter (PRESENT: 4-bit S-boxes, a bit permutation) for the Boolean candidates' layers at 4×4 with w = 4 and at the D4 and production widths; the long-trail counter (SPARKLE: ARX-boxes on 32-bit words) at the D4 width and at the production width; the exhaustive tables (the 4-bit PRESENT S-box) for boxes of 8 and 16 bits at w = 4 and 8 and for the cross-checks' internal-trail search; the cross-checks cover operation sets only (ANA-069); a use that no known result covers gets a further known result, fixed with its source before the tool's first validation, from research R19's approved papers where one of its kind is there, or else self-written and logged before the tool is drafted, for example the difference and linear tables of (x, y) ↦ (x ⊞ y, y) at w = 4 and 8, whose entries the formulas of Lipmaa and Moriai and of Wallén give exactly, and a use without one writes `not evaluated` | B: treat the counters and the tables as generic in width and box size, so that one known result covers every use (looser). | PRO-061 | FR-044, data-model §18 and the cli contract compare each rule's text "at the two commits of each round", each named by its hash, while only the fifth list names its commits (812415c and 2826842); the first run's and the re-run's rows were applied in commits that hold other edits too (f8c6680, f9b2e92); it is not stated whether a round's alignment commits fall inside its pair; and the comparison runs only in `create`, after the owner has confirmed every list at the tasks approval, so that a place it finds changed without a row comes to light after the confirmation it should inform. | PRO-062 | FR-044 says "The log is created, and no evidence run starts, only after the owner has confirmed every list", while no guard shared by every script refuses an evidence run while `research/004/protocol-changes.jsonl` does not exist or lacks its approved first entries, and data-model §1 and research R16 give a record's `protocol_seq` and `protocol_line_sha256` the value `none` "before the log exists", which reads as allowing evidence records then. ## Fourth re-run of 2026-11-02

The 25 items other than conflicts that the fourth re-run of 2026-11-02 found over the complete
documents and the repository, in checklist order: 9 security, 5 design-rationale, 6 analysis, 1
protocol, 1 hardware and 3 reproducibility. They were answered in advance on 2026-11-02 with the
recommended options, and applied, as were the 11 conflicts above.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-064 | FR-050 forbids describing any model as "secure", "unbreakable" or "cryptographically secure", SC-015 counts those words and FR-069 requires the claim test to report any use of the wording FR-050 forbids, while FR-069's rules and data-model §15 flag them only beside a number of bits or inside the mapping chapter, so that a line such as "phyco-v3 is unbreakable" passes. Does the test flag them in every line it scans, and are the uses that stay allowed named? | Add: the claim test flags "secure", "securely" and "unbreakable", "cryptographically secure" among their uses, in every line it scans, as it flags "random"; standing phrases, each with its reason, cover the rule lines of FR-050 and SC-015 and the negative statements, such as the README's "**not** cryptographically secure", and any other flagged line needs an entry of its own; a flagged word quoted in the checklists and checklist-choices.md passes as now; and the sample sentences gain "phyco-v3 is unbreakable", which must be flagged | B: flag them only beside a model ID, "the model", "the generator" or "the design", as "DRBG" is. C: unchanged: a sentence calling a model unbreakable passes away from a number of bits | spec FR-069, SC-015; data-model §15 |

Does a guard keep `claim_level` from reading L1 while that row is not Approved at HEAD? | Add: `check_claims.py` reads the "L1 recorded" row as committed at HEAD and fails, exit 1, while `protocol/claims.json` gives `phyco-v3` a level above L0 and the row does not read Approved; the row joins the first list, set by the owner, and the guard list and its test name it | B: report the disagreement in the claim test without making the row an owner's act. What is the outcome when a paper a mode theorem rests on cannot be obtained or read, and is another copy a new download? | Settle: a paper that cannot be obtained or read leaves its theorem `unverified`, recorded in `downloads.json` as `not obtained` with the reason. Where another theorem of research R10's list covers the same call and its paper is on disk and read, the mode chapter uses it, in a new version reviewed again; where none does, no published theorem's preconditions can be shown met, and the feature recommends stopping under the stop rule. Another copy of a paper, from any source, is a new download that needs the owner's permission for that file (R19) | B: stay open, "L1 recorded" blocked, until the owner obtains the paper, with no end stated. Is the design document's version defined, with a header that holds no approval or review status? | Add: the header holds the title, the disclaimer and "Version: 0.<k>", 1.0 once frozen, as data-model §17's draft versions state, and nothing else, and `rationale.md` carries the same version; each committed change before the freeze makes the next 0.<k>, and a revision after a decline or after the owner design review is logged under the candidate with its old and new version; `protocol/evidence.py` reads `design_version` from that line; the first record that cites a version stores it as `design-versions/<sha256>.md`, as `spec-versions/` does; and `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 on any other header line, which a test checks | B: no version in the header, the version being the git history and the provenance log alone. Does the sentence cover every outcome of the D4 record? | Align: the spec's selection sentence gains "or, where the D4 record's outcome is `not computable`, of both families", as research R6 and data-model §9 state, and the cli contract's `--select` names that case beside the fallback | B: leave the case to the D4 rule's own sentence, "both families are analysed", and to FR-043's decline. The log writer closes a `replacement` entry for "the budget spent" only when R7's rows and the protocol change log's entry that lowered the budget show it, and exits 2 otherwise | B: drop "the budget allows", so that a replacement is always entered while its family has fewer than three. Must the chapter cite every record that decided the outcome? | Add: the Alternatives chapter cites, by record ID or by path and SHA-256, `d4/choice.json`, and `d4/tie.json` and `d4/tie-choice.json` where they exist, every selection record with its tie choice, each decline's log entry and, for each candidate not selected, the entry or record that decided it, so that every reason it gives traces to its record | B: cite the latest selection record only, which chains to the earlier ones.
