| ANA-061 | Research R4 and data-model §7 count the sets of each family in which the bias test fires, 82 to 97 of 100, while each set also holds S − 1 unplanted bits, each tested at 0.005/S, so that a family of 100 sets holds a firing of an unplanted bit with probability up to about 1 − 0.995¹⁰⁰, near 0.39. Is a set counted only when its planted bit fires, and is an unplanted firing reported, counted or a failed validation? | Settle: a set counts toward 82 to 97 only when its planted bit fires, and every unplanted firing is reported with its set, position and count; more than 3 of a family's 100 sets with an unplanted firing makes the validation `not re-found`, since for a correct test the number of such sets is binomial with n = 100 and p at most 0.005, so that 4 or more have a probability of at most about 0.0017 (computed on 2026-11-02) | B: count a set when any bit fires: looser, since a test that fires on the wrong bit would pass. C: unchanged: a firing is not defined | spec FR-039; research R4, R12; data-model §7; cli contract `validate_tool.py` |
| ANA-063 | The spec defines S as "the number of bits tested at that size and round count", and research R4 runs both tests "on each call type's permutation", while R4 and data-model §5 fix S = 4b, and S_A, per permutation, so that at T call types T·4b bits are tested at 0.005/(4b) each, and FR-028 and R4 state the chance of a raised R, about 21% at R = 32, for one permutation. Does S count every call type's bits, or is the per-permutation threshold the rule, with the chance for T call types stated? | Settle: S counts the bits of one call type's permutation, S = 4b, and S_A likewise, each permutation tested at its own threshold and a firing in any raising R, as R4 has it, and the spec's "Bias rule" says so. Beside every firing, and in FR-028 and R4, the chance of a raise on uniform outputs is stated for the T call types tested: 1 − 0.995^(3⌈R/2⌉·T), about 62% at R = 32 with T = 4, and 1 − 0.995^(T·(3⌈R/2⌉ + 3)) with the avalanche test, about 64% (computed on 2026-11-02); with one stream, T = 1, the stated 21% and 23% | B: S counts the bits of all T call types, so that each test runs at 0.005/(T·4b): looser, since a bias that the per-permutation threshold catches could pass. C: unchanged: S's definition and the stated chance disagree with what is run | spec "Bias rule", FR-028; research R4; data-model §5 |
| ANA-064 | Research R13 and data-model §5 state one operation's degree, w − 1 for a w-bit addition, take "each phase's degree" from a box's or S-box's truth table "where its width allows", and multiply the phases' degrees for a round, while research R11's forms also hold subtraction, AND, OR, NOT, XOR, rotations, shifts and constants, an update combines F with ⊙, a phase holds an update at every node, and an ARX-box spreads an addition and a dependent operation over two phases. Are each operation's degree, an update's and a phase's, and the place of a box's truth-table degree stated, so that two implementations give the same count? | Settle: the rule's count is defined bottom-up over each phase's input lanes, each of degree 1: a constant has degree 0; XOR, NOT, a rotation and a shift take the largest degree of their arguments; a bitwise function, AND and OR among them, takes the largest sum of its arguments' degrees over the monomials of its algebraic normal form, its truth table's degree, so 2 for AND or OR of two lanes; ⊞ and ⊟ take w − 1 times the larger of their operands' degrees, a subtraction counting as an addition (R5); `dst ← dst ⊙ F(srcs)` takes its result's degree and `dst ← rotl(dst, k)` 1; a phase the largest of its updates' degrees, a lane it does not write counting 1; and a round the product of its phases'. A box's truth table spans more than one bit position, so it is computed only at w = 4 and 8, where no `degree` record is kept: there, in the derivation's validation, its exact degree is reported beside the rule's count and never replaces it. The upper bound uses the same rules with w for ⊞ and ⊟, and the known result holds an ARX-box spread over two phases and a bitwise S-box | B: compose every operation by product, AND and OR included: looser, since a higher count raises R_dist at fewer round counts. C: unchanged: two implementations of the rule may count differently | research R13 "Algebraic degree", "Known results"; data-model §5 `degree`, §7 |
| ANA-065 | `gen_constants_v3.py` takes no candidate and writes only `phycoweb/constants_v3.py` and its JSON copies, while research R15 checks "every table the script writes", every record whose result depends on the constants names "the constant table it read by path and SHA-256", research R7 makes "the constant table for the new R with its checks" first after a rise of R, and a refused-list match in a candidate's table makes it fail FR-023. Are a writer, a path and a record stated for each candidate's tables before selection, one per variant and call type? | Add: `gen_constants_v3.py --candidate ID --variant VARIANT` writes, before the freeze, the table of each variant (n, w, R) a candidate uses, per call type where its streams are distinct, from the instance of the rule in its log entry's `parameters`, as `research/004/candidates/<id>/constants/<variant>.json`, with every check of research R15, and a `generator-run` record under `$REP/generator-runs/` holding each table's path and SHA-256 and each check's verdict; it adds nothing to the sidecar and refuses after the freeze, exit 2. A table is written before the first record that reads it, a rise of R writing the new variant's first (R7); a failed check or a refused-list match makes the candidate fail FR-023, logged; and `design_freeze.py create` exits 2 unless `phycoweb/constants_v3.py`'s tables equal the selected candidate's at each supported (n, w) and its final R | B: each evidence script generates the table it needs in memory, its SHA-256 in the record, with no committed file. C: unchanged: candidates' tables have no writer, path or record | spec FR-023; research R7, R15 "Tables per size"; data-model §1, §5 "Constants cited", §11, §13; cli contract "Generators of constants and vectors", `design_freeze.py`; plan "Source Code (repository root)" |
| ANA-066 | Research R13 checks the sums and XORs of the pressure and vein lanes "over each colour of the checkerboard (even n)" and names no form for the three colour classes of a schedule at odd n, which a supported 3×3 has, and FR-037, R13 and data-model §5 state no size for the invariant check, as "every supported size and variant" is stated for the inverse tests. Are the sizes and the per-colour forms at odd n stated? | Settle: the invariant check runs at every supported size and at every variant a candidate uses as evidence, after one round and after a full call, as the inverse tests do; its forms are the sums modulo 2^w and the XORs of the pressure lanes and of the vein lanes, over the whole state and over each colour class of the size's schedule, the checkerboard's two at even n and at odd n the three of research R14's colouring c(i, j). At odd n the colour forms are a new set tested in addition, as R4 tests a new set: the forms already stated keep their level, and the new set is tested at 0.005 divided by the number of all its forms and round counts, so that no firing is lost; nothing changes at the claimable sizes, all even; and at 2×2 and 3×3 the result is a fact about that size alone (FR-032) | B: state the sizes only, the forms at odd n waiting for a three-colour schedule in the design document. C: unchanged: an invariant per colour class at odd n goes unchecked, and no size is stated | research R13 "Invariants"; spec FR-037; data-model §5 `invariant` |
| ANA-067 | Research R7 makes heavy every run of a kind it budgets, so that validations of the exhaustive-bijectivity tool over 2³² inputs, of the frequency test on S14's bias, of the avalanche test on its planted results and of the other structural tools are heavy, and FR-039 and FR-045 charge such runs to the feature-wide row, while R7 lists that row's runs as "validations and re-validations on published values (R12), heavy reproductions (R16) and FR-053's test with two worker counts", and R12 and the cli contract's Budget guard name only validations on published values. Is a row stated for heavy validations on other known results? | Align: research R7's feature-wide row and "What else is charged", research R12 and the cli contract's Budget guard name every validation and re-validation run, on published values or on any other known result (the `phyco-v2` re-findings S12 to S15, the bias tests' synthetic families and planted results, each structural tool's known results and every self-written second known result), as FR-039 and FR-045 do: each charges no candidate and is capped at 8 h per instance, and a heavy one projects first against the feature-wide 66 h row and is refused, exit 2, once it does not fit; the cap of 462 h is unchanged | B: a validation row of its own beside the feature-wide row: looser, since it would raise research R7's cap. C: unchanged: heavy validations on other known results have no row in R7 or the Budget guard | research R7, R12; spec FR-039, FR-045; cli contract "Budget" |
| PRO-060 | The plan's tree names `protocol/candidates.py` as the candidate log's writer and `scripts/owner_record.py` for the owner's records, while data-model §18 leaves the owner's approval to "the writer's approval step", no script creates `research/004/protocol-changes.jsonl`, writes its first entries or proposes a later one, and FR-067 and data-model §14 name no writer for `provenance.jsonl`. Is a writer named, with its command, for each log, and which writer refuses an incomplete entry? | Add: `protocol/changelog.py`, run as `scripts/protocol_change.py`, is the one writer of `research/004/protocol-changes.jsonl`: `create` writes the first entries from the lists "Rows that change the protocol", each rule's text at the two commits of its round, after PRO-058's check, and `propose FILE` a later entry; each ends in data-model §18's approval step, which appends only when the owner types the first 16 hex digits at an interactive terminal, and it refuses, exit 2, an entry without `direction`, a `settled` one without `readings`, or one without `last_record_id` or `results_known`. `protocol/provenance.py`, run as `scripts/provenance.py add` and `review`, is the one writer of `provenance.jsonl` and refuses, exit 2, an entry with a field missing or a `sha256` that is not the named file's. Both take the log-append lock, the cli contract gives each a section, and `tests/test_protocol_004.py` covers the refusals | B: `protocol/evidence.py` writes both logs, with the approval step in it. C: unchanged: the approval step and the refusals belong to no named code | spec FR-044, FR-067; plan "Source Code (repository root)"; data-model §14, §18; cli contract, two new sections |
| HW-072 | FR-025 has the nonce reach "its lanes through the absorption tree where they are rate lanes", while its selector sets each lane "from a key register, the nonce chain or its stated constant", FR-013 has "from a key register, the nonce or its stated constant", and the plan's Complexity Tracking names key registers and the nonce chain. Are the selector's sources stated the same way everywhere, with where the rate lanes' nonce words are held until the copy, whether they are written or absorbed, and whether their registers are zeroized and estimated? | Align: FR-013, FR-025 and the Complexity Tracking name the same four sources of the selector at every lane: a key register, the nonce chain, the nonce register of a rate lane that holds nonce bits, or its stated constant. Each such lane's node holds its nonce word in a register, filled through the absorption tree from the word's transfer on the absorption interface, and the selector writes it into the lane in the cycle of the copy, never as an XOR, so that every lane is set in that one cycle and the initial state is the one FR-013 and research R10 place; these registers are cleared by zeroization with both chains and every copy, are parts of FR-026's estimate and of research R5's list beside the nonce chain, and their transfer enters the instantiate figure | B: absorb the rate lanes' nonce words over the rate after the copy, into lanes the copy sets to zero: no registers, but the copy then waits on the host's word order. C: unchanged: the rate lanes' nonce words have no stated path into the state | spec FR-013, FR-025 "Key loading and reset", "Zeroization", FR-026; research R5 "Size estimate"; plan Complexity Tracking |
| REP-071 | The freeze hashes `evidence_v3.py`, `round_rule.py`, `d4_choice.py`, `mode_limits_v3.py`, `recheck_004.py`, `protocol/devinputs_v3.py` and `protocol/evidence.py` as the scripts that compute verdicts, but not `scripts/validate_tool.py`, whose `outcome` decides whether a tool's results count, nor `scripts/fut_sec8.py`, which exits 1 "if a target is not recovered"; and FR-065's list omits the tool validations, the cross-checks between kinds and the D4 choice. Is every verdict script frozen, and every verdict re-checked? | Align: FR-043's and data-model §13's verdict scripts gain `scripts/validate_tool.py` and `scripts/fut_sec8.py`, and FR-065's list reads: the demonstration; the tool validations and re-validations and the cross-checks between kinds; the inverse, bijectivity, dependency, bias, uniform-control, symmetry, invariant, degree and bound checks; the D4 choice; the check of generated files on each platform; the size profile's generic-bound checks and the bound at the limits; and eligibility and selection, each recomputed from committed counts by exact arithmetic, as now; `recheck_004.py`'s section lists them per kind | B: freeze the verdict functions of `protocol/evidence.py` alone, the lists naming those functions rather than the scripts. ## Third re-run of 2026-11-01

The 61 items other than conflicts that the third re-run of 2026-11-01 found over the complete
documents and the repository, in checklist order: 10 security, 7 design-rationale, 11 analysis, 8
protocol, 13 hardware and 12 reproducibility. They were answered on 2026-11-02, "all recommended",
and applied, as were the 9 conflicts above.

How does each rule match, and what does a standing entry pass? | Add: every rule matches whole words, ignoring case, with listed inflections: random, randomly, randomness, randomize and its forms, randomization; high-entropy; proven, provably; compliant, compliance; conform, conforms, conformance, conforming; approve, approved, approval; constant time and constant-time. A word is a run of letters and digits, so "random-nonce", "random_state" and `numpy.random` hold "random" and `os.urandom` does not. A standing entry stays one fixed phrase and passes only a line that holds it; each other form of an allowed use gets a standing phrase of its own, listed with its reason in data-model §15, or a line entry. The project's name holds no flagged word, so its standing entry goes. A test runs every rule and every standing phrase on sample sentences that must and must not be flagged, as research R18's test does for the mapping chapter's rule | B: patterns, a regular expression per allowed use: fewer entries, but a pattern can pass a line it was not written for. | ANA-050 | Research R7's 12 h row, one per candidate, holds the frequency and avalanche tests "with their controls", while each test has one control record per size that "serves every candidate", and "A `control` run belongs to no candidate". Which budget pays for a control, and what are its `--candidate` argument, its projection, its candidate-log entry and its model and variant IDs? | Settle (no row gains hours, and the order of candidates changes nothing): every candidate's 12 h row is charged, at each size, that size's control hours, whether the controls ran for it or before it, and its reservation counts them by projection until they exist. A control still runs once per test and size and belongs to no candidate: `evidence_v3.py --kind control` takes no `--candidate`, projects first and runs only if every entered candidate's row can hold it; its record names no model or variant (`model_id` null) and takes its inputs from the `control` namespace; and it writes no candidate-log entry, the Budget guard reading the control records when it charges each row | B: charge each control to the first candidate that reaches the size: later rows would hold more hours, looser than research R7's row. C: unchanged: a heavy run of a kind research R7 budgets would charge no row | research R4, R7; data-model §1, §5 `control`; cli contract "Budget", `scripts/evidence_v3.py` |
| ANA-051 | Data-model §7 asks only that "the planted gap of R13 re-found", so a counter that fires on every bit would pass, and no known result covers the counting of the related-key and related-nonce set or of the rotational pairs, each a different path from the flip set. What must match, and are the other two paths covered? | Stricter (known results only added): the gap is re-found only if, at every round count tested, every difference bit of the unmixed lane fires and no other bit does, bit for bit against the stated set. Two known results join research R13's table, each written and logged before the tool is drafted: an instantiation variant in which a stated key bit and a stated nonce bit never reach a stated lane, flagged exactly; and an operation list in which one stated lane commutes with word rotation, flagged exactly. The avalanche tool's validation record covers all three paths | B: let the dependency cross-check stand for the other two paths: no new results, but a fault in their counting code would pass. C: unchanged: a counter that fires on every bit passes the validation | research R4, R13 "Known results"; spec FR-039; data-model §7 |
| ANA-052 | A re-validation needs "a second known result of its kind as well as the first, named in the defect record before the fix runs", but research R19 approves eight papers with "Nothing else is covered" without naming each trail tool's second value, a self-written result cannot be written "before the tool it validates is drafted" once the tool has failed, and a result named after the failure can be chosen knowing how the tool failed. Are the second known results fixed before the first validation? | Stricter (known results only added, and fixed earlier): each tool's second known result is fixed before its first validation, with its source, beside the first in the provenance log: a second published value from research R19's approved papers (paper, table, value) where one of the kind is there, or else a self-written result of the kind, written and logged before the tool is drafted, as research R13's are. A tool with neither says so in its first validation record; a failed validation then leaves it unvalidated and its results `not evaluated`, as research R12 treats a value that cannot be obtained. The defect record cites the result fixed in advance | B: name it in the defect record from any source the owner then approves: possibly one more paper, and chosen after the failure. C: unchanged: the second result could be chosen knowing how the tool failed | research R12 "A failed validation", R13, R19; spec FR-039, FR-046; data-model §7, §14 |
| ANA-053 | Research R12's ARX search "must reproduce the exhaustive box tables", which give differentials that several trails can share, and the active-S-box counter "must reproduce the exhaustive trail optima", which are weights, not counts, over rounds and a grid size not stated, while "A failed cross-check is a tool defect". Which quantity is compared with which, over which rounds and sizes? | Settle (like with like, by exact equality): the ARX search's optimal single-trail weight over r rounds of the box must equal the optimal trail weight computed from the exhaustive tables by a search over characteristics, the best product of one-round entries, for every r from 1 to the largest at which that computation finishes within its 8 h cap, one round equalling the table's best entry. The active-S-box counter's minimum over r rounds at 4×4 must equal the exhaustive minimum from the nonlinear layer's tables over the same r. The record lists both values per r, and a trail weight above a differential's weight, as clustering gives, is expected and not compared | B: compare with the best differential weight: clustering would make a correct tool fail. C: unchanged: two implementations could compare different quantities | research R12 "Cross-checks between kinds"; spec FR-039; data-model §6 `cross_check`, §7 |
| ANA-055 | Research R12 and data-model §6 turn a floating-point solver value into a rational "by rounding in the direction that weakens it", while every floating-point value is already a binary rational, 2^(−W) is irrational for every weight W that is not an integer, and an integral objective reported as 9.9999999 for 10 would lose a whole box. To which grid is a value rounded, and may a dual bound within a tolerance below an integer be taken as that integer? | Settle (conservative reading): W is kept as a rational, rounded down from the float to a multiple of 2⁻¹⁶, never up, and the D4 comparison p_A^(L_B) < p_B^(L_A) is made exactly as W_A·L_B > W_B·L_A. An active-box or active-S-box count is the integer at or below its proven dual bound, with no tolerance, so 9.9999999 counts as 9. The records hold the float and the rounded value. A lost box may be confirmed only by an exact method where one exists, research R12's cross-checks at w = 4 and 8, never by rounding up | B: take the integer within 10⁻⁶ below it: looser, since a tolerance would stand in for a proof. C: unchanged: two implementations could round to different grids | research R6, R12 "Solver settings"; spec FR-038; data-model §6 `bound` |
| ANA-056 | FR-038 and research R12 carry a bound to a larger size "only by a proof in the design document", which is completed after selection for the selected candidate only, while an eligible candidate "has an established R_bound at every claimable size" before selection. Can a carried bound serve an unselected candidate, which record holds it, and does the 48 h reservation keep the searches it replaces? | Settle (nothing that exists only after selection decides eligibility): before selection, every candidate's R_bound at each claimable size comes from a bound computed at that size (data-model §6), and the 48 h reservation keeps every such probe. A carrying proof may be written in the design document for the selected candidate and serves only as a check beside the computed bounds, reported in the rationale. A candidate whose row cannot hold a 16×16 search has no R_bound there and is `ineligible`, as for any spent row | B: carry by a `carried-bound` record per candidate, with the proof, its SHA-256 and its check at 8×8: fewer 16×16 searches, but looser than the reading in which only the design document's proof carries, and a proof accepted after selection would decide eligibility before it. C: unchanged: eligibility needs a document that exists only after selection | spec FR-038, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Eligibility and selection"; research R7, R12 "Bounds per size"; data-model §6, §9 |
| ANA-057 | The phase rule, "within one phase no update reads a lane that another update of that phase writes", is not checked by code, the interpreters' semantics are not stated (list order, or the values at the phase's start, as hardware reads them), the two agree only where the rule holds, and whether it holds depends on n, while FR-032 transfers bijectivity from 2×2. Is the rule checked at every size, and which semantics hold? | Add: the operation-list loader checks the phase rule at every supported size, from the declared reads and writes after the neighbourhood is resolved, and refuses a list that breaks it, exit 2, before any test; a committed test runs it on every list at every size, with a stated list that breaks it at one size only, which it must refuse. Both interpreters apply a phase as hardware does, each update reading the values at the phase's start, which equals list order where the rule holds, checked on the vectors. Bijectivity by construction then holds wherever the check passes, so FR-032's transfer rests on the rule at each size | B: use list-order semantics with the rule unchecked: the model stays bijective, but hardware could compute a different function where the rule fails. C: unchanged: the model and the hardware could compute different functions | spec FR-019, FR-025 "Stages", FR-032; research R11, R13, R14 |
| ANA-058 | Data-model §1's classification table names only "No finding", a firing or a trail that raises R, "A test that does not fire" and a finding of a tool not yet validated, while other kinds and outcomes exist: a reproduction that differs, a `dependency` record showing incomplete diffusion, a `degree` record that raises R_dist, a failed `inverse`, `bijectivity` or `symmetry` check, a kept invariant, a firing with 2r ≤ R, a proven bound, a fired `control`, the demonstration record and the `recheck`, `lock-cleared`, `cost`, D4, round-rule and selection records. Is every kind and outcome classed, and when is "strong theoretical concern" given? | Add: the table gives a class to every kind and outcome, computed by code: a firing, a validated trail or a `dependency` record showing incomplete diffusion, raising R or not, is a `confirmed vulnerability` of the reduced rounds; a failed `inverse`, `bijectivity` or `symmetry` check, or a kept invariant, is a `confirmed vulnerability` of the candidate, worded as the failed property, with its decision; a `degree` raise of R_dist, RAT-049's inside-out counts among them, is a `strong theoretical concern`, since an argument shows it and no run demonstrates it; a proven bound is `no weakness identified within the analysis performed`, with its scope; a fired `control` is `none`, with `status` `inconclusive` for that test and size; a differing reproduction is an `unverified hypothesis`, `blocked` until ANA-048's re-run; the demonstration record is a `confirmed vulnerability` of `phyco-v2`'s seed bound once every target is recovered, a disagreement in its match counts being an `unverified hypothesis` until the FUT-SEC-8 result review; and the `recheck`, `lock-cleared`, `cost`, D4, round-rule and selection records are `none` | B: class every other record `none`, with a reviewer's note: FR-060 makes the class the code's, not a reviewer's. C: unchanged: records of several kinds and outcomes have no class | data-model §1 "Classification"; spec FR-060; security.md §9.1 (cited, unchanged) |
| PRO-050 | Research R1 writes each environment record "with the owner's approval, on any change", and every evidence script exits 2 until "the owner then approves a new environment record", yet no command writes the record and none of its fields holds the approval; and an `owner-provided` entry of the downloads manifest opens `validate_tool.py`'s source guard with no writer named. Are these the owner's own acts, each with a writer, a confirmation and a record? | Add: both are owner's own acts outside the standing authorization, and FR-044's list gains them. A new `scripts/owner_record.py` writes each: `environment`, a new environment record; and `owner-provided`, an entry of `$REP/downloads.json` with the owner's words, the source, the date and the value's SHA-256. Each mode prints the SHA-256 of what it writes and refuses unless standard input is an interactive terminal and the owner types its first 16 hex digits, and the record keeps `approved_by`, `approved_utc` and that confirmation. Each gets a Review & Approval row of its kind, "Environment record <n>" or "Owner-provided value <n>", set by the owner and committed, which the environment guard and `validate_tool.py`'s source guard read at HEAD, as `repair_log.py` reads "Log repair <n>" | B: let the agent write both on the owner's quoted word: no new command, but a record that opens a guard would rest on a quote the agent wrote. C: unchanged: two guards open on records that no one is named to write | spec FR-044, Review & Approval (the note under the table); research R1, R19; data-model §1, §7; cli contract "Guards shared by every script", `validate_tool.py`; plan "Project Structure" |
| PRO-051 | The change log takes "one entry per listed row", each with one `rule`, `old_text` and `new_text`, while a row may be listed under several rules (the second re-run's ANA-037 under four), rows were committed together, so that a row's own before and after exist in no commit, and rules whose direction is "no change" still get entries. Can each first entry hold what data-model §18 asks, and what follows if the owner does not confirm a list? | Settle: each first entry holds `rules`, every rule the row is listed under, with `old_text` and `new_text` per rule: that rule's text, in each place its heading names, at the commit before the row's round was applied and at the commit that completed it, with the row's ID, question and chosen option, marked `granularity: round`. Under a rule whose direction is "no change" the `direction` is `settled`, with its `readings`, whatever the row's kind, and a row listed under several rules has a direction per rule. The log is created, and no evidence run starts, only after the owner confirms every list; a row the owner takes out of a list is recorded beside it with the owner's reason, that it changed no rule | B: one entry per rule and round: one text pair per entry, but the rows' reasons and directions shared. C: unchanged: the entries cannot hold what data-model §18 asks | data-model §18; spec FR-044, Review & Approval "Tasks approval"; checklist-choices.md "Rows that change the protocol" |
| PRO-052 | Class 5 plants each set's bit "at position j mod S", with S = 4b being 2¹², 2¹⁴ and 2¹⁶ and j at most 99, so every planted bit lies in positions 0 to 99 of a bit order no document states, and a counting defect elsewhere passes; and after an approved numpy upgrade the same seeds give new counts. How are the planted bits spread, what is the bit order, and which record decides? | Stricter (a known result added): class 5 stays as settled, and the validation gains a second family of 100 sets per size, j = 100…199, drawn the same way from `phycoweb-004/dev/synthetic/n<n>/<j>`, each planting its bit at position ⌊(j − 100)·S/100⌋, spread over all S bits, both sets and both directions; each family must fire in 82 to 97 of its 100 sets. The test's bit order is stated: set 1, then set 2, within each the b bits after Π, then the b bits after Π⁻¹, lanes in index order and bits most significant first, as each set's SHA-256 covers them. After an approved numpy upgrade the old record stays the validation in the old environment and the new record decides in the new one: outside 82 to 97 the tool is unvalidated there and its results `not evaluated` until a defect is found and fixed | B: change class 5's rule itself to ⌊j·S/100⌋: one family, but a change to a settled rule, which research R3's "nothing may change" does not allow. C: unchanged: a counting defect outside positions 0 to 99 passes | research R3 (class 5), R4; spec FR-039, FR-040; data-model §2, §7; cli contract `validate_tool.py` |
| PRO-053 | The edge case says that "another candidate may be entered in its place within the stop rule's budget before the set closes", "or the feature recommends stopping", with no rule between the two; `--close` needs that "no replacement is pending", which no event of the log records; the decision can follow the others' R at 4×4; and whether the decline's further candidate can itself be replaced is open. Who decides, by when, and how is it recorded? | Settle (no decision waits for results): a replacement is entered for every candidate that becomes `rejected` or `ineligible` while its family has fewer than three evaluated candidates and the budget allows; the agent proposes it and the owner approves its entry, as for any candidate. The candidate log gains a `replacement` event, `pending` when logged and closed by the replacement's `proposed` entry or by a stated reason (family full, budget spent), which `--close` reads. The decline's further candidate is replaced the same way, within the one reopening | B: never replace, and close the set with the others: fewer hours, but unused candidates, and the edge case's replacement dropped. C: unchanged: a free decision with no record, which `--close` cannot read | spec Edge Cases, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Eligibility and selection", "Stop rule"; data-model §3; cli contract `round_rule.py` |
| PRO-054 | Research R6 sends only the chosen family on to the production-width analysis, while the fallback to the other family needs its R_bound at every claimable size, `round_rule.py --close` waits until "every candidate has a final decision", and `withdrawn` is allowed only "before its first tool run". Is the other family analysed, when, and is "final decision" defined? | Settle (the fallback can work, and no hours go to a family that cannot be selected): the other family is analysed at the production width only if the chosen family ends with no eligible candidate, once every candidate of the chosen family has its final decision, in research R7's order, its rows reserved meanwhile. If the chosen family has an eligible candidate, each candidate of the other family gets `ineligible` with the reason "not analysed at the production width: D4 chose the other family", which the final report states. "Final decision" means `rejected` or `ineligible`, or, for an eligible candidate, its round-rule record at every claimable size | B: analyse both families from the start: the fallback is ready, but hours go to a family that may never be selectable. C: unchanged: the fallback could never select | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Nonlinearity choice rule", "Eligibility and selection"; research R6, R7; data-model §3, §9; plan phase 5 |
| PRO-055 | If, after `selection.json` exists and before the freeze, the selected candidate becomes ineligible or its R at 4×4 rises above another eligible candidate's, does `round_rule.py --select` run again, rewriting a file that "stays as the script wrote it", given that `selection-2.json` is for a decline only, that a tie choice is "never changed" and that the security-target review follows selection? | Settle (the rules, not the order of events, decide): any change before the freeze that touches any candidate's eligibility or R at 4×4 makes `--select` run again over the whole set, writing `selection-<k>.json`, k = 2, 3, …, which cites the previous record; earlier records stay, and a decline's re-selection is one such record. A tie choice binds only the record whose hash it names, and a new tie needs a new choice. If another candidate is selected, the security-target review and every later review are held again for it, each in a row of its own; if the same one is, the reviews held stand for what did not change, as FR-043 says | B: rewrite `selection.json` in place, keeping the old content under `versions/`: one name, but "stays as the script wrote it" and the tie's hash fail. C: unchanged: a change before the freeze has no stated outcome | spec Edge Cases, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Eligibility and selection", FR-043, FR-044, Review & Approval; data-model introduction, §9; cli contract `round_rule.py` |
| PRO-056 | Research R7 states "The most the feature can spend is six candidates × 66 h" as a cap on the feature as a whole, but validations on published values, re-validations included, are unlimited in number, heavy reproductions charge no row with no limit on n, FR-053's test "charges no candidate", and FUT-SEC-8 has its own row. Is the total a cap, and whose request starts a heavy reproduction? | Stricter (a budget where there was none): the total becomes a cap that stops runs. Beside the six candidates' rows and the FUT-SEC-8 row, one feature-wide 66 h row holds every heavy run that charges no candidate (validations and re-validations on published values, heavy reproductions, FR-053's test), each projected first and refused, exit 2, once the row is spent. The most is then six × 66 h, that row and the FUT-SEC-8 row, which research R7 states. A heavy reproduction runs only on the owner's request, quoted with its date in its record; the agent never starts one on its own, and a light reproduction needs no request | B: call the total an estimate only, with runs outside the rows unbounded: no new row, but the cap on the feature as a whole would not hold. C: unchanged: the stated total bounds nothing | research R7, R12, R16; spec FR-045, User Story 4; cli contract "Reproduction", "Budget" |
| PRO-057 | The spec's Status line reads "checklist choices of the first run and of the re-run answered and applied (2026-11-01)", while the Review & Approval table holds a third round, "Checklist choices, second re-run", Approved and applied the same day, and checklist-choices.md's Status then read "all 306 rows answered". Which status follows which, and when is each updated? | Align: the Status line names every round in the table, now "Spec and plan approved (2026-11-01); checklist choices of the first run, the re-run and the second re-run answered and applied (2026-11-01); the third re-run's choices await the owner's answer; tasks approval, reviews and design freeze pending", and it is updated in the same commit as each table row and each answered round. The table is the source; the Status line and checklist-choices.md's Status follow it, and a test compares them | B: make the Status line read "see Review & Approval": one place, but the first lines would not say where the work stands. C: unchanged: the Status line falls behind the gates it summarizes | spec Status line, Review & Approval; checklist-choices.md "Status" |
| HW-058 | FR-025 loads the key "once per key along a registered shift chain through those nodes" and copies it "in one cycle at every instantiate", while the nonce reaches the lanes outside the rate "along the same chain otherwise", that is at every instantiate. Where are the nonce's words held without shifting the key out, on which interface does the host give them, do their cycles enter the instantiate figure, must the nodes form a path of neighbours, what is the "key-loading buffer", and how are FR-013's other lanes set at each instantiate? | Add: a nonce chain of its own runs beside the key's, through the nodes that hold nonce bits in lanes outside the rate, and the host gives the nonce on the absorption interface at each instantiate, in the model's word order, so that the key chain shifts only at key loading. The nonce chain's length enters the instantiate figure, and Complexity Tracking lists it as a local data path of public length. Each chain passes, registered, through the nodes between those that hold its bits, so no path of neighbours is required, and its length counts those registers. The "key-loading buffer" is the key chain's registers, with the nonce chain beside them in the zeroization list. At each instantiate a selector at every lane sets it, in the same cycle as the copy, from a key register, the nonce chain or its stated constant (FR-013), and the selectors join FR-026's parts (HW-067) | B: route the nonce through the key chain after moving the key into a second set of registers: a second copy of the key, and the chain's length in every instantiate. C: unchanged: the nonce would shift the key out of its registers | spec FR-025 "Key loading and reset", "Zeroization", FR-013; plan "Complexity Tracking" |
| HW-059 | FR-025 names three interfaces, "Each interface (key loading, absorption, output) uses valid and ready", while the host must also choose among a key load, instantiate, generate, reseed and zeroize, "A generate request gives its length in 32-bit words" on no named interface, and no port list is required, though FUT-HW-5's criteria cite "the port list with no read-back path". Is a command interface stated, with its format and handshake, and a port list of every port? | Add: a fourth interface, the command interface, uses valid and ready like the others: one 32-bit word per call, with the call type (key load, instantiate, generate, reseed, zeroize) in stated bits and, for generate, the length in 32-bit words in the rest. A refused command is answered on the error-code output, and the length word of a personalization string or a reseed input stays on the absorption interface. The design document gives the port list: every port's direction, width, value after configuration and value after reset, `selftest_fail` and the error-code output included, and FUT-HW-5's "no read-back path" is checked against it | B: a first command word on the absorption interface: commands mixed with inputs, and a key load would need it too. C: unchanged: the host has no stated way to start a call | spec FR-025 "Flow control", "Registers and read-back", "Call sequence", FR-059; Security Policy traceability "FUT-HW-5" |
| HW-060 | FR-025's instantiate figure runs "from the last nonce or personalization word accepted to the first output valid", while instantiate gives no output and the first output valid follows a generate request whose arrival the host sets, so that the figure is not "fixed by public values". Does it end when instantiate is done, or does it assume a generate request issued at once? | Clarify: the figure ends when instantiate is done, its forget call complete and the core `instantiated` and ready for a command; a generate request's own figure runs from its acceptance on the command interface (HW-059) to its first output valid. Both are fixed by public values, and FR-025's sentence reads so | B: assume a generate request of a stated length issued at once: the figure's end would still depend on when the host acts. C: unchanged: the figure depends on the host's timing | spec FR-025 "Key loading and reset", FR-020 |
| HW-061 | The state table's `not-reset` "refuses every call" while every register starts at zero, the error-code register among them, `failed` "refuses every call with `selftest_fail` set until the next reset", and the reset sequence, while the self-test runs, has no state. Is each refusal a call never accepted or one refused with a code, is `selftest_fail` an output of its own, and does a zeroization other than reset run the self-test? | Add: a new state `resetting` runs from reset released to the self-test done and the state zeroized. In `not-reset`, `resetting` and `failed`, ready is held low on every interface, so no call is accepted and no code is raised; the error-code register holds zero, meaning no error, and the codes start at 1. `selftest_fail` is an output of its own, latched in `failed` until the next reset. A zeroization by any other trigger runs no self-test and goes to `no-key`; a failed self-test's zeroization goes to `failed` and starts nothing further. The tests cover a call in each state | B: accept each call and refuse it with a code: a code would then have to be valid in `not-reset`. C: unchanged: the response in three stretches is not stated | spec FR-025 "Call sequence", "Zeroization", FR-059, FR-020 |
| HW-062 | The state table has no state for a key load in progress, and `no-key` "accepts a key load only", so a zeroize is refused while a partial key sits in the chain and only a reset clears it; and during a request "a stall, which the consumer sets in public, holds only the buffer and delays the next call", for as long as the consumer likes. What happens to an abandoned key load, and is a zeroize accepted while a block waits? | Add: a new state `loading` runs from the first key word to the last; it accepts the remaining words and zeroize only, and the key is held only after the last word, so that an instantiate is refused with its code and changes nothing. A zeroize in `loading` clears the chain and returns to `no-key`, where a new load starts afresh. The key-loading figure counts a finished load, and the figure of a zeroize from `loading` is stated. While a block waits in the output buffer, the command interface accepts zeroize, which clears the buffer, the trees and the state; no other call is accepted until the block is taken or a zeroize or reset clears it | B: let a new key load restart a partial one without a zeroize: abandoned key bits would sit in the chain. C: unchanged: only a reset clears a partial key | spec FR-025 "Call sequence", "Flow control", "Zeroization", FR-020 |
| HW-063 | "The self-test runs instantiate with the key and nonce 0 of the frozen vectors", held as constants, while FR-025 loads a key from the key-loading interface "along a registered shift chain", and "Its blocks go only to its comparator", whose width is not stated. How do the self-test's key and nonce reach their registers, and how wide is the comparator? | Add: the self-test feeds the key and nonce chains from its constants through a selector at each chain's input, word by word as the host would, with the cycles entering the reset figure and no parallel load path. The comparator takes each whole r-bit block as the output buffer receives it, compares it with the expected block and holds the result until the last block; its width, r bits, and the expected output's storage join FR-026's parts. Afterwards the selectors return to the interfaces, and the zeroization clears both chains | B: load the key registers in parallel: fewer cycles, but a second key path that no host load tests. C: unchanged: the self-test's key path and the comparator are not stated | spec FR-059, FR-025 "Key loading and reset", "Zeroization", FR-026 |
| HW-064 | FR-020 counts "the cycles in which the core works: on output, the cycles with ready high, and on input (key loading, a personalization string, a reseed input), the cycles with valid high while the core's ready is high", so that, read literally, calls of Π are counted only while the consumer holds ready high, and on input the calls between absorbed blocks are not counted at all. Is the rule stated as every cycle of a call except those in which the core waits for the host? | Clarify (as FR-020 means it, "a measured count matches its formula at any pace the host keeps"): a call's count is every cycle from its start to its end except those in which the core waits for the host: on output, valid high with ready low; on an input, the core's ready high with valid low. A call starts when its command is accepted (HW-059) and ends when the core is ready for the next command. FR-020 reads so, and feature 008 measures at the ports | B: count every cycle and state the host's pace: counts would then match their formulas at one pace only. C: unchanged: a measured count would depend on the host's signals in cycles where the core works | spec FR-020, FR-025 "Flow control"; Security Policy traceability "FUT-HW-5" |
| HW-065 | FR-026's implied rate is "r/(P·R + ⌈r/i⌉) bits per cycle", while FR-020's formulas also count the start signal's fan-out and the output tree at every call and the clearing at a request's end; and the request-level figure counts "the trees' latency" in FR-026 but not in research R5 or data-model §10's `throughput_implied`. Do the rate and the three request-level figures count what FR-020 counts? | Align: on FR-020's formulas, the per-call rate is r/(P·R + ⌈r/i⌉ + t_s + t_o), with t_s the start tree's latency and t_o the output tree's where it is not overlapped. The request-level figure counts the request's output calls, its forget call, the trees' latency and the clearing at the request's end, with instantiate's calls spread over the stated number of requests. FR-026, research R5 and `throughput_implied` (data-model §10) state the same formula, with r/(P·R) and i beside it, and FR-026's example is computed again once the design states the trees' latencies | B: count the trees at the request level only: the per-call figure would overstate the rate. C: unchanged: three statements of one figure disagree | spec FR-026 (and its example), FR-020; research R5; data-model §10 |
| HW-066 | FR-025 lets each stage hold "at most two serial w-bit carry chains", while "A stage is one colour phase and takes one clock cycle", each update adds at most one chain, and the phase rule keeps any path within a phase from passing through two updates, so that only a public counter can add a second chain. Is the limit restated as one, with the counter as the only second chain, or may a stage hold two dependent updates? | Align: on the phase rule, a stage holds at most one serial w-bit carry chain from the round's updates on any path, and at most one more from a public counter held as 32-bit words on the same path, and FR-025 reads so. P, the cycle formulas and research R5's depth rule stay. A candidate whose ARX-box needs an addition and a dependent operation in one step spreads them over two phases | B: allow two dependent updates per stage: a stage would no longer be one colour phase, and P, the formulas and research R5's rule would change. C: unchanged: the limit counts a path the phase rule forbids | spec FR-025 "Stages", FR-019; research R5, R11 |
| HW-067 | FR-026's estimate gives "each part with its own figure", which feature 008 checks part by part against Vivado's hierarchical utilization report, but it does not name parts that FR-025 adds: the fan-out trees of reset and of the start signal, with the call-type field at every node, the selector at every lane that instantiate sets, the controller with the call-state table, the registered error-code output, and the self-test's comparator and sequencer. Is each counted, or stated negligible with its reason? | Add: FR-026 names, each with its own figure, the reset and start fan-out trees with the call-type field at every node; the instantiate lane selectors, up to 2w·n² bits, estimated per state bit and stated where merged with stage logic; the controller with the call-state table; the error-code register; the self-test's sequencer, comparator, expected output and input selectors (HW-063); the nonce chain (HW-058); and the command interface (HW-059). A part is negligible only with its figure and reason, and feature 008 checks each against Vivado's hierarchical utilization report | B: one "control" line for all of them: the lane selectors alone could take a LUT per state bit, hidden in one figure. C: unchanged: parts that FR-025 adds have no figure | spec FR-026, FR-025, FR-059; Security Policy traceability "FUT-HW-2"; research R5 |
| HW-068 | The key and nonce travel "as whole words in a stated word and byte order", while "The key-loading interface is generic in c: a c-bit key and a c/2-bit nonce" and c need only be "a multiple of w and of 16", so that a keyed variant with c = 32, at 2×2 with w = 8 or 16, takes a 16-bit nonce. Is c restricted to multiples of 64 for keyed variants, or are the unused bits of a last partial word stated? | Align: on whole words, a keyed variant's c is a multiple of 64, so that the key and the nonce are whole 32-bit words. Research R14 and the model contract's variant rules state it, and the model refuses a keyed variant with another c; keyless variants keep the wider rule. `n2-w16-R8-c64` meets it, and a keyed 2×2 variant needs w ≥ 16 | B: allow c = 32 with a stated half word, its other half zero and refused otherwise: one more refusal and one more format. C: unchanged: a 16-bit nonce has no stated format | research R14; model contract "Variant IDs"; spec FR-025 "Key loading and reset", "Flow control" |
| HW-069 | Feature 008's board wrapper "holds reset for a stated number of cycles after configuration" and derives the clock through an MMCM, which is not usable until it locks, while "Reset is synchronous" and a reset request from the board, a button or the host is asynchronous to that clock. Must the wrapper hold reset until lock, release it synchronously, and reset the core again if lock is lost? | Add (carried to feature 008, as the clock split is): the wrapper holds the core's reset while the MMCM reports no lock and for the stated cycles after lock, releases it synchronously to the derived clock through a two-stage synchronizer, synchronizes every asynchronous reset request the same way, and asserts reset again whenever lock is lost, so that the core sees only a synchronous reset. FR-025 states these as the wrapper's duties, and the FUT-HW-1 and FUT-HW-2 rows cite them | B: leave it to feature 008's design: the core's synchronous reset would rest on a wrapper no requirement states. C: unchanged: the reset the core needs is not stated against the clock it runs on | spec FR-025 "Key loading and reset", "Call sequence"; Security Policy traceability "FUT-HW-1", "FUT-HW-2" |
| HW-070 | The model contract's "Supported values: n = 2…16 where the design document's update schedule covers n" and research R14's exploration at every n from 2 to 16 admit sizes that FR-018 counts as not supported, while the frozen constants hold "one constant table per supported (n, w)" and "A size without a table cannot be built until the same script generates one". Is "supported" one set of sizes, where do an exploration run's constants come from, and what is "R from 1 to the frozen R" at a size with no frozen R? | Align: on FR-018's list, "supported" means FR-018's sizes, 2×2, 3×3 where specified, 4×4, 8×8 and 16×16. Other n from 2 to 16 are for exploration only, from a table the same script writes for that (n, w) and checks as for a supported size, kept under `research/004/exploration/`, never frozen and never evidence. The model contract's "Supported values" reads so, an exploration ID carries its R, and "R from 1 to the frozen R" applies to supported sizes only. FUT-GRID-1's cell reads "the generics cover n = 2…16 in the model, through constant tables generated on demand at the other sizes" | B: refuse every other n in the model: no exploration, and FUT-GRID-1's cell changes. C: unchanged: two sets of sizes are both called supported | spec FR-018, FR-023, Security Policy traceability "FUT-GRID-1"; research R14, R15; model contract "Variant IDs" |
| REP-057 | The demonstration record is `fut-sec-8/demonstration.json`, a name without the `record_id` that is "unique; part of the file name" of every record, while "A record is never overwritten", the sweep may be repeated after a defect with `--repeat-after-defect ID`, and an existing record makes `fut_sec8.py` exit 2. Where does the repeat write its record, and which record do the FUT-SEC-8 result review, the best-attack register and `--reproduce RECORD` take? | Add: each demonstration record is named `fut-sec-8/<record_id>.json`, its ID beginning with `demonstration` (REP-068), so that a repeat after a defect writes beside the first. The repeat holds `supersedes`, the earlier record's ID, and `defect`, the defect record's ID, and a run exits 2, as now, while a record exists and no new defect is named. The FUT-SEC-8 result review names the record it reviews by ID and SHA-256 in its row, and the register row cites that record. `--reproduce RECORD` and `--verify RECORD` take a record by ID, so that every earlier record stays reproducible and every run is reported | B: keep a fixed-name `demonstration.json` with `versions/`: one name, but a kept record would be stored as an old version of another. C: unchanged: the repeat has no file name, and an existing record makes it exit 2 | data-model introduction, §4; cli contract `fut_sec8.py`; spec Edge Cases, Review & Approval "FUT-SEC-8 result review"; plan "Project Structure" |
| REP-059 | FR-053 and data-model §1 compare "each deterministic output's `sha256` and `bytes`", while a `p_values` file is "compared by its values" and its hash may differ between the two environments, and when p-values fall outside the tolerance it is open whether the outcome is `differs`, a finding, or `match`, since a `p_values` file "decides nothing". Is a `p_values` output deterministic, what does a value outside the tolerance give, and do data-model §1 and §5 state one rule? | Clarify: a `p_values` output is marked `deterministic` false with `compared: values`, so that its `sha256`, `bytes` and `uncompressed_sha256` are reported only. Its values are compared under data-model §1's rule, a relative difference of at most 10⁻⁶ or both values below 2⁻¹⁰²², and §5 cites that rule instead of restating it. A value outside makes the outcome `differs`, exit 1, with the values listed: a finding about the run's reproducibility that blocks no candidate, since a p-value decides no verdict (ANA-048) | B: leave the outcome `match` with the values outside listed: a tolerance that never fails compares nothing. C: unchanged: the documents state two rules for one output | data-model §1 "Reproduction", `outputs`, §5 "Exact verdicts"; research R4, R16; spec FR-053 |
| REP-060 | A run of `gen_constants_v3.py`, `gen_vectors_v3.py` or `gen_constructed_v3.py` leaves only "one entry per generator run with its `record_id`" in the sidecar, without most of FR-061's fields, while a generator's reproduction "compares every file it writes with the committed one", that is with the files as last committed, not those the named run wrote. Does each generator run write a record its reproduction can compare with, and does each generator have `--reproduce`? | Add: each generator run writes, beside its sidecar entry, a `generator-run` record under `$REP/generator-runs/` with every field of FR-061 and each written file's path and SHA-256, and the sidecar entry cites its `record_id`. `--reproduce RECORD` on each of the three generators runs that record's command again and compares every file, in memory, with the SHA-256 the record holds, so that a later regeneration changes nothing; the synopsis gives one line per generator. After the freeze the sidecar takes no new entry, as now | B: compare with the committed files, as now: only the last run is reproducible, and earlier runs could no longer be checked. C: unchanged: a generator run leaves no record its reproduction can compare with | data-model §1 "Fields compared, per kind", §11; cli contract "Generators of constants and vectors"; spec FR-053, FR-061; plan "Project Structure" |
| REP-061 | A reproduction compares "each source file's entry in `code_sha256`", and the committed-code guard exits 2 "unless every source file in its `code_sha256` matches HEAD", so that a record is reproducible only while HEAD holds the code it ran, and the re-check runs "the verdict functions of `protocol/evidence.py`" as they stand. Which code do `--reproduce` and the re-check run for a record whose code has changed since its run? | Add: `--reproduce RECORD` runs in a separate worktree of the original's `git_head`, with the code files of `code_sha256` checked against that commit's blobs, and the reproduction record names that commit; the committed-code guard applies to that commit, not to HEAD's files, and the outputs go to the main tree's `reproductions/` (REP-058). FR-061's test reproduces, per light kind, its latest record at HEAD and one made before the last change to its code. The re-check runs, per record, the verdict functions at that record's `git_head` from the same kind of worktree and names each commit, so that a function changed before the freeze is never applied to records it did not compute | B: refuse, exit 2, once the code has changed: records would stop being reproducible at the next edit of `protocol/evidence.py`. C: unchanged: the first edit to a record's code leaves it without a reproduction | cli contract "Reproduction", "Guards shared by every script", `recheck_004.py`; data-model §1 "Reproduction", `code_sha256`; research R16; spec FR-053, FR-061, FR-065 |
| REP-062 | Every frequency and avalanche record cites `research/004/tools/critical_counts.json` by path and SHA-256, a file that a stricter change may extend with counts for a new N; the sidecar is rewritten at every generator run; `research/004/candidates/superseded.json` grows with every superseded operation list; and `dirty_paths` "may be data and report files only". Is each such file append-only or versioned, committed before a run cites it, and is `critical_counts.json` code or data? | Add: `critical_counts.json` is code, under "`research/004/ir.py` and its tools", in `code_sha256` and matching HEAD; counts for a new N are added as new entries and existing ones never change, which a test checks against the file's history. The sidecar and `superseded.json` are append-only, their earlier entries unchanged, and a record cites the file's SHA-256 with the entry it used. Every evidence script exits 2 if a file a record would cite is modified in the working tree, so that `dirty_paths` never holds a cited file. The data-model introduction lists these files with their rule | B: keep each under `versions/`: one version per entry. C: unchanged: a record could cite a hash that no commit holds | data-model introduction, §1 `dirty_paths`, `code_sha256`, §5 "Exact verdicts", §11; research R4, R7, R16; cli contract "Guards shared by every script" |
| REP-063 | FR-061's test reproduces each light kind "through the script's `--reproduce` mode", but `check_claims.py` writes no record, `recheck_004.py` has no `--reproduce` mode, and `--check`'s `generator-check` records, `clear_lock.py`'s `lock-cleared` records, the cross-checks between kinds and the reproduction records have neither a mode nor a row in data-model §1's table; and a run is heavy if it "is projected to take, or last took, more than 10 minutes". Is each light kind given its reproduction or stated outside the test, and is light a property of a kind or of a run? | Add: light is a property of each run, by research R7's test, and FR-061's test reproduces, per kind, one run that was light. `recheck_004.py`, `--check` and the cross-checks between kinds gain `--reproduce RECORD` and rows in data-model §1's table, compared on their verdicts and counts. Outside the test, each with its reason: the claim test, which writes no record and runs as a test; `lock-cleared`, which records an event; and the reproduction records, which are not reproduced | B: decide light per kind from its 4×4 run: a kind light at 4×4 would be reproduced at 16×16 as if light. C: unchanged: some light kinds cannot be reproduced as the test requires | spec FR-061, FR-053, User Story 4 Independent Test; research R7, R16; data-model §1 "Fields compared, per kind"; cli contract `recheck_004.py`, `check_claims.py`, `clear_lock.py`, "Generators of constants and vectors" |
| REP-064 | Research R3 records "The fingerprint of each kind, of the state kind at every supported n and w" in `protocol/devinputs_v3.py`, written in plan phase 1, while each candidate logs its own reduced variants after setup and the rule is frozen ("nothing may change"). Does `load` refuse an (n, w) with no fingerprint, and is adding one a change to a frozen module? | Clarify: research R3's "every supported n and w" means FR-018's sizes with research R14's widths (4 at 2×2 only, 8, 16 and 32), at most 16 pairs, all written in plan phase 1 before the first record. `load` refuses, exit 2, any other (n, w) or a kind with no fingerprint, naming it. A variant outside these pairs is outside R14 anyway, so nothing is added later and the module stays as frozen; a test checks that every allowed pair has a fingerprint | B: an append-only file of fingerprints beside the module: flexible, but research R14 allows no other pair, and it would be a second file to cite. C: unchanged: an (n, w) with no fingerprint has no stated response | research R3; data-model §2; spec FR-040; plan "Phases and gates" 1 |
| REP-065 | Research R1 says that a reproduction "may run in either environment, whichever made the original, and in no other", while data-model §1 and FR-053 say "either of research R1's two, as recorded" and "either of the two of research R1, as recorded", and some compared values depend on the environment (the synthetic sets' numpy version, the MILP solver's scipy, the C tools built in WSL only). Is the environment stated per kind? | Align: on research R1's narrower text, a reproduction runs in the environment that made the original, as its environment record shows, and in no other; data-model §1 and FR-053 read "the environment that made the original, as recorded". The environment-dependent kinds (the synthetic draws, the MILP bounds, the C tools) need it anyway. A run in the other environment is not a reproduction and compares nothing with the original | B: allow either environment and compare only the fields that do not depend on it: the dependent fields would get no reproduction. C: unchanged: three texts state the environment two ways | research R1, R16; data-model §1 "Reproduction"; spec FR-053 |
| REP-066 | A probe on 2026-11-01 found the Store distribution of Python 3.10.11, with numpy, scipy, nistrng and pip 26.2.2 in the user site, which `-I` disables, while research R19 runs any Python that reads a downloaded file with `-I` and installs the Hypothesis wheels "into the two existing environments", and "the full `pip freeze`" differs with and without `-I`. Which interpreter path does a record's `command` give, how is `pip freeze` taken, and how are the wheels installed? | Add: research R1 states the Windows environment as found: the Store distribution of Python 3.10.11, named in `command` by the full path of the versioned prefix's `python.exe` (`sys.executable`, the alias resolved), with its packages in the user site, which evidence runs use. `pip freeze` for the environment record and for the start check is `<that path> -m pip freeze --all`, without `-I`. The wheels are installed with `<that path> -I -m pip install --user --no-index --no-deps` followed by the paths of research R19's three Windows wheels, run from outside the download directory, so that pip reads the downloaded files under `-I` as R19 requires; if that pip refuses a user install under `-I`, the install stops for the owner's decision, and nothing runs from the download directory. A check after the install compares `pip freeze` with research R18's pins | B: use the user site's pip 26.2.2 without `-I`: Python would read the downloads without isolation, against research R19. C: unchanged: which pip installs the wheels, and where, stays open | research R1, R18, R19; data-model §1 `command`, `environment`; spec FR-061; plan "Phases and gates" 1 |
| REP-067 | Research R18 adds `hypothesis==6.168.5` and `sortedcontainers==2.4.0` with no marker once Hypothesis is approved, not once it is installed, while an environment record "is written only when its Python and pinned packages match this section and the pins of `requirements.txt` for that platform", and research R17 and R19 allow a wheel not obtained. What does `requirements.txt` hold without a wheel, and how does the test suite behave without Hypothesis? | Align (the open item research R17 and R19 allow): the pins enter `requirements.txt` only once the wheels are installed and checked in an environment, marked for that platform, and until then they are commented out with the open item named, and the environment record is written against the file as it stands. Without Hypothesis the property tests skip with a reason naming the open item, quickstart P0 reads "all tests pass, or skip with a listed open item, and none fails", and the final report lists the skipped tests. When the wheels arrive, the pins take effect, a new environment record is approved (PRO-050), and the property tests run before any later record | B: hold all evidence until both wheels are installed: no skips, but the feature waits on a download. C: unchanged: the environment could never match its pins | research R17, R18, R19; spec FR-058, FR-053; quickstart P0 |
| REP-068 | `kind` is "one of the kinds of sections 4–11, `projection`, `lock-cleared`, `reproduction` or `recheck`" and `record_id` begins with it, but the demonstration, a tool validation, a cross-check, the D4 choice, its tie and tie choice, a round rule, a selection, the size profile, the limits and a generator run have no kind name, and file names take several forms (`candidates/<id>/<kind>-<record_id>.json`, `controls/<test>-<record_id>.json`, `rechecks/<record_id>.json`, `d4/choice.json`). Is every kind named, is every file name built by one rule, and are the eight hex digits random or derived? | Add: data-model §1 lists every kind, `demonstration`, `validation`, `cross-check`, `d4-choice`, `d4-tie`, `d4-tie-choice`, `round-rule`, `selection`, `selection-tie-choice`, `size-profile`, `limits`, `generator-run`, `generator-check`, `projection`, `lock-cleared`, `reproduction` and `recheck`, with sections 5 and 6's kinds, and `fut-sec-8-reproduction` goes (REP-056). One rule names every record file, `<directory>/<record_id>.json`, the ID beginning with its kind and the directory being the kind's place (`candidates/<id>/`, `controls/`, `validation/`, …); fixed-name files keep their names, with `versions/`. The eight hex digits are the first eight of the SHA-256 of the record's canonical JSON without `record_id` and its own path | B: list the kinds only: two implementations could still name a record differently. C: unchanged: another implementation would write different names | data-model §1, §4 to §11; cli contract `evidence_v3.py` and every script that writes records; spec FR-061 |
| REP-069 | The plan's tree lists two `design_freeze.py`, one under `protocol/` ("new: freeze create, approve and validate (FR-043)") and one under `scripts/` ("create, approve and validate the freeze"), while the contracts run `scripts/design_freeze.py` only and the freeze record hashes neither; and its reports tree omits `locks/`, where `clear_lock.py` writes, and the `versions/` directory beside each fixed-name file. Is the tree completed, with each file's role stated once? | Align: on the contracts, `protocol/design_freeze.py` holds the freeze's code, create, approve and validate as functions, and `scripts/design_freeze.py` is the command that calls it, as the other scripts call `protocol/evidence.py`. The tree states each role once, and FR-043's list and data-model §13's hashes add both files. The reports tree gains `locks/`, for the `lock-cleared` records, and `generator-runs/` (REP-060), and a line saying that every fixed-name file keeps its earlier contents in `versions/` beside it | B: keep one script holding everything: the freeze's code would sit in a script while every other record writer is in `protocol/`. C: unchanged: which file holds the freeze's code, and which one is hashed, stays open | plan "Project Structure"; spec FR-043; data-model introduction, §13; cli contract `design_freeze.py`, `clear_lock.py`; quickstart F1 |

## Second re-run of 2026-11-01

The 56 items other than conflicts that the second re-run of 2026-11-01 found over the complete
documents and the repository, in checklist order: 6 security, 5 design-rationale, 9 analysis, 6
protocol, 13 hardware and 17 reproducibility. They were answered on 2026-11-01, "all recommended",
and applied, as were the 15 conflicts above.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-045 | Constitution Principle IV forbids describing the generator as "random", "high-entropy" or "cryptographically secure" beyond what committed evidence supports, while FR-050, FR-069's rules and SC-015 name only "secure" and its kin. Do they cover "random" and "high-entropy", with the uses that stay allowed named? | Add: FR-069 gains a rule that flags "random", "truly random", "randomness" and "high-entropy" in every scanned line, and FR-050 and SC-015 name them beside "secure". The uses that stay allowed pass through standing allow-list entries, one fixed phrase each with its reason: the project's name, PhycoWeb-PRNG; "random permutation" and "ideal permutation" in a bound's statement; random nonces as a usage rule; the random baseline and the uniform controls of the tests; and "an established cryptographic random-number generator" in the disclaimer. Any other flagged line needs an entry of its own (data-model §15) | B: leave "random" and "high-entropy" to the owner's review: Principle IV's own words would go unchecked by the test. C: unchanged | spec FR-050, FR-069, SC-015; data-model §15 `allow_list`; research R18 (`tests/test_claims.py`) |
| SEC-046 | How far do "near a standard's name" and "beside the model" reach, and does a rule's own text pass of itself or only through an allow-list entry, given lines such as the Assumptions' AIS 20/31 sentence, FR-025's "as with constant time, this is a discipline" and security.md §6's "Approved DRBG mechanisms"? | Clarify: "near" and "beside" mean the same sentence or table row, as they do for "target". Every flagged line, a rule's own text included, passes only through an allow-list entry with its reason, so "outside the rules that forbid it" is met by one entry per rule line. The scan covers every document of this feature as FR-069 lists it, README and security.md included, since FR-051 changes both; security.md §6's rows, which state a standard's own status, get entries with that reason. FR-025's sentence, which says what the model does not promise, gets an entry, and the Assumptions' AIS 20/31 sentence is reworded ("is among the downloads the owner allowed"), since its "approved" refers to a download | B: let a rule's own text pass by its section heading: any sentence under that heading would pass unseen. C: unchanged | spec FR-011, FR-025, FR-069, Assumptions; data-model §15 `allow_list`; research R18 |
| SEC-047 | Who records an approval in the Review & Approval table and commits it, and does a guard such as "Order of US1" read the spec as committed at HEAD, as `repair_log.py` does, or the working tree, now that the agent may commit and its commits carry no AI co-author line? | Add: the rows that open a guard (FUT-SEC-8 result review, D4 tie, Selection tie, Owner design review, Design freeze and each "Log repair <n>") are set to Approved and committed by the owner, as "Log repair <n>" already is; any other approval is written by the agent only on the owner's explicit word for that row, quoted with its date in the row's notes. Every guard that reads the table reads it as committed at HEAD and exits 2 while the working tree's table differs from HEAD's. The spec states that the history, with no AI co-author line, cannot show who wrote or committed a row, and the owner's review at each ⛔ gate lists the approval commits since the last gate | B: the owner signs every approval commit with a key the agent never uses, and each guard checks the signature (`git verify-commit`): authorship is shown, at the cost of a signing setup the owner keeps. C: unchanged: an uncommitted or agent-written row could open the key-loading gate | spec Review & Approval (the note under the table), FR-001, FR-003; cli contract "Order of US1" and every guard that reads the table; constitution IX (cited, unchanged) |
| SEC-049 | When is the security-target review held, given that it cites records of FUT-SEC-5's measured part (diffusion through instantiation and research R4's first-order related-key and related-nonce differences), which exist only after the candidates of phase 5, while the plan writes the chapter in phase 4 and FR-011 closes the functionality class at the same review? | Clarify: the security-target chapter is drafted in phase 4 and marked "draft, under review". The review is the first step of phase 8, after the selection and before the owner design review, with a Review & Approval row of its own; it cites the selected candidate's records of FR-029's diffusion through instantiation and R4's first-order differences, closes FR-011's functionality class, and is followed by the "L1 recorded" row when its gate passes (SEC-048) | B: hold the review in phase 4 and amend it after phase 5: the review would approve a chapter whose cited records did not exist yet. C: unchanged | plan "Phases and gates" 4 and 8; spec FR-011, FR-012, Review & Approval (a "Security-target review" row); data-model §15 |
| SEC-050 | FR-016 treats the reseed input as attacker-chosen "unless it comes from a validated SP 800-90B source", while FR-009 and data-model §10 never count it as secret "even from a secret source", D7 seeks no prediction resistance and this feature has no validated source. What does the exception do? | Align: the exception is removed, so FR-016 reads "The reseed input MUST be treated as chosen by the attacker (T9)" without exception, and adds that a later feature that brings a validated entropy source and seeks prediction resistance states its own rule under its own protocol. FR-009, data-model §10 and the edge case on a reseed without a validated source stay as written | B: keep the exception and state its effect (a reseed from a validated source counted as entropy): a property this feature can neither use nor test. C: unchanged | spec FR-016; Edge Cases "Reseed without a validated entropy source" (cited, unchanged) |
| SEC-051 | T11 opens "quantum queries where the oracle allows them", while FR-010's only quantum check, k/2 ≥ s, holds with equality; multi-target quantum key search over u keys falls below 2^s for every u > 1 in some cost models, and quantum attacks on the capacity through queries to the public Π are not addressed. Are they adopted with a bound or excluded with a reason, and is security.md's "A 256-bit key keeps a margin" right? | Add: FR-008's threat model states that the 128-bit target is a classical one and that, against T11, this feature covers only single-target key search, k/2 = s, met with equality and no margin. Multi-target quantum key search (about 2^((k − log₂ u)/2) in some cost models), quantum attacks on the capacity through queries to Π and every other quantum generic attack are excluded with that reason and listed open for feature 005, and the size profile records the equality as such. security.md §12.2 reads "A 256-bit key meets k/2 = 128 with equality and no margin; multi-target quantum search falls below it for u > 1" | B: adopt a quantum target: (k − log₂ u_max)/2 for a stated u_max and a quantum capacity bound in the size profile, a target this feature has no analysis for. C: unchanged | spec FR-008, FR-010; data-model §10 (`parameters.json`); security.md §5 T11, §12.2 (version 2.13) |
| RAT-041 | Is the rotation-amount search charged to a budget row of research R7 and bounded by it, and how does a point of the search space rank when its trail search reaches the 8 h cap and gives no bound? | Add: R7's list of what is charged names the rotation-amount search: its searches are trail searches on the candidate and count against its 48 h row, each instance capped at 8 h, and its projection reserves hours like any other run (ANA-043). A point whose search reaches its cap has no bound and ranks below every point with both bounds, in the stated tie order among such points, and the full ranking records each point's bounds or `inconclusive`. If no point has both bounds at the search width, the candidate has no amounts there, so it is not scored at that width (R6) | B: a row of its own for the search (for example 12 h): it could not consume the hours the production-width bounds need, at the cost of a fourth budget row. C: unchanged | research R7 "What else is charged", R15 "Rotation amounts"; spec FR-024; data-model §3 |
