| RAT-042 | What counts as "the constant table contains a listed sequence": how many consecutive words make a match, which tables it applies to, and how a listed constant of another width is compared, given that at w = 4 a single-word match is certain? | Settle: each listed constant set is read as one bit string, its words in published order and most significant bit first, and a match is a run of consecutive table words of at least 64 bits (16 words at w = 4, 8 at w = 8, 4 at w = 16 and 2 at w = 32) equal to as many consecutive w-bit words of a listed string read from any multiple of w, also after one word rotation or the complement applied to every word. The check covers every table the script writes, one per supported (n, w), and each call type's stream. The record states the chance of an accidental match, at most T·L·2w·2⁻⁶⁴ for T table words and L listed words, so a failure shows reuse rather than chance | B: a single 32-bit word at w = 32 and 64 bits at smaller widths: accidental matches would become likely enough to block the freeze by chance. C: unchanged | spec Re-decided feature-002 requirements "FR-007", FR-023; research R15 "Refused constants" |
| RAT-043 | Who adds a design to the refused list after it is frozen, how is the addition recorded, which designs named later are added, and what follows when an addition matches, before or after the freeze? | Add: an addition is proposed by the agent and approved by the owner as a stricter change in the protocol change log, since the list only grows, with the list's new SHA-256. Designs that the spec, the plan, research.md or security.md name later are added when named, as the rationale's citations are, and the check runs again. A match makes each candidate whose polynomial or table it touches fail FR-023, an eligibility item, so that candidate is `ineligible` and the rules select among the others; a match in the shared polynomial touches every candidate and ends in a stop. The rule is never changed to escape a match. A match found after the freeze is a finding recorded at once in security.md and the report (FR-051), and `phyco-v3` stays as frozen | B: let a later match change the rule to the next polynomial: looser, a rule changed to escape a result. C: unchanged | research R15 "Refused constants"; spec FR-023, FR-044, FR-051, Re-decided feature-002 requirements "FR-007"; data-model §18 |
| RAT-046 | A decline at the owner design review may end in "a further candidate within the stop rule, which the rules must then select", yet the review cannot put another eligible candidate in the rules' place. If the declined candidate stays eligible and the rules select it again over the further one, what is the outcome? | Settle: the declined candidate stays eligible and in the set, the decline's reasons are logged, a further candidate is entered within the stop rule and evaluated like any other, and `round_rule.py --select` runs again over the whole set. If the rules select the further candidate, it goes to the review; if they select the declined one again, the outcome is a stop, logged with the review's reasons. The declined candidate is never brought back to the review unchanged, and a revision of its document, the decline's first outcome, stays possible instead | B: make the declined candidate ineligible: the review would then choose the runner-up, putting a candidate in the rules' place. C: bring it back to the review: a loop with no end | spec FR-041, FR-043, Review & Approval "Owner design review"; data-model §3, §9 |
| RAT-047 | FR-030 asks for an evidence record (FR-037, FR-039) for each replaced mechanism, yet S5 and S6 are answered by specification (the rate part after a full call; fixed operations), S11 by the designed nonlinear component, whose evidence is a trail bound (FR-038), and FR-051's lessons table cites records for S1, S3 and S4, which the key, the security target and the mode answer by specification. What evidence counts for each? | Clarify: for each of S1–S15 the lessons tables cite one of two kinds of evidence, named per row: a record of FR-037, FR-038 or FR-039 where a measurement or search shows that the failure does not recur (S2, S7, S12–S15, S8's rotational pairs, and S11's trail-bound records with the D4 choice record), or, where the answer is a property of the specification, the design document's section that states it and the test that checks it (S1 and S3 the key and the security target, S4 the mode, S5 the full call's rate output, FR-015, S6 the fixed operations, FR-020, S9 and S10 bijectivity by construction and the generic bound). FR-030 and FR-051 read "the evidence record, or the section and test where the answer is by specification" | B: require a record for every mechanism: S1 and S3 to S6 would need records of facts no measurement shows. C: unchanged | spec FR-030, FR-051; the design document's and the final report's lessons tables; security.md §9.2 (cited) |
| ANA-037 | With which R_cur do a candidate's frequency and avalanche tests start, in what order do the kinds of evidence that feed the round rule run, and does a rise of R found after the bias tests extend them to the new R? | Settle: at each size the kinds run in plan phase 5's order: trail bounds (R_bound), then the structural checks (R_f, R_b, symmetries, invariants and the degree bounds of RAT-044), then the frequency test, then the avalanche test. The bias tests start at the R the earlier kinds give, max(2·R_dist, R_bound + 2, 2·max(R_f, R_b)), and R_cur rises as R4 says. Any later rise, from either bias test, a reproduction's finding or a stricter change, extends every bias test already run at that size to the new R, as a new measurement of the same kind charged to the 12 h row (R7); if the row cannot hold it, that size's bias evidence is inconclusive and the candidate `ineligible` (PRO-046) | B: run the bias tests at a fixed, generous count (for example 2·R_bound + 8) and never extend: simpler, but a rise above it would leave rounds unmeasured. C: unchanged | research R4 "Rounds measured", R7, R13; spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule" and "Bias rule"; plan phase 5 |
| ANA-038 | Must the avalanche test's detection power be shown before its first counted use, as the frequency test's is by 100 synthetic sets planted at its 90%-power biases, given that research R4 states 0.0443, 0.0451 and 0.0465 at 90% power, while R13's planted gap and the cross-check show only differences that never flip? | Add: the avalanche test's validation record also shows its verdict code and its power without new inputs: the verdict function is applied to every count 0 to N_a of one difference bit and must fire exactly on the counts at or beyond the two critical values of `critical_counts.json`, and the power at δ_A = 0.0443, 0.0451 and 0.0465 is computed exactly from the binomial distribution and must be at least 0.9, as R4's table states. R13's planted gap and the cross-check keep validating the counting. One record per size covers the flip set, the related-key and related-nonce set and the rotational pairs, which share N_a and the threshold. R3 does not change | B: 100 synthetic sets with a planted difference bit, as for the frequency test: an addition to R3's class 5 after its freeze, recorded as REP-029's was. C: unchanged: the test's power to detect a bias stays unshown | research R4 "Validation", R13 "Known results"; data-model §7; spec FR-039 |
| ANA-039 | What happens when a uniform control fires by chance, given that the controls involve no candidate, so the firing recurs for every candidate and in the one re-run, which runs its control again with the same counts, while there is no tool defect to fix? | Settle: each test's control runs once per size, before the test's first use at that size by any candidate, and its one record serves every candidate. If it fires, that size's bias evidence is inconclusive for every candidate, so none is eligible at that size, and the feature ends in a stop (recommend stopping) citing the control record; the control inputs are never redrawn or replaced. R4 states the chance accepted: at most 0.0025 per size for the frequency control by the union bound (2b bits at 0.005/S) and below 0.005/128 for the avalanche control. The re-run's "control again first" applies only after a recorded tool defect in the control's own code | B: a second public control set (`control2`), fixed now and used once after a firing: looser, since a firing could be replaced. C: unchanged: the outcome is implied but never stated | research R3, R4 "Uniform control", R7; spec Edge Cases "The bias rule fires on its uniform control"; data-model §5 `control`; quickstart E3 |
| ANA-040 | Is a trail tool of research R12's fixed set identified by name or by its exact hash, and may a search that reached its 8 h cap run again with a faster validated version of the same tool? | Settle: the fixed set names tools by their rows in R12's table (ARX search, active-S-box counting, long-trail active-box counting, exhaustive tables); each version is identified by its exact hash and counts only with a `re-found` record of that hash, so a fixed or faster version of a named tool is the same tool and may serve searches not yet run, after its re-validation. A search that reached its cap is never run again, with another tool or another version of the same one, except as R7's one re-run after a recorded tool defect, and a speed-up is not a defect. The "Trail tools" guard checks the name against the fixed set and the hash against a `re-found` record | B: allow one re-run of a capped search with a faster validated version: looser, a second attempt after an unwelcome cap. C: unchanged | research R7 "Re-runs", R12 "Fixed tool set", "Re-validation"; cli contract "Trail tools", "Tool validation"; spec FR-039 |
| ANA-041 | Are research R12's cross-checks between kinds (the ARX search reproducing the exhaustive box tables at w = 4 and 8 before it counts at 16 and 32, and the active-S-box counter reproducing the exhaustive trail optima of "a χ-like candidate" at w = 4) validation runs or the "solver cross-checks" of R7's 48 h row, once per tool or per candidate, and which design serves when no χ-like candidate is entered? | Clarify: they run on each candidate's own boxes, once per candidate and tool version, as the "solver cross-checks" of that candidate's 48 h row, each instance capped at 8 h: the ARX search on an A candidate's ARX-box at w = 4 and 8 before its searches at 16 and 32 count, and the active-S-box counter on a B candidate's nonlinear layer at w = 4, whatever its form, so "a χ-like candidate" reads "each Boolean candidate". A failed cross-check is a tool defect (R7). The validations of R12's table, on published values, stay validation runs charged to no candidate | B: run them once per tool version on fixed public designs, as validation runs: cheaper, but they would not check the tool on the boxes it bounds. C: unchanged | research R7 "What else is charged", R12 "Cross-checks between kinds"; data-model §6, §7 |
| ANA-042 | Research R12 takes HiGHS's optimal or infeasible status as a proof of a lower bound, under feasibility and integrality tolerances that scipy does not expose. Is that assumption stated with its limits, and is an exact or independent confirmation required for a bound that sets R_bound? | Add: every MILP bound record states that its status rests on HiGHS's built-in tolerances, recorded as R12 says, and the design document and the final report say so beside R_bound. A bound that sets R_bound is confirmed where an exact method exists: at w = 4 and 8 by the exhaustive tables (R12's cross-checks), with weights kept as rationals rounded down as now. At 16 and 32 no exact confirmation exists without a further tool, so those bounds are reported as proven by the solver within its tolerances, a limitation stated in each record's `limitations` (data-model §1) | B: require an independent confirmation of each bound that sets R_bound, for example by a SAT solver: a candidate tool only, whose download needs the owner's permission for that file (name, source, size, licence) before the tool set is fixed. C: unchanged: an unstated assumption under every bound | research R12 "Solver settings", "What counts as a bound"; data-model §1 `limitations`, §6; spec FR-038 |
| ANA-043 | Does research R7's reservation protect the kinds of evidence that share a budget row, given that a run starts when its projection plus the probes of "the same kind of run at every larger claimable size" fit the hours left, so the frequency test could use hours the avalanche test needs; and in what order do a row's kinds run? | Stricter (a run starts in fewer cases; the reservation only grows): a run starts only if its projection, plus the probes of every run its row still needs (its own kind at every larger size and every other kind of the row at every claimable size), fits the hours left. Each row's kinds run in a fixed order: the 12 h row the frequency test, then the avalanche test, each with its control; the 6 h row the inverse tests, exhaustive bijectivity, dependency matrices, symmetry and invariant checks and the degree bounds (RAT-044); the 48 h row the reduced-width and rotation-amount searches, then box tables, box-trail searches, active-box or active-S-box counting and the solver cross-checks. The order of sizes stays | B: split each row into fixed shares per kind (for example 6 h and 6 h): simpler, but one share could run out while the other's hours sit unused. C: unchanged | research R7 "Projection before every heavy run"; cli contract "Budget" |
| ANA-044 | Is "the tools' validation" a property that does not depend on the neighbourhood, as FR-032 and FR-052 assume when they let it transfer from 2×2, given that at 2×2 opposite neighbours coincide, that the symmetry check's only known result is S7's rotation structure on a 4-node ring, and that the grid size of the dependency propagation's operation list is not given? | Align: FR-032 and FR-052 transfer from 2×2 only the validation of tools whose behaviour does not depend on the neighbourhood (box and S-box tables, the bijectivity bitmap, the cost model's counts). The dependency propagation is validated on its stated small operation list at 3×3 and 4×4, with hand-derived matrices at each; the symmetry check, beside S7's result, on stated lists with planted torus symmetries (a translation, a rotation by 90° and a reflection) at n = 3 and 4, which it must find, and on one with none, which it must clear. Each list and its expected result is written with its SHA-256 and entered in the provenance log before the tool is drafted (R13) | B: validate at 4×4 only: covers the smallest claimable size, but leaves the odd neighbourhoods of 3×3 unvalidated. C: unchanged | spec FR-032, FR-052; research R13 "Known results"; data-model §7 |
| ANA-045 | Who or what assigns a record's `classification`, given that `status` is computed by code but `classification` is not said to be, that a reproduction compares it and that every record holds one, records without a finding included; and which class does a firing that raises R, or a search that reached its cap, get? | Clarify: `classification` is computed by code (FR-065) from a table in data-model §1 by kind and outcome, and a reviewer may add only a note. Records without a finding (vectors, constants, projections, validations, a search at its cap) hold `none`, since security.md §9.1 classifies findings; a capped search keeps `status` `inconclusive`. A firing, or a trail found by a validated tool, that raises R is a confirmed vulnerability of the reduced rounds it covers, worded with its round count and the stated family-wise chance of a false firing, since a possible weakness only raises R (data-model §5); a test that does not fire is "no weakness identified within the analysis performed", with its scope; a finding of an unvalidated tool stays an unverified hypothesis, `blocked` | B: class a firing that raises R as a strong theoretical concern: a softer label for a result that still raises R. C: unchanged | data-model §1 `classification`; spec FR-060, FR-065, SC-017; security.md §9.1 (cited) |
| PRO-042 | Which acts stay the owner's own under constitution 1.3.0's standing authorization (the freeze's step (1) "the owner commits the feature files", step (3) `design_freeze.py approve`, step (4) "the owner commits the record", and the approval of a protocol change), and what shows that the owner performed each, given that commits carry no AI co-author line, that `approve --by NAME` takes any name and that a change-log entry's `approver` is filled in by its writer? | Add: FR-043 and FR-044 list the owner's own acts, outside the standing authorization as constitution amendments are: the freeze's steps (1), (3) and (4), the approval of every protocol change, and every Review & Approval decision that opens a guard (SEC-047). The agent never performs them, even when asked in general terms. `design_freeze.py approve` and the change log's approval step print the hash being approved and refuse unless standard input is an interactive terminal and the owner types its first 16 hex digits; each records that confirmation. The spec states that the history cannot show who acted, and the owner's review at each ⛔ gate lists these acts since the last gate | B: the owner signs the commits of these acts with a key the agent never uses, and `design_freeze.py validate` checks the signatures: authorship is shown, with a signing setup the owner keeps. C: unchanged | spec FR-043, FR-044; data-model §13, §18; cli contract `design_freeze.py`; research R16; constitution IX (cited, unchanged) |
| PRO-043 | The Pre-registered Design Protocol's "Development inputs" paragraph names a closed class of constructed inputs with four members, while FR-039, FR-040, research R3 and data-model §2 add class 5, the bias tool's synthetic counts (the settled answer of REP-029). Does the pre-registered paragraph agree? | Align: the paragraph lists (5), "synthetic counts for the bias test's validation only, drawn by a public rule from seeds hashed from public strings (research R3)", so the protocol's own text names the class the documents use. The class stays closed, no input changes, so "the derivation does not change" holds, and the change-log entry for REP-029 records the paragraph's old and new text | B: leave the paragraph and cite R3: the pre-registered text would name four members of a five-member class. C: unchanged | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Development inputs"; data-model §18 (the entry for REP-029) |
| PRO-045 | The Review & Approval rows for the checklist choices say "ticking stays with the reviewer", while on 2026-11-01 the owner asked the agent to verify every item and tick each one that holds. Is it recorded who ticked each item, on whose instruction, and whether the tasks approval includes the owner's review of the agent's ticks? | Align: the two rows' notes read "On 2026-11-01 the owner asked the agent to verify each item against the text and tick it if it holds; the agent did so, with its evidence for each item in its report. The owner's tasks approval includes the review of those ticks, and an item the owner does not accept is unticked." Each checklist's Notes record the instruction, its date and the commits that ticked items, so a ticked item shows a determination the owner delegated and then reviewed, as each Review Ownership note requires; items of later runs follow the same rule. This document's "ticks no checklist item" stays true of itself | B: untick every item the agent ticked and leave them to the owner: the note's literal rule, at the cost of the verification already done. C: unchanged: the records would say the reviewer ticked items the agent ticked | spec Review & Approval (both "Checklist choices" rows, "Tasks approval"); the six checklists' Notes |
| PRO-047 | How are the protocol changes of checklist rounds after the first two recorded, given that the change log's first entries are the answered rows "of both runs of 2026-11-01", fixed by row ID before the log is created, while this round's rows may be answered before the log exists; where is that list of row IDs written, and who approves it? | Add: this document gains, per round, a list "Rows that change the protocol", naming each answered row whose option alters a pre-registered rule, whatever its kind (Add, Align, Clarify, Settle or Stricter), with the rule it alters; the owner's answer to a round approves its list. Data-model §18 and plan "Values this plan fixes" read "the answered rows of every checklist round answered before the log is created, by those lists, in round order". A round answered after the log exists adds entries the usual way, proposed by the agent and approved by the owner (FR-044) | B: one list for all rounds, written when the log is created: the rows would be picked after the fact. C: unchanged | checklist-choices.md (a list per round); data-model §18; plan "Values this plan fixes", phase 1; spec FR-044 |
| PRO-048 | Is the moment of the D4 choice fixed by a condition `d4_choice.py` checks, given that research R6 computes it "after every candidate entered for it has been scored", while the script refuses only when `choice.json` exists or no entered set is recorded; and when does the choice proceed if an entered candidate cannot be scored, for example because a reduced-width search reached its cap? | Settle: `d4_choice.py` exits 2 until every candidate of the recorded entered set has, at 16 and, where R6 needs it, at 8, a final reduced-width outcome for each bound kind: a bound record, the `inconclusive` record of a capped search, or an `ineligible` or `rejected` log decision. A capped search counts as no bound at that width, so the choice proceeds once every entered candidate has an outcome, whatever it is, and the choice record lists each outcome. No search is retried to reach a score (ANA-040) | B: proceed at a fixed point (for example once the reduced-width hours of every trail row are spent): a candidate whose search still runs would be left out by the clock, not the rule. C: unchanged | research R6 "When"; cli contract `d4_choice.py`; data-model §8 |
| PRO-049 | When does the candidate set close and `round_rule.py --select` apply selection and the stop rule, given that the edge cases say that the feature "tries another candidate within the stop rule's budget or recommends stopping" and that "a different candidate may be tried" when R is expensive, with no rule that decides, so that a candidate added after the values of R are seen could change which one the smallest R at 4×4 selects? | Settle: candidates are entered before any is scored (the entered set of R6), and a further candidate may be entered later only in place of one that became `rejected` or `ineligible`, within the stop rule's three per family, never because another's R is high; the edge cases read so. The set closes at a `set-closed` log entry, written by `round_rule.py --close` once every candidate has a final decision and no replacement is pending, and `--select` exits 2 before it. A decline at the owner design review reopens the set once, for one further candidate (RAT-046) | B: close the set at a date fixed now: a candidate still being evaluated at that date would be dropped by the clock. C: unchanged | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Eligibility and selection", "Stop rule", Edge Cases, FR-041; cli contract `round_rule.py`; data-model §3 |
| HW-045 | FR-025's list of zeroization triggers names "a per-key limit reached", while its failure paths, like the model contract, zeroize on "a call refused at a per-key limit". Which moment zeroizes, and can the host tell from the ports which refusals zeroized and which changed nothing, given one error signal and FR-020's single formula for "a refused request"? | Align: the trigger reads "a call refused at a per-key limit", as in the model: the call that would pass the limit is refused and zeroizes, and nothing happens when a counter merely reaches the limit. The core gains a registered error-code output beside the error signal and valid with it, with one code per refusal that changes nothing (a length, a call out of sequence, any other limit) and one per zeroizing refusal (a per-key limit, an error path), listed in the design document. FR-020 gives the cycle count of a zeroizing refusal apart from the others, since it adds the zeroization's latency | B: zeroize as soon as a counter reaches a per-key limit: the key would leave one call earlier, but the device's next refusal (no key) would differ from the model's (a limit). C: unchanged | spec FR-025 "Zeroization", FR-020; model contract "Per-key limit", "Failure paths" (cited); the design document |
| HW-046 | Is the one-block output buffer the "output register" that zeroization clears, and must it and the registers of both trees be cleared when a request ends, so that neither the last block delivered nor the discarded rest of a partial last block stays in the core between calls? | Add: FR-025 names the one-block buffer as the output register. It and the registers of both trees are cleared when a request ends, with or before its forget call, so the rest of a partial last block is discarded by clearing, not left in place; the clearing's cycles join FR-020's formulas, and feature 006 checks it in simulation (FR-058). The model, which holds no buffer, is unchanged | B: clear them only at zeroization: the last block delivered would stay in the core until the next call, against FR-004's backtracking resistance across the forget call. C: unchanged | spec FR-025 "Flow control", "Zeroization", FR-020; the design document |
| HW-047 | How does a zeroization other than reset (an explicit zeroize, a failed self-test, an error path, a per-key limit) reach every node, both trees, the buffer and the replicated counters; how does each food node's ROM learn the call type where call types draw on distinct constant streams; and are both paths among the grid-wide signals the plan's Complexity Tracking justifies? | Add: every zeroization trigger drives the reset's registered fan-out tree, so it reaches every register within the reset's latency, which each trigger's figure counts. The call type travels with the start signal on its registered tree, as a field of ⌈log₂ t⌉ bits for t call types, registered beside it at every node, and selects the ROM's stream. Complexity Tracking lists both beside the trees, the clock, reset, the start signal and the counters, each justified as a public control signal with one stated latency | B: a zeroize tree and a call-type tree of their own: independent latencies, at twice the registers. C: unchanged | spec FR-025 "Zeroization", FR-018, FR-023; plan "Complexity Tracking"; the design document |
| HW-048 | Is the hardware path that places the key and the nonce at their stated positions at every instantiate specified (where the key register sits, how its bits and the nonce reach their lanes, with what latency), and is that path justified in Complexity Tracking, given that FR-018's registered trees run over the rate lanes only? | Add: the design document specifies the key path: a key register at each node that holds key bits, loaded once per key along a registered shift chain through those nodes and copied into its lanes in one cycle at every instantiate. The nonce reaches its lanes through the absorption tree where they are rate lanes, and along the same chain otherwise. The chain's length enters the key-loading figure and the copy the instantiate figure (FR-025); Complexity Tracking lists the chain as a local data path of public length, and zeroization clears every copy | B: one central key register with a registered tree to the key lanes: a grid-wide path carrying secret data, to be justified like the output tree. C: unchanged | spec FR-013, FR-018, FR-025 "Key loading and reset"; plan "Complexity Tracking"; the design document |
| HW-049 | Is the hardware call sequence specified as a whole, including the states the model has no counterpart for (no key held, after reset or a zeroization; a key held and no instantiate yet), with the calls each state accepts and the response to every other call? | Add: the design document gives the call sequence as a state table: `no-key` (after a reset with a passed self-test, or after a zeroization) accepts a key load only; `key-held` accepts instantiate and zeroize; `instantiated` accepts the calls the model's call sequence allows, and zeroize. Every other call is refused with its error code (HW-045) and changes nothing, among them a key load while a key is held (zeroize first) and an instantiate with no key. The refusals match the model's `CallSequenceError` rules where the model has the state, and FUT-HW-5's "key zeroization on reset" is tested from the ports: after a reset, an instantiate is refused as `no-key` | B: let a key load while a key is held replace the key without a zeroization: state from the old key could survive into the new key's use. C: unchanged | spec FR-025 "Key loading and reset", "Usage rules across resets"; model contract "Call sequence" (cited); the design document; traceability row FUT-HW-5 |
| HW-050 | Does the start-up self-test's output stay inside the core, compared there and never driven on the output port, with output-valid low throughout, given that output-valid stays low after reset only "until an instantiate completes" and the self-test runs an instantiate? | Clarify: the self-test's blocks go only to its comparator, which takes each block as it is produced, so no consumer handshake is involved; output-valid stays low throughout and the output register holds zeros. FR-025 reads "until an instantiate requested by the host completes after a passed self-test", and the zeroization that follows the self-test (FR-059) clears what it computed | B: drive the self-test's output on the port with a flag: a testability aid that would put a known-answer block on the port at every reset. C: unchanged | spec FR-025 "Zeroization", "Flow control", FR-059; the design document |
| HW-051 | What does the core do between FPGA configuration and the first reset, given that reset is synchronous and the self-test is a step of the reset sequence, so a device configured but never reset would run without it? | Add: every register of the core has a stated initial value of zero, given as initial values in the VHDL, which configuration applies, and the call-sequence state starts as `not-reset`: output-valid is low and every call is refused with its error code until a reset has run the self-test (HW-049). Feature 008's board wrapper also holds reset for a stated number of cycles after configuration, and FR-059's tests cover a call made before the first reset | B: rely on the board wrapper's power-on reset alone: the core's own behaviour before it would stay unspecified. C: unchanged | spec FR-025 "Key loading and reset", FR-059; the design document; traceability row FUT-HW-5 |
| HW-052 | Does FR-020's counting rule cover the input interfaces as well as the output, so that the cycles in which the core waits for the host's data during key loading, a personalization string or a reseed input are excluded as the consumer's stalls are? | Clarify: FR-020's formulas count the cycles in which the core works: on output, the cycles with ready high; on input, the cycles with valid high while the core's ready is high, so a pause by the host on any interface is excluded. FR-020 states both directions, and feature 008 counts the same cycles from the interface signals, so a measured count matches its formula at any pace the host keeps | B: count every cycle from a call's start to its end: the measured count would depend on the host's pace. C: unchanged | spec FR-020, FR-025 "Flow control"; traceability row FUT-HW-5 |
| HW-053 | Are the data formats of the key-loading and absorption interfaces stated (bits per transfer, how the core learns the length in bytes of a personalization string or a reseed input before it pads it, the value of unused bits in a last partial word), and is the hardware's rule for a request length that is not a whole number of output words stated? | Add: the design document states 32-bit transfers on the key-loading and absorption interfaces; a personalization string or reseed input preceded by one 32-bit word giving its length in bytes, refused above the design's maximum; unused bytes of a last partial word that must be zero, a word with a nonzero unused byte being refused with its error code; and the key and nonce as whole words in a stated word and byte order, matching the model's. A generate request gives its length in 32-bit words, so a length that is not a whole number of words cannot be expressed, as the model refuses an `nbits` that is not a positive multiple of 32, and a length of zero is refused | B: a last-word flag with a byte count, as AXI-Stream's byte enables do: no length word, but the length known only when the last word arrives. C: unchanged | spec FR-025 "Flow control", FR-014, FR-020; model contract "Output" (cited); the design document |
| HW-054 | Does FR-026's LUT and flip-flop estimate count what FR-025 and FR-059 add beside the nodes, trees and ROMs (the one-block output buffer, the key register and key-loading buffer, the replicated counters, the limit counters, the self-test's stored key, nonce and expected output), and is block RAM estimated, as FUT-HW-2 asks for "LUT/FF/BRAM use"? | Add: FR-026's estimate lists each part with its own figure: the nodes and stages, the trees, the ROMs, the output buffer (r bits, 16,128 at 16×16) with its read-out to the interface width, the key register and key-loading buffer, the replicated round and phase counters, the limit counters, and the self-test's key, nonce and expected output held as constants; and block RAM, expected to be 0 unless a ROM is mapped to it. Feature 008 compares each part with Vivado's hierarchical utilization report | B: one stated allowance for everything beyond the nodes: a single number feature 008 could not check part by part. C: unchanged | spec FR-026, FR-025, FR-059; research R5; traceability row FUT-HW-2 |
| HW-055 | Is P, the number of phases in a round, the number of colours (two for even n, three for odd n) or the number of phases in the candidate's operation list, which could visit a colour twice, and do the cycle formulas use that one P per candidate and size? | Clarify: P is the number of phases in one round of the candidate's operation list at that size, each phase one cycle (FR-025), so a colour visited twice counts twice, and "P = 2 for a red-black schedule" is the case of one visit per colour. FR-020's formulas, FR-026's rate and cost per output bit and research R5's cost model use that P, recorded per candidate and size in its log entry (data-model §3) | B: P as the number of colours, with extra visits counted as extra rounds: R would no longer count rounds of the operation list. C: unchanged | spec FR-025 "Stages", FR-026, FR-019; research R5, R11; data-model §3 |
| HW-056 | Must the stated three-colour rule for odd n give a proper colouring of the torus, wrap-around links included, at every odd n the generics cover, checked at each, given that a rule as simple as (i + j) mod 3 gives two neighbours across the wrap the same colour at n = 7 and 13? | Add: FR-018 requires the rule to give a proper colouring at every odd n from 3 to 15, wrap-around links included, and a test checks every link at each odd n. A rule that holds is c(i, j) = (f(i) + f(j)) mod 3, with f a proper 3-colouring of the n-cycle such as f(k) = k mod 2 for k < n − 1 and f(n − 1) = 2: two neighbours differ in one coordinate, where f differs by 1 or 2. The design document states its rule and the test's result, and FUT-GRID-1's odd sizes cite them | B: support only the odd n with n mod 3 ≠ 1 (3, 5, 9, 11 and 15) under (i + j) mod 3, with 7 and 13 listed as unsupported for that reason. C: unchanged | spec FR-018, FR-019, Edge Cases "Odd grid sizes"; research R11, R14; traceability row FUT-GRID-1 |
| HW-057 | Are FUT-HW-3's power measurement (the board's VCCINT current sensor through the XADC, beside the Vivado estimate) and its on-chip counters of words per second carried to feature 008, given that the traceability row names only the implied throughput? | Add: the FUT-HW-3 traceability row and the final report's open items for feature 008 name each measurement FUT-HW-3 asks for: words per second by on-chip counters at the achieved clock, per claimable size that fits, compared with the C stream; and power through the board's VCCINT current sensor and the XADC, beside Vivado's estimate. This feature supplies only the implied throughput (FR-026) | B: carry only the throughput: power would drop out of the requirements without a decision. C: unchanged | spec Security Policy traceability "FUT-HW-3"; the final report's open items (FR-052) |
| REP-038 | Are the fields a reproduction compares listed per kind apart from those it only reports, given that the demonstration's `cost`, every heavy record's projected and used hours and measured speed, a bound record's `budget` and the raw solver log hold timings, so that, read as written, no reproduction of these kinds can match? | Clarify: data-model §1 gains a per-kind table of compared and reported-only fields. Timings (`cost`'s seconds and steps per second, projected and used hours, measured speed, `budget`'s hours used) and the raw solver log are never compared; values, statuses, counts and verdicts are. "Each output's `sha256` and `bytes`" reads "each deterministic output's", and `outputs` marks the solver log as not deterministic, its `sha256` and `bytes` reported only. FR-053's "searches and solver runs reproduce their values and verdicts" then holds, and `fut_sec8.py --reproduce` compares the demonstration's listed fields | B: compare every field, with a tolerance on timings: a faster or slower machine would still fail. C: unchanged | data-model §1 "Reproduction", §4, §6; spec FR-053, FR-061; cli contract `fut_sec8.py` |
| REP-039 | Is `design_sha256` compared only for the kinds whose code reads the design document, given that it is `none` for records made before the document exists and that the document passes through draft versions, so that a reproduction after any edit fails on a field its run never read? | Clarify: `design_sha256` is compared only for the kinds whose code reads the design document, listed in data-model §1's per-kind table (`gen_constants_v3.py`'s restatement check, and any other reader named there), and reported for every other kind; `none` stays `none`. The FUT-SEC-8 reproduction and the tool validations on `phyco-v2` compare without it | B: compare it everywhere and freeze the document before any reproduction: every reproduction would wait for the freeze. C: unchanged | data-model §1 "Reproduction", §4, §17; spec FR-053; cli contract `fut_sec8.py` |
| REP-040 | Must every code file whose SHA-256 a record holds be kept under that hash or committed before the run, given that a reproduction needs each entry of `code_sha256` to match, that one edit to `protocol/evidence.py` leaves the code earlier records name only in git history (or nowhere if it was never committed), and that FR-053 and data-model §1 still expect `git_dirty` to be "often true"? | Add: a run that writes evidence exits 2 unless every source file in its `code_sha256` matches HEAD, committed and unmodified, which constitution 1.3.0's standing authorization to commit makes routine; data and report files may still be dirty, and a binary is rebuilt from committed source, its hash reported only. Each record's code is then found at its `git_head`, and a test checks each `code_sha256` entry against the blob at that commit. FR-053 and data-model §1 replace "with `git_dirty` often true" by "code is committed before every evidence run; `git_dirty` may be true for data and reports only, and the record lists the dirty paths" | B: keep code by hash, as spec versions are, under `code-versions/<sha256>`: works with uncommitted code, at the cost of a copy of every version. C: unchanged | spec FR-053, FR-061; data-model §1, §3; research R16; cli contract "Guards shared by every script" |
| REP-041 | Can the protocol version under which a verdict was computed be identified from its record, given that the values that decide verdicts live in research.md and change only through the protocol change log, while a record cites only `spec_sha256`; and which version does `recheck_004.py` apply to each record? | Add: every record holds `protocol_seq` and `protocol_line_sha256`, the change log's last entry when the run started (`none` before the log exists), and `research_sha256`, with research.md kept as `research-versions/<sha256>.md` as spec versions are. `recheck_004.py` recomputes each verdict under the rules in force at that entry and, where a later stricter change applies (FR-044), also under the current rules, listing both; a disagreement in either is reported | B: cite only the change log's entry and rebuild the rules from the log: the log holds changes, not the full text, so the rule in force would be reconstructed, not read. C: unchanged | data-model §1, §18; research R16; cli contract `recheck_004.py`; spec FR-044, FR-053, FR-065 |
| REP-042 | How does each record kept under a fixed name change (`candidates/<id>/round-rule.json`, `freeze/selection.json`, `security-target/parameters.json` and `limits.json`, the environment records, `downloads.json`, the review package's manifest), given the rule that a record is never overwritten, so that every record citing one by path and SHA-256 names content that still exists? | Add: these fixed-name files are the stated exceptions to "never overwritten": before one changes, `protocol/evidence.py` keeps its current content as `<dir>/versions/<sha256>.json` beside it, and the new content holds `previous_sha256`. A record that cites one gives its path and SHA-256, and the re-check resolves either from the versions. The data-model introduction lists the exceptions | B: version each by identifier (`round-rule-<record_id>.json`) with a pointer file: no file ever changes, but every reader needs the pointer. C: unchanged | data-model introduction, §1, §9, §10, §16; research R1, R16, R19; spec FR-043; cli contract `round_rule.py`, `mode_limits_v3.py` |
| REP-043 | Where are local-only outputs written, and which `.gitignore` rule keeps them out of `git status`, given that the freeze needs `git_dirty` false and counts them among the ignored files, that `.gitignore` ignores nothing under `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/` but `*.log` files, and that `protocol/evidence.py` refuses other suffixes there; and is a local-only output staged by mistake, or a committed output above 10 MB, refused? | Add: local-only outputs go under `data/local/004/<record_id>/`, which `.gitignore` gains as `data/local/`, never under `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/`, and each record's `outputs` gives their paths with `committed` false. `protocol/evidence.py` refuses a committed output above 10 MB, naming it, and a test fails if any path under `data/local/` is tracked or a committed output exceeds 10 MB; `git add` refuses an ignored path without `-f`, so a local-only output is not staged by accident | B: keep them under `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/local/` with an ignore rule there: beside the records, but one wrong rule would commit hundreds of MB. C: unchanged | research R16, R18 (`.gitignore`); data-model §1, §13; spec FR-043, Edge Cases "Large evidence records"; plan phase 1 |
| REP-044 | Does research R18's list of new line-ending pins cover every new committed file whose hash a record holds, such as `research/004/tools/critical_counts.json`, `research/004/candidates/superseded.json`, each candidate's committed reference outputs and the self-written known results, to which `git check-attr` gives `text: auto` with no `eol` on a machine with `core.autocrlf=true`? | Add: `.gitattributes` gains `research/004/** text eol=lf` and `protocol/*.json text eol=lf`, placed before the `*.hex`, `*.bin` and `*.gz` lines so that those keep precedence, and R18's list names them. R18's test (every hashed path has an explicit rule or `binary`, or its hash is marked `lf-normalized`) runs from plan phase 1 on, so a path missed later fails before any record cites it | B: pin each file by name when it is created: each new path would need its own line, and a missed one would surface only when the test runs. C: unchanged | `.gitattributes`; research R18; spec FR-053; data-model §3, §5, §7 |
| REP-045 | Is research R16's test that "checks the header of every committed `.gz` file" limited to the files this feature writes, given that feature 003's 1,000 tracked `.gz` files, listed with their hashes in its `SHA256SUMS`, all hold a file name and a nonzero time and may not change? | Clarify: the test covers the `.gz` files under this feature's paths (`reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/`, `research/004/`, `vectors/phyco-v3/` and `baseline/phyco-v2/`); feature 003's files, listed with their hashes in its `SHA256SUMS`, are outside its scope and stay unchanged | B: cover every `.gz` file with an exception list for feature 003: 1,000 entries listed one by one. C: unchanged: the test fails on files this feature may not change | research R16 "Compressed files", R18; data-model introduction "Format rules"; spec FR-053 |
| REP-046 | What record does a check of the generated files write on each platform, given that `--check` regenerates in memory and writes nothing and the sidecar `MANIFEST.json` holds only the command, interpreter, model hash, platform, speed and time, while SC-009 needs evidence computed by code; and is the sidecar among the vector files the freeze hashes? | Add: `--check` writes a `generator-check` record under `$REP/generator-checks/` with the fields of FR-061, each generated file's path and SHA-256 and the verdict `match` or `differs`; SC-009's evidence is the pair of records, one per platform, which quickstart M1 to M3 and the final report cite. The sidecar is frozen with the vectors, as the record of the runs that produced them; after the freeze a generator runs only with `--check`, which writes its record under `$REP` and never to the sidecar | B: leave the sidecar out of the freeze: it could keep growing, but would no longer record what produced the frozen files. C: unchanged | cli contract "Generators of constants and vectors"; data-model §11, §13; research R8; spec SC-009, FR-036, FR-065; quickstart M1–M3 |
| REP-047 | Is the request schedule of the mode vectors exact, given that "requests of 1, 7, 64 and 1,000 words, repeated, then the remainder" leaves 352 of 10,000 words after nine cycles, read as one request of 352 or as 1, 7 and 64 and then 280, which give different outputs since a forget call follows every request; and is `<variant>` in the file names the variant ID with `/` replaced by `_`? | Settle: the cycle runs in order and the last request takes what is left: 1, 7, 64 and 1,000 words repeated, then 1, 7 and 64, then 280 for 10,000 words, which tests more request boundaries; the header gains the field `schedule=cyclic-truncated` after `requests=1,7,64,1000`. `<variant>` is the variant ID with `/` replaced by `_`, as data-model §11 says of a variant directory's name, and the files sit in the one directory `vectors/phyco-v3/` | B: one request of the remainder (352 words): fewer request boundaries tested. C: unchanged: two readings, two outputs | research R8; data-model §11; spec FR-036 |
| REP-048 | How does `gen_vectors_v3.py` import the model's `pi` and `ResearchDuplex` on its first run, before `phycoweb/selftest_v3.py` exists, when the model contract's module rules have the model import `phycoweb.selftest_v3`? | Add: the model imports `phycoweb.selftest_v3` inside `Generator`'s constructor only, so `pi`, `pi_inv`, `pi_trace` and `ResearchDuplex` import without it; a `Generator` built before the module exists fails its self-test and leaves no object, as on any self-test failure. The model contract's module rules say so, and a test imports the model with the module absent | B: commit a placeholder `selftest_v3.py` that step (2) replaces: a checkout before step (2) would hold a self-test with no expected output. C: unchanged | model contract "Module rules", "Start-up self-test"; data-model §11 "Order of generation"; cli contract "Generators of constants and vectors"; spec FR-059 |
| REP-050 | Is the start check of `protocol/evidence.py` defined against the platform's environment record as a whole (its full `pip freeze`, Python, gcc and make) or against research R1's versions and the pins of `requirements.txt` only, and which change makes a new environment record? | Clarify: the check compares the running environment with the platform's environment record as a whole: Python, every package and version of `pip freeze` (one added, upgraded or removed), gcc and make in WSL, and the Windows build or the WSL distribution and kernel. Any difference exits 2, naming it, a Windows or WSL update included; the owner then approves a new environment record, with R1 updated when a pinned version changes, and each later record cites the record in force | B: check the pins only and record the full `pip freeze` in every run: lighter, but a record could cite an environment record that no longer describes the machine. C: unchanged | research R1, R18; data-model §1; cli contract "Guards shared by every script" |
| REP-051 | Should the markers of `requirements.txt` state a lower bound of Python 3.10, given that `python_version < "3.14"` also admits Python 3.8 and 3.9, on which numpy 2.2.6 and scipy 1.15.3 cannot be installed? | Add: the markers read `python_version >= "3.10" and python_version < "3.14"` for numpy 2.2.6 and scipy 1.15.3, and `>= "3.14"` as now for the newer pins. The file's comment says that the analysis tools need Python 3.10 or later and that the model alone needs only the standard library, so FR-035's "expected, not tested" on older versions concerns the model only | B: leave the markers and let pip fail on 3.8 and 3.9: the error would name a package, not the rule. C: unchanged | research R17, R18 (`requirements.txt`); spec FR-035 (cited) |
| REP-052 | Which bytes does each synthetic set's SHA-256 cover, are the sets committed or kept local (8,601,600 counts, about 60 MB as JSON and 17 MB as gzip, above research R16's 10 MB), and do "hashed and never redrawn" and "a reproduction in the same environment draws the sets again and compares them" read as one rule, also after a numpy upgrade? | Settle: a set's SHA-256 covers its counts as decimal text, one per line in the test's bit order, each line ending in LF, as `protocol/seedsets.py` writes values. The sets are local-only (R16), regenerated by their deterministic command, and the validation record commits each set's SHA-256, the numpy version and a per-set summary (the planted bit's count and every fired bit), so a fresh clone recomputes the 82-to-97 verdict from committed files and a regeneration checks every count. "Never redrawn" means that no set is ever replaced: a later draw must reproduce every hash in an environment with that numpy version, and a draw that differs is reported and counts for nothing. After an approved numpy upgrade the old record stands, and a new validation runs as a new record | B: commit the sets as compressed parts of at most 10 MB each: no draw needed on a fresh clone, about 17 MB more in the repository. C: unchanged | research R3, R4, R16; data-model §2, §7; spec FR-039, FR-065; cli contract `validate_tool.py` |
| REP-053 | Is the text whose SHA-256 forms each fingerprint fixed exactly: the case of the hex digits, a final newline after the value for i = 99, a state as one hex string or one field per lane, a personalization string at 32 bytes or at the design's maximum m, and whether `synthetic_seed` has a fingerprint? | Clarify: the fingerprint text is the values for i = 0…99, one per line, each line ending in LF, the last included, as `protocol/seedsets.py` writes. Keys, nonces, personalization strings and reseed inputs are lower-case hex of their bytes, as digests are written; a state is one string of its w-bit words in upper-case hex in the design document's lane order, as the format rules write states. A personalization string's fingerprint is taken over its full 32 bytes, so it does not depend on m, which the design fixes later; a use with m < 32 takes a prefix the fingerprint already covers. `synthetic_seed(n, j)` has a fingerprint at each size over j = 0…99, as decimal integers. Data-model §2 states this text, so another implementation computes the same fingerprints | B: lower case for everything, states included: one rule for fingerprints, but a state's text would differ from how states are written elsewhere. C: unchanged | data-model §2, introduction "Format rules"; research R3; spec FR-040 |
| REP-054 | Is the interface of `protocol/devinputs_v3.py` consistent: do `key(i)` and the other functions take the namespace their strings contain, can they be called outside `load` and so bypass its namespace and index-range checks, as quickstart P2's `d.key(0)` does, and are the fingerprints checked at import, as P2 expects, or at load, as data-model §2 and research R3 say? | Clarify: scripts take inputs only through `load(ns, kind, allow, count, ...)`, as feature 002's scripts use `protocol/seedsets.py`'s `load`: it checks the namespace against `allow` and the kind's fingerprint (for `state` at the given n and w) and returns the values i = 0…count − 1, so no index outside the declared range can be read. The derivation functions take the namespace, as their strings do (`key(ns, i)` and the others), and a test fails if code outside the module and its own tests calls one. Fingerprints are checked at load, as research R3 and data-model §2 say. Quickstart P2 reads `python -c "from protocol import devinputs_v3 as d; print(d.load('dev', 'key', allow=('dev',), count=1)[0].hex())"`, with "64 hex digits; fingerprint checked at load" | B: keep `key(i)` and the others public without the namespace, as P2 uses them: a script could read any index of `dev` with no check of its range. C: unchanged | data-model §2; research R3; quickstart P2; spec FR-040, FR-061 |
| REP-055 | Does the downloads manifest hold "one entry per file of these tables" (research R19), a file not yet fetched included, or "one entry per file fetched" (plan, Setup); and does data-model §7's `source` cover a value the owner provides with its source, which `validate_tool.py` accepts but which has neither a path in the repository nor a manifest entry? | Align: the manifest holds one entry per file of R19's tables from setup on, each with a `status`: `approved` (not yet fetched, with no SHA-256, date or path), `fetched` (with all three) or `not obtained` (with the reason); the plan's Setup reads so. A value the owner provides gets an entry of its own, `owner-provided`, with the owner's words, the source the owner names, the date and the value's SHA-256, and data-model §7's `source` cites that entry | B: one entry per file fetched, with owner-provided values in the validation record only: the manifest would not show which approved files were never fetched. C: unchanged | research R19; plan phase 1 ("Setup"); data-model §7; cli contract `validate_tool.py` |

## Re-run of 2026-11-01

The 25 items other than conflicts that the re-run of 2026-11-01 found in the text the answers
changed, in checklist order: 4 security, 2 design-rationale, 3 analysis, 5 protocol, 5 hardware and
6 reproducibility. They were answered on 2026-11-01, "all recommended", and applied, as were the
four conflicts above.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-041 | A refused variant, a wrong key length or a failed self-test at construction leaves no object, so neither failure-path row can apply. Do construction failures get their own row, with the order of the constructor's checks? | Add: the failure-path table gains "A failed construction: no object exists; any state the self-test used is zeroized; no copy of the key was stored", and the refused variant moves there from the first row. The constructor checks in a stated order, each refusal raising one exception: the variant (`VariantRefused`), then the key length (`ValueError`), then the self-test (`SelfTestError`); only then does it store the key. Each refusal is tested with an input that would also fail every later check, so the order shows | B: the key length first, then the variant: the same exceptions in another order. C: leave the order to the implementation: which exception a call wrong twice raises would go untested | model contract "Generator", "Failure paths", "Exceptions"; spec FR-058 |
| SEC-042 | Python cannot overwrite an immutable `bytes` key in place. What does "clears the key" mean, so that the model can meet it and a test can check it? | Clarify: the object stores its own copy of the key as a `bytearray`; `zeroize`, and every path that zeroizes, overwrites that copy with zeros in place and then drops it. The state's integers, which Python cannot overwrite, are dropped. The model contract states this as a discipline, as it does for constant time: it governs what the object keeps, not what Python's memory may still hold, and the caller's own copy of the key is outside the rule. A test keeps a reference to the buffer, calls `zeroize`, and checks that the buffer reads all zeros and that the object no longer refers to it | B: hold the key as an integer and drop it: nothing is overwritten, so "clears" would mean "drops". C: no statement about memory: "clears" stays untestable | model contract "Generator", "Module rules"; spec FR-025, FR-058 |
| SEC-043 | A `Generator` cannot tell a random nonce from a counter value. How is the per-key limit of 2³² random-nonce instantiations enforced? | Stricter: one instantiation counter per object, held at the smaller limit, R9's random-nonce limit (at most 2³²), whatever nonce the caller uses; the instantiate past it raises `LimitExceeded` and ends the object, as other per-key limits do. Across objects with one key, the same limit is a usage rule for the caller, stated with the other per-key limits. Direction: a limit falls, which R9's "Stricter means" allows | B: a nonce mode declared at construction, `counter` or `random`, with counter mode refusing a nonce not above the last: counter users keep a larger limit, checked only within one object. C: a usage rule for the caller only (looser: the object would not enforce the limit) | research R9 "Nonces"; spec FR-006, FR-007; model contract "Other limits", "Per-key limit"; data-model §10 |
| SEC-044 | FUT-SEC-5 is "met by specification for its specified part", yet that part includes diffusion through instantiation and research R4's first-order differences, which are measured, while met by specification means that nothing remains to measure. Which status holds? | Align: the FUT-SEC-5 row, FR-012 and SC-002 read "met by specification for key loading, and by evidence for diffusion through instantiation (FR-029) and research R4's first-order related-key and related-nonce differences; related-key and related-IV attacks beyond first order, weak-key classes and key-dependent effects open for feature 005 (FR-049)", and the claim register (data-model §15) records the same split. For L1, whose gate asks for FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-6 met by specification (security.md §15), the security-target review accepts the design document's section on key loading and cites the evidence records of the measured part, which exist by then, so nothing remains to measure | B: the specified part becomes key loading alone, with diffusion and the first-order differences reported as evidence beside the status. C: unchanged: the status and its definition disagree | spec "Security Policy traceability" row FUT-SEC-5, FR-012, SC-002; data-model §15 |
| RAT-037 | Are the constant rule's open values given (the polynomial's degree, the order of enumeration, the seed, the reading order), or the record that fixes them before the first score named, given that the design document stating them comes only after selection? | Settle: R15 fixes them now. Degree 64; the polynomials x⁶⁴ + c(x) with c(0) = 1, in ascending order of the integer whose bits are c's coefficients; the first primitive one not on the frozen refused list (RAT-038). Fibonacci form s(t + 64) = Σ cᵢ·s(t + i) over GF(2), with s(0)…s(63) the bits of ASCII `PhycoWeb`, most significant first (0x506879636F576562, 32 of 64 bits set), and output from s(64) on. Each table is read from the start of the output, w bits per word, most significant bit first, in the order call type (if the streams are distinct), round, food node, with RAT-036's skip. The values become `settled` entries of the change log before the first score; the design document restates them, and the script checks that the two agree | B: degree 32 with seed 1: a shorter register, but the words after a seed of weight 1 are sparse. C: values fixed per candidate only, in its log entry (looser: FR-023 fixes one rule before the first score) | research R15; spec FR-023; data-model §18; plan "Values this plan fixes" |
| RAT-039 | Must the design document state how the 2×2 torus differs from the larger sizes, where a node's opposite neighbours coincide, and how that bears on reading 2×2 evidence as evidence about the claimable sizes? | Add: the size profile states that at 2×2 a node's opposite neighbours coincide, so its four links join two distinct nodes, two channels from each (FR-018), and a translation by one node is its own inverse. The final report reads 2×2 evidence only for properties that do not depend on the neighbourhood, such as bijectivity and the tools' validation, and states 2×2's R_f, R_b, symmetries and invariants as facts about 2×2 alone, never as evidence about a claimable size | B: make 2×2 a permutation-only size: it conflicts with FR-018 and with security.md §20.1's "the keyed variant each size has". C: no statement: 2×2 results could be read as if 2×2 were a small 4×4 | spec FR-018, FR-032, FR-052; the design document's size profile |
| ANA-033 | Must the known results this feature writes itself (the small operation list with its hand-derived matrices, the map with collisions, the variant with a planted gap, research R5's hand-counted examples) exist before the code of the tool they validate? | Add: each is written with its SHA-256 and entered in the provenance log (data-model §14) before the tool it validates is drafted, so its entry's `seq` comes before the tool's first entry. The validation record cites both entries, and a test checks their order and that the hashes still match | B: an owner commit of the known results before the tool's first commit: a stronger record, but it waits on a commit at the right moment. C: no order rule: a tool could be validated against expectations derived from its own output | spec FR-039; research R5, R13; data-model §7, §14 |
| ANA-034 | Is "what must match" stated for the known results that are not published values (S12 to S15, the FUT-SEC-8 recovery, the structural tools' known results), so that `re-found` follows from a stated comparison? | Add: data-model §7 states, per known result, exact equality for every deterministic fact: S12's ranges and classes; S15's 16 steps and its bound 2¹²⁸ · (2³¹ + 1)⁴; S13(a)'s merged pair 16m + 15 and 16m + 16; S7's three rotation relations; each structural tool's counts and matrices, entry by entry; R5's counts; and FUT-SEC-8's three seeds and its vector check. A measured fraction, such as S13's 41% and 32% or S14's bit frequencies, matches when the tool's count lies in the binomial interval around the recorded fraction, for the tool's sample size, with at most 0.005 in each tail. Anything else is `not re-found` | B: exact equality everywhere: a measured fraction would almost never match. C: the analyst judges each comparison: `re-found` would rest on judgement | data-model §7; spec FR-039, SC-007; research R12, R13 |
| ANA-035 | Is "heavy run" defined by a rule, given that FR-045 and the cli contract give two different lists of examples and runs such as the exhaustive box tables at w = 8 appear in neither? | Add: a heavy run is any run that is of a kind research R7 budgets, uses more than one worker process or thread, or is projected to take, or last took, more than 10 minutes. Every heavy run takes the lock, never overlaps another, projects first and records `heavy_run` true. FR-045's and the cli contract's lists become examples of the rule, and a test checks that every budgeted kind and every script with a worker option takes the lock | B: one merged list of examples: the next new kind could be missed again. C: a time threshold alone: a short run with many workers could overlap another heavy run | spec FR-045; cli contract "Heavy runs", "Budget"; research R7; quickstart E3 |
| PRO-036 | Is the `settled` direction defined alike in FR-044 and in data-model §18 and research R16, whose "a value fixed before its first use" drops FR-044's never-looser condition? | Align: data-model §18 and research R16 take FR-044's full definition: `settled` fixes a case the protocol leaves open, only before any result it affects exists, with the owner's approval, and never looser than any reading of the existing text. A `readings` field lists the readings compared, so that each entry's claim to be no looser can be checked | B: align the wording only, with no `readings` field: the claim stays uncheckable. C: unchanged (looser: the log's definition drops the never-looser condition) | data-model §18; research R16; spec FR-044 |
| PRO-037 | Is it specified how the owner's approval is written into the draft freeze record, and what `validate` checks about it, so that step (3) of the freeze cannot alter a frozen hash unnoticed? | Add: `design_freeze.py approve --by NAME --wording TEXT`, run by the owner, changes `status` from `draft` to `approved`, adds `approved_by`, `approved_utc` and `approval_wording`, and changes nothing else. `create` stores `draft_sha256`, the SHA-256 of the record's canonical JSON without that field and the approval fields. `validate` recomputes it and every hash, checks the format rules, and exits 1 if anything else changed and 2 while `status` is `draft` | B: a separate `freeze/approval.json` citing the record's SHA-256: the record never changes, and step (3) reads "beside it". C: the approval typed into the record by hand: nothing would show that only those fields changed | data-model §13; cli contract `design_freeze.py`; spec FR-043; research R16 |
| PRO-038 | Which answered rows of checklist-choices.md become the protocol change log's first entries, and what does `last_record_id` hold while no evidence record exists? | Clarify: one entry for every answered row whose applied change altered the text of a pre-registered rule, whatever its tag: the 28 rows tagged Stricter or Settle (17 and 11), and the Add, Align and Clarify rows that changed such a rule, for example ANA-028's known result for the bias tool. Each entry cites its row ID and takes `stricter`, in the rule's stated direction, or `settled`. The list is fixed by ID before the log is created. `last_record_id` holds `none`, and `results_known` is empty | B: the 28 Stricter and Settle rows only: an Add row that tightened a rule would go unlogged. C: one summary entry for all the answers: no entry could be checked against its rule's old and new text | data-model §18; spec Clarifications; plan "Values this plan fixes" |
| PRO-039 | Is recovery from a stale `data/heavy-run.lock` or `data/log-append.lock` specified, when a crash or a reboot leaves one behind: who removes it, how the holder is shown gone, and how the removal is recorded? | Add: both locks hold the PID and that process's start time, the environment (`windows` or `wsl`), the host, the command and the time the lock was taken. A script that finds a lock exits 2 as now, naming the lock and whether its holder still runs, checked in the lock's environment. Only `scripts/clear_lock.py LOCK`, run by the owner or at the owner's request, removes one, and only when no process with that PID and start time runs; it writes a `lock-cleared` record under `$REP/locks/` with the lock's content. A heavy run that died holding the lock then gets the `inconclusive` record every started run writes (data-model §1) | B: the owner removes a lock by hand: nothing would record the removal. C: locks that expire after a stated time: a long heavy run could lose its lock while still running | cli contract "Heavy runs", "Hash-chained logs" and a new `clear_lock.py` section; research R16; data-model §1; spec FR-045 |
| PRO-040 | Where is the owner's go-ahead for a log repair recorded, and what does `repair_log.py --approval REF` name, so that the script can refuse a repair without one? | Add: a row "Log repair <n>" of the spec's Review & Approval table, naming the log, the `seq` of the break and the date, set to Approved by the owner and committed. `--approval` names that row; the script reads the spec at HEAD and exits 2 unless the row reads Approved and names the same log and position | B: an entry of the protocol change log: a repair is not a protocol change. C: free text: the script could not tell a go-ahead from any other string | cli contract `repair_log.py`; research R16; spec Review & Approval table |
| HW-038 | Is FR-025's latency "from the last key word accepted to the first output valid" consistent with the model contract's split, in which the key is loaded once and held and each `instantiate` takes only a nonce? | Align: FR-025 gives three latencies in cycles: from reset released to the self-test done and the state zeroized; key loading, from the last key word accepted to the key held, a call of its own whose cycle count joins FR-020's list; and instantiate, from the last nonce or personalization word accepted to the first output valid, the trees' latency included | B: add an instantiate figure and keep the key-word figure: one figure would still span two calls. C: unchanged: the figure describes an interface the model no longer has | spec FR-025, FR-020; model contract "Generator"; the design document |
| HW-039 | Is "an error path" among the hardware zeroization triggers defined, naming the conditions that count as an error after the state has changed, apart from a refused request that changes nothing? | Clarify: the design document lists every condition the RTL detects after the state has changed, each of which zeroizes, raises the error signal and needs a new key, or states that there is none, since with valid and ready a bad request is refused before anything changes. Reset and clock glitches stay deferred to feature 008, as the spec already says | B: a fixed list now: input arriving early or past its stated length, and replicated round or phase counters that disagree. C: drop "an error path" from the triggers (looser: a zeroization trigger removed) | spec FR-025 "Zeroization"; model contract "Failure paths"; the design document |
| HW-040 | Is recovery after a failed start-up self-test specified for the device, and is the failure visible on an output rather than only as refused calls? | Add: a failed self-test sets a `selftest_fail` output, latched until the next reset; every reset runs the self-test again, and a pass clears the latch, as a new `Generator` runs it again in the model. Until then every call is refused. The output is listed with the ports in the design document and tested by feature 008 | B: latched until power is removed: a transient fault would need a power cycle. C: automatic retries without a reset: a failing device could pass a later try unnoticed | spec FR-059, FR-025, FR-056; the design document |
| HW-041 | Is the carried rule "the board validates 4×4 before 8×8 and 16×16" reconciled with FUT-HW-2's "at 4×4 and at the largest grid that fits", so that feature 008 is never required to validate a size the XC7A100T cannot hold? | Align: feature 006's RTL simulation runs 4×4 before 8×8 and 16×16. Feature 008's board runs 4×4 first, then each larger claimable size that fits the XC7A100T, in order, the largest that fits last (FUT-HW-2). FR-026's LUT and flip-flop estimate predicts the fit and Vivado's post-route report decides it; a size that does not fit is listed in feature 008's spec with its reason and stays covered by simulation. This corrects the wording HW-035 carried into FR-036, FR-052, FR-056 and security.md §22 row 5a.5 | B: 4×4 and the largest size that fits only, as FUT-HW-2 says: 8×8 would go untested on the board whenever 16×16 fits. C: unchanged: feature 008 could be required to validate a size the device cannot hold | spec FR-036, FR-052, FR-056; security.md §22 row 5a.5, with a 2.12 history row |
| HW-042 | Is it specified how a stall (ready low) reaches every node and every replicated round and phase counter on the same cycle, given that the counters run in lockstep and no counter value crosses the grid? | Add: the core never stalls in mid-call. Output leaves through a buffer of one block (r bits), and the next call starts only after the consumer has taken the block, so a stall holds only the buffer. The start signal reaches the nodes and the replicated counters through a registered fan-out tree with one stated latency to every node, as reset does, so lockstep holds. The buffer's size and the tree's latency enter FR-020's cycle formulas and FR-025 | B: a clock enable in mid-call through a registered tree whose latency every node compensates: stalls at any cycle, at the cost of a second balanced tree. C: a global combinational enable: Principle III would need a justification the plan does not give | spec FR-025 "Flow control", FR-020; plan Complexity Tracking; the design document |
| REP-028 | Are the file kinds that may be committed under `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/` listed, given that its `text eol=lf` rule makes a binary file there text, and is research R18's statement on the archived `phyco-v2` copies corrected for the vector copies? | Add: files committed there are limited to `.json`, `.jsonl`, `.md`, `.csv`, `.txt` and `.gz`; raw counts and p-values are JSON or gzip-compressed JSON, never `.npy`, and a solver's log is `.txt`, since `*.log` is ignored. `protocol/evidence.py`, which writes every record, refuses any other suffix there. R18's sentence on `baseline/** -text` gains that the later `*.hex text eol=lf` line governs the two vector copies, as `git check-attr` showed on 2026-11-01, which is harmless while their sources stay LF, as they are | B: a `binary` rule for each binary kind that might appear (`.npy`, `.npz`, `.png`, `.pdf`, `.zip`): a kind not foreseen would still be committed as text. C: none: a binary file written there would be committed as text, and its hash would change | research R16, R18; data-model introduction and §1; spec FR-053 |
| REP-030 | Does the reproduction rule's list of excluded fields cover every field that legitimately differs between environments or analysts, such as `command`, `config`, `parallelism`, `analyst` and `spec_sha256` once the spec has changed, or should the compared fields be listed instead? | Clarify: the rule lists the compared fields, and every other field is reported only: `kind`, `model_id`, `variant_id`, `design_sha256`, each source file's entry in `code_sha256`, `inputs`, each output's `sha256` and `bytes` (not its path), `exit_status`, `assertions`, `status`, `classification`, and the fields of its kind. A different source hash makes it a new run, not a reproduction, and a binary's hash is reported only. The verdict is recomputed under the spec version the original cites (`spec-versions/<sha256>.md`), so a later spec change cannot alter it | B: extend the exclusion list with the fields above: the next new field would again be compared by default. C: as now: a record made on Windows could not be reproduced in WSL | data-model §1 "Reproduction"; spec FR-053; research R1, R16 |
| REP-031 | How does a reproduction compare the reported p-values, given that they come from scipy 1.15.3 on Windows and 1.18.1 in WSL, and that only the verdicts rest on exact integer critical counts? | Clarify: counts, critical counts and verdicts are compared byte for byte; p-values are compared within a relative tolerance of 10⁻⁶ and never decide a verdict. A `p_values` file is a reported output whose hash may differ between the two environments, and the reproduction lists any p-value outside the tolerance | B: p-values byte for byte within one environment only: a reproduction across the two could not compare them at all. C: drop p-values from the records: security.md §11.6 asks for them | data-model §1 "Reproduction", §5; research R4; plan "Technical Context" |
| REP-032 | Are the hashed bytes defined where a hash is not over a whole file's raw bytes: the chain's previous line (with or without its LF, and the digest's letter case) and research R16's `lf-normalized` content (CR LF only, or also a lone CR)? | Clarify: `prev_sha256` is the SHA-256 of the previous line's UTF-8 bytes as stored, its final LF included. Every digest is written as 64 lower-case hex digits, as the existing records already are; the format rules' upper case applies to vector and state words only. `lf-normalized` means each CR LF pair replaced by LF on the file's bytes, as Git normalizes, a lone CR kept, and never a file read in text mode, whose universal newlines also turn a lone CR into LF | B: the line without its final LF: a shell check that pipes `sed -n 3p LOG` into `sha256sum` would not reproduce it. C: as now: two correct implementations could write different chains | data-model introduction, §3, §14, §18; research R16; cli contract "Hash-chained logs" |
| REP-033 | Is the order in which the self-test data and the vectors are generated specified, given that the self-test's expected output comes from the vectors, a `Generator` runs the self-test in its constructor, and the model may import only `phycoweb.constants_v3`? Does `ResearchDuplex` run a self-test? | Add: three steps, each generator's `--check` passing in this order. (1) `gen_constants_v3.py` writes the constant tables. (2) `gen_vectors_v3.py` writes the vectors through `pi` and `ResearchDuplex`, which runs no self-test, as its contract then states, and also writes `phycoweb/selftest_v3.py`, the self-test's inputs and expected output, copied from the vectors. (3) `Generator` imports `phycoweb.selftest_v3`, which the module rules add to the allowed imports. Tests check that `Generator` reproduces the vectors and that `selftest_v3` equals them, and the freeze hashes `selftest_v3.py` with the vectors | B: write the self-test data into `constants_v3` in a second step: one module, but two generators writing it. C: the model reads the vector files at construction: the production model would depend on files outside its package at run time | model contract "Module rules", "Generator", "Research duplex"; cli contract "Generators of constants and vectors"; spec FR-059, FR-036; data-model §11, §13 |
| REP-034 | Is the byte format of a gzip-compressed record fixed, or are its hash and its reproduction defined over the uncompressed content, given that a gzip header holds a modification time and may hold a file name? | Add: every `.gz` file is written by `gzip.compress(data, compresslevel=9, mtime=0)`, so its header holds no file name and a zero time; on 2026-11-01's probe the two environments then wrote identical bytes.
