Its raw hash is over the compressed bytes, and its record also holds the SHA-256 of the uncompressed content, which a reproduction compares, since another zlib may compress differently. A test checks the header of every committed `.gz` file | B: hashes over the uncompressed content only: no recorded hash would match the committed file. C: as now: the same record compressed twice could differ in its header | research R16; data-model introduction and §1; spec FR-053 |

## Security

[checklists/security.md](./checklists/security.md): 96 items. SEC-010, SEC-020, SEC-048, SEC-052,
SEC-061, SEC-066, SEC-084 and SEC-096 are under Conflicts, SEC-041 to SEC-044 under "Re-run of
2026-11-01", SEC-045 to SEC-047 and SEC-049 to SEC-051 under "Second re-run of 2026-11-01", SEC-053
to SEC-060, SEC-062 and SEC-063 under "Third re-run of 2026-11-01", SEC-064, SEC-065 and SEC-067 to
SEC-073 under "Fourth re-run of 2026-11-02", SEC-074 under "Fifth re-run of 2026-11-02", SEC-075 to
SEC-077 under "Sixth re-run of 2026-11-02", SEC-078 to SEC-083 under "Seventh re-run of 2026-11-02",
SEC-085 to SEC-088 under "Eighth re-run of 2026-11-02", SEC-089 to SEC-092 under "Ninth re-run of
2026-11-02" and SEC-093 to SEC-095 under "Tenth re-run of 2026-11-03"; the other 38 follow.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| SEC-001 | Does FR-006's 256-bit key apply to the production parameter sets only, with each reduced variant's bound kept apart from a claimable strength? | Clarify: FR-006 applies to the production parameter sets (claimable sizes, w = 32, c = 256). A keyed reduced variant takes a c-bit key and a c/2-bit nonce (R14), and its size-profile row states s = c/2 as a bound on that variant only, with status `research` and "no claim"; FR-032's "its bound" names that row | B: one 256-bit key everywhere, absorbed over several blocks at small sizes (R14's rejected alternative: the construction changes, so evidence transfers less). C: rely on R14 and leave FR-006 general: spec and plan keep reading differently | spec FR-006, FR-032 |
| SEC-002 | Does the key-source assumption say what the source must provide, and that it must be an established cryptographic generator? | Clarify: FR-006's assumption reads: the key is 256 bits with full entropy, uniform and secret, from an established cryptographic random-number generator, never from a PhycoWeb-PRNG model. The generator cannot check this, and a key with less entropy caps the strength at that entropy (FR-010). The security target repeats it among the caller's obligations | B: also name example sources (an SP 800-90A DRBG, the operating system's generator): helpful, but close to conformance language (SEC-029). C: leave the source unstated: FR-006 asks for it | spec FR-006, Assumptions; design document security target |
| SEC-003 | Are FUT-SEC-3's "defined uniqueness rules" stated beyond "at most once per key", with the collision probability of random nonces? | Add (stricter for R9's limits): two ways are allowed. (1) A counter per key, never repeated or wrapped, kept by the caller across objects and devices. (2) Random 128-bit nonces, whose collision probability, at most q²/2¹²⁹ for q instantiations, becomes a term of R9's bound; random nonces get their own per-key instantiation limit, at most 2³², so the term stays within ε = 2⁻⁶⁴ | B: counters only: simplest to state, but a caller with several devices cannot always keep one counter. C: random nonces only: the collision term applies at every limit | spec FR-006, FR-007; research R9; data-model §10 `limits.json`; design document security target |
| SEC-004 | Does a `Generator` detect a nonce repeated within one object, or is nonce reuse only stated behaviour in every case? | Clarify: never detected, in any case, as the model contract already says. The edge case's "not an error the generator can detect across instantiations" becomes "not detected, within one object or across objects". No bounded record covers the per-key instantiation limit, other objects or other devices, and the RTL keeps no nonce history, so a partial check would make the model differ from the hardware and suggest a protection that does not exist. A test shows the stated behaviour (FR-058) | B: the object keeps the nonces it has used and raises on a repeat: catches one mistake inside one object, costs memory up to the instantiation limit, and has no hardware counterpart. C: the object draws its nonces from an internal counter: no reuse within an object, but the caller loses the nonce input FR-013 defines | spec Edge Cases "Nonce reuse", FR-058 |
| SEC-005 | Does FR-009's list of public parts include the nonce, the personalization string and reseed inputs? | Clarify: FR-009's list reads "constants, domain-separation bits, counters, the nonce, the personalization string, reseed inputs and every output". A reseed input is never counted as secret state, even from a secret source, since FR-016 treats it as chosen by the attacker | B: count a reseed input from a validated SP 800-90B source as secret: FR-016 allows such a source, but the project has none, so it would count something unshown. C: keep the list open-ended: the size profile could count a public input | spec FR-009; data-model §10 `reachable_secret_state_bits` |
| SEC-006 | Is it specified how the key and nonce enter the state, and whether later inputs are absorbed over the rate only, with each choice a precondition of the theorem? | Add: FR-013 states that key and nonce are placed in the initial state at stated positions, the rest of it set to stated constants, as the full-state keyed duplex initializes (R10). Later inputs (personalization string, reseed input) are absorbed over the rate only (FR-018), and the mode chapter must show that this is a special case of the theorem's absorption. Both are listed among the preconditions, "unverified" until the paper is read; if the paper's form differs, the design takes it, or the feature stops (R10) | B: absorb key and nonce as rate blocks into a constant state: the key passes through the outer part, under another theorem's preconditions. C: absorb later inputs over the full state, as the theorem permits: more input per call, but the absorption trees would reach every lane | spec FR-013, FR-018; research R10; design document mode chapter |
| SEC-007 | Is FR-014's injectivity given a form and an acceptance criterion, including call types that draw on distinct constant streams? | Add: both. (1) A proof in the design document that the encoding of every (call type, input) pair into absorbed blocks is injective, padding rule and domain bits stated. (2) A committed check over every input length from 0 to each input's maximum and every call type, showing distinct (call type, length) pairs give distinct block layouts, and a test that distinct development inputs give distinct block sequences. Acceptance: 0 collisions, and the proof accepted at the owner design review. Where call types use distinct constant streams, the proof says whether the streams or the domain bits carry the separation | B: the proof alone: no mechanical check of the lengths. C: the check alone: no check can cover every value | spec FR-014; design document mode chapter |
| SEC-008 | Is "secret state reachable from the key" defined precisely enough to verify per size: which bits, at which moment, proven or counted? | Clarify: the bits of the state that no public input sets and no output reveals, at every call boundary (after instantiate, after each generate with its forget, after each reseed): the capacity part, c bits, for the duplex as designed. The value is counted from the specified layout (which lanes form the capacity part, and that no call writes or outputs them), recorded as "by specification", not measured | B: the whole state before the first output (b bits): the rate part is output after each call, so it overstates what stays secret. C: a count from the dependency matrices: measures dependence, not secrecy | spec FR-009, FR-010; data-model §10; design document security target |
| SEC-009 | Must the reachable secret state stay at least 2s after every forget and reseed call, as well as after instantiate? | Add: FR-009 requires the value at every call boundary of SEC-008, and the size profile records it per boundary; a size below 2s at any boundary fails FR-010 | B: after instantiate only, as written: a forget or reseed that overwrote part of the capacity would go unnoticed. C: one general argument for the mode: does not show that the stated layout meets it | spec FR-009, FR-013, FR-016; data-model §10 |
| SEC-011 | Does every supported size have at least one keyed variant, so that FUT-GRID-2's "key and IV loading defined for every supported size" holds? | Add: FR-018's list of supported sizes names each size's keyed variants: the production variant at every claimable size, and at 2×2 at least one keyed reduced variant, such as `n2-w16-R8-c64` with an 8-byte key and a 4-byte nonce (quickstart M6); keyless variants are listed as permutation-only. A supported 3×3 gets one as well | B: allow sizes with keyless variants only and record FUT-GRID-2's clause as open for them. C: drop such sizes from the supported list | spec FR-018, Security Policy traceability (FUT-GRID-2); research R14; data-model §10 |
| SEC-012 | Does FR-007 cover every online quantity the theorem's bound counts, or only output volume and u? | Align: FR-007 adds "and every other online quantity the theorem's bound counts (calls of Π, instantiations per key, reseeds per instantiation), each with a limit and a counter", as R9 and the model contract already require; SC-010 covers a request beyond any limit | B: keep FR-007 to output and u, and leave the rest to R9: the spec would not require limits its bound depends on. C: none | spec FR-007, SC-010 |
| SEC-013 | Are the output limits stated in one unit, with the conversion between units given? | Clarify: the theorem's unit, calls of Π, is primary, and each limit is also given in bits at every claimable size. A request of n bits takes ⌈n/r⌉ output calls plus one forget call, and instantiate's calls are stated; `limits.json` lists both units | B: bits only: simple for the caller, but every check against the theorem then converts. C: per-size tables without a rule | spec FR-007; research R9; data-model §10 |
| SEC-014 | Does the security target state that u and the per-key limits are usage rules the caller keeps across objects, processes and devices? | Align: FR-007 adds the sentence R9 and the model contract carry: "Across objects, processes and devices, the per-key limits and u are usage rules that the caller keeps; the model enforces them only within one object." The security target lists them with the caller's other obligations (key source, nonce uniqueness) | B: leave it to R9 and the contract: the spec's target would read as if the generator enforced them. C: none | spec FR-007; design document security target |
| SEC-015 | Are the bounds FR-010 only requires to be "stated" given acceptance criteria that could fail? | Add: each gets a check in `parameters.json` that can fail: output volume, every limit at most what the theorem allows at ε (R9's doubling check); quantum search, k/2 ≥ s, which holds with equality at k = 256 and s = 128; nonce reuse, the stated behaviour of SEC-004 with its test | B: "stated" only, read by a reviewer: nothing could fail. C: state the quantum bound without a check | spec FR-010, SC-003; data-model §10 |
| SEC-016 | Is the cost unit of "costs less than 2¹²⁸" fixed, and used the same way in FR-004, FR-005 and the round rule's R_dist? | Clarify: one unit, evaluations of Π or Π⁻¹ (R9's N), with every data block observed counted as at least one evaluation. FR-004's structural distinguisher, FR-005's generic attacks and R_dist all use it, and an attack's data must also stay within the output limits | B: time on stated hardware: not comparable with published attacks. C: a unit per statement: two statements could use different units | spec FR-004, FR-005, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule" |
| SEC-017 | Is backtracking resistance defined with the moment of compromise and the exposure it allows, in the security target? | Add: FR-004 defines it. A state compromised between calls reveals nothing about output before the last forget call; one compromised within a request reveals, through Π⁻¹, that request's earlier blocks back to the last forget call, at most the per-request cap of 2¹⁹ bits. For a first request that walk could reach instantiate's state and the key, so instantiate also ends with a forget call, in the form the theorem covers (R10; HW-020) | B: between calls only, as security.md §3 phrases it: the in-request exposure stays unstated. C: also lower the per-request cap below 2¹⁹ bits: less exposure, more forget calls (allowed, stricter) | spec FR-004, FR-013; research R9, R10; design document security target, mode chapter |
| SEC-018 | Is "reseeding MUST NOT weaken any property sought" made verifiable? | Add: FR-016 requires the mode chapter to map reseed to the theorem's absorption call with attacker-chosen input and to list that as a precondition, "unverified" until the paper is read (R10); a reseed never resets a counter (model contract) | B: a statement without a precondition: not checkable. C: drop reseed, which FR-013 makes optional: nothing to verify and one call fewer, but no way to add fresh input later | spec FR-016, FR-013; research R10; design document mode chapter |
| SEC-019 | Is "met by specification" defined: which artefact states what, who accepts it, and how it differs from "met by evidence"? | Add: a definitions line in the traceability section. "Met by specification": a cited section of the design document (version and section) states how the requirement is met, nothing remains to measure, and the owner has accepted that section, at the security-target review that records L1 for FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-6 and FUT-SEC-9 (FR-012) or else at the owner design review. "Met by evidence": a record of FR-061 shows it, computed by code (FR-065). The Evidence column cites the section or the record | B: met when the spec contains the requirement: "documents exist" is what FR-065 forbids as a pass. C: evidence only: FUT-SEC-9's named route has nothing to measure | spec Security Policy traceability, SC-002, FR-012 |
| SEC-021 | Is FUT-SEC-1's time–memory trade-off clause traced to the generic bounds that answer it, with the attack named? | Add: the FUT-SEC-1 row cites FR-010's "key plus nonce at least 2s" and "reachable secret state at least 2s" as the answer to time–memory–data trade-offs on the key and on the state, the attack security.md applies to `phyco-v2`'s seed in S3 and §5 | B: leave it implicit in FR-010. C: none | spec Security Policy traceability (FUT-SEC-1); design document security target |
| SEC-022 | Is FUT-SEC-5 split into what this feature delivers and what feature 005 delivers, with weak-key classes assigned and the first-order test's limits stated? | Add: the FUT-SEC-5 row splits it. Here: key loading specified, diffusion through instantiation (FR-029), and R4's first-order related-key and related-nonce differences, reported as "a single-difference avalanche measurement, not a related-key analysis". Feature 005: related-key and related-IV differential and linear attacks beyond first order, weak-key classes and key-dependent effects. FR-058's boundary keys are tests of the model, not a weak-key analysis | B: one "open for feature 005" for the whole analysis: R4's results would have no named place. C: count R4's test as the related-key analysis (not allowed: it overstates the test) | spec Security Policy traceability (FUT-SEC-5), FR-049; final report open items |
| SEC-023 | Does FUT-SEC-9 count as met by specification while D8's functionality class is unconfirmed, and are the open item's owner and closing condition stated? | Clarify: FUT-SEC-9 asks for a named route, so naming D8's route meets it by specification. The class within AIS 20/31 version 3.0 stays an open item, "class unconfirmed" beside the route, closed when the approved download (R19) is read and the class confirmed or changed in the design document, at the security-target review | B: FUT-SEC-9 unmet until the class is confirmed: L1 would wait on a detail the route does not need. C: none | spec FR-011, Assumptions "AIS 20/31", Security Policy traceability (FUT-SEC-9) |
| SEC-024 | Is the line between the allowed "proven bound" and the forbidden "proven secure" drawn precisely enough for the claim-register test to decide each use? | Clarify: FR-050 adds that "proven" may appear only with its object named in the same sentence or table row (a trail bound, a theorem's bound on an advantage) and its record cited; "proven" or "provably" never takes the design, the generator or a security property as its object. The test treats every other use as a finding | B: forbid "proven" altogether and write "bound" with its method: simplest to test, but it drops security.md §15's vocabulary. C: leave it to review: the test could not decide | spec FR-050, FR-069 |
| SEC-025 | How does the claim-register test recognise a target that is not labelled as one, such as "128-bit security"? | Add: the test flags any strength (a number of bits beside "security", "strength" or "secure", or a cost such as 2¹²⁸ beside "security") unless the same sentence or row carries "target" or quotes a rule; a flagged line passes only through an allow-list entry in the register, each with its reason | B: a fixed list of phrases: misses new wordings. C: manual review only | spec FR-069, SC-018; data-model §15 (the allow-list) |
| SEC-026 | Do the requirements forbid presenting frequency, avalanche or battery results as evidence of security, and wording that implies prediction resistance? | Add: FR-050 adds that frequency, avalanche and battery results are reported only as inputs to R_dist or as statistics, never as evidence of security or of mixing beyond what they measured, and that no text implies prediction resistance (D7). The claim test gets both as forbidden patterns | B: rely on the disclaimer's last sentence: no test of individual wording. C: none | spec FR-050, FR-069 |
| SEC-027 | Is FR-068's design-form disclaimer stated to be the exact text that FR-050 calls "the disclaimer at the top of this spec"? | Clarify: the three sentences now quoted inside the spec's "Update input" become their own block directly under the spec's title, as in research.md and the other documents; FR-050 and FR-068 both name it "the design-form disclaimer", and `DESIGN_DISCLAIMER` holds exactly that text. The claim test compares every document's copy with the constant | B: leave the text inside the quoted update input and cite its lines: correct, but a reader may not recognise a quotation as the disclaimer. C: none | spec title block, FR-050, FR-068; research R18 |
| SEC-028 | Does the claim-register test cover code comments and docstrings, which SC-015 names? | Align: FR-069's scope adds the comments and docstrings of every file this feature adds or changes (`phycoweb/`, `protocol/`, `scripts/`, `research/004/`, `tests/`, and `fast/seed_search.c`), as SC-015 names them | B: narrow SC-015 to documents (looser: it narrows a success criterion). C: none | spec FR-069, SC-015 |
| SEC-029 | Is it stated how values borrowed from standards are cited without implying conformance to SP 800-90A or SP 800-90B? | Add: FR-011 adds that a value from a standard is cited as "the value SP 800-90A uses for its largest request", never as compliance, and the design document states once that `phyco-v3` conforms to no standard; the claim test flags "compliant", "conforms" and "approved" near a standard's name outside that statement | B: avoid borrowed values: the request cap would need a reason of its own. C: none | spec FR-011, FR-069; design document security target |
| SEC-030 | Is constant time by construction stated as a property of the design's sequence of operations, not of the Python model or the C tools? | Clarify: FR-020 adds that constant time by construction is a property of the design's sequence of operations and of a hardware implementation of it; the model and the C tools are branch-free on data where stated, but their timing is not claimed, since Python integers are not constant time (HW-012) | B: also claim it for the C tools: they are analysis tools, and feature 006 writes the C implementation. C: none | spec FR-020; model contract "Module rules" |
| SEC-031 | How does the claim register record that L2 is started but not reached, without implying a level above L1? | Add: `claim_level` keeps the highest level reached (L0 or L1); a separate field `in_progress` names L2 with its start date and "not reached"; the claim test checks that no document states a level above `claim_level` | B: a level value such as "L2 (started)": reads as a level reached. C: prose only | data-model §15, §17; spec FR-012, FR-069 |
| SEC-032 | Is each claim level tied to a named gate in the Review & Approval table, and what happens to L1 when a theorem paper cannot be read? | Add: the Review & Approval table gains a row "L1 recorded (FUT-SEC-8 and the security-target chapter reviewed; FR-012)". L0 needs no row, and the start of L2 is recorded with the first counted trail-bound record. While any theorem the mode chapter uses is "unverified" (R10), the L1 row reads "blocked" and names the missing paper | B: record L1 at the owner design review: later than FR-012 needs. C: record L1 with "unverified" theorems (looser than FR-012 and R10) | spec Review & Approval, FR-012; data-model §17 |
| SEC-033 | Is "instantiation (key-loading) code" defined clearly enough to decide whether work done before the FUT-SEC-8 review crosses that gate? | Clarify: FR-001 defines it as code that loads a key into a state of `phyco-v3` or of any candidate: the mode's instantiate in the model, the research duplex, and any script that runs instantiate, such as R4's instantiation states. Not included: the development-input loader (it derives keys but loads none), keyless operation lists of Π, and the design document's text. The cli contract's guard covers candidate states too | B: only `phycoweb/model_v3.py`'s instantiate: a candidate's research duplex could load keys before the review. C: none | spec FR-001, FR-003; cli contract "Guards" (order of US1); `tests/test_protocol_004.py` |
| SEC-034 | Is the external design review distinguished from an evaluation "intended to support a claim", which the Security Policy defers? | Clarify: the spec's "External review" paragraph adds that the review asks for comments on a design that makes no claim, is not an evaluation intended to support one, and is never cited as one; the review package (FR-063) carries the disclaimer and says so | B: defer the external review until FUT-SEC-1 to FUT-SEC-9 are met with evidence: the strictest reading of the Security Policy, and D9 changes. C: none | spec Context "External review", FR-043, FR-063 |
| SEC-035 | Does the spec state that no claim, evaluation or certification may follow from this feature alone, and which features close each gap? | Add: FR-052's final report states that, with FUT-SEC-7 (feature 007) and FUT-SEC-5's analysis (feature 005) open and L2 to L5 not reached (features 005 to 009), nothing of that kind may follow from this feature alone, and lists each gap with its feature, as security.md §22 rows 5a.2 to 5a.6 name them | B: leave it to the disclaimer: the gaps would have no named owner. C: none | spec FR-052, FR-049 |
| SEC-036 | Which security-target parameters fall under FR-044's stricter-only rule, as ε and the request cap do? | Stricter: FR-044 and the plan's table list them, each with its direction: s, the key and nonce lengths and c may only rise; ε may only fall and N only rise (as now); u, every output limit and the per-request cap may only fall | B: ε, N and the cap only, as now: s, c and the lengths could still move. C: none | spec FR-044; plan "Values this plan fixes" |
| SEC-037 | Is the new-model-ID rule stated at every point where the design could change after evidence exists: after the freeze, and when feature 005 raises R_dist? | Add: FR-028 adds that after the freeze any change to a frozen file, a raise of R by feature 005, a new claimable size or a correction of a design-document and model disagreement (PRO-032) is a new model ID with its own vectors and freeze; before the freeze a change is a new design-document version and a log entry | B: as written: the correction case stays open. C: none | spec FR-028, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule", Edge Cases |
| SEC-038 | Are zeroization requirements defined for every path that leaves key-dependent state behind, not only for the zeroize call? | Add: per path, each tested (FR-058). A refused request changes nothing and the object stays usable (as now). A failed self-test zeroizes and leaves the object unusable (as now). An exception after the state has changed zeroizes the whole state and ends the object, so no half-updated state survives. A call refused at a per-key limit zeroizes and ends the object, since nothing more may be done with that key. A key mismatch changes nothing, and with HW-010 A it no longer exists | B: zeroize only on `zeroize()` and a failed self-test, as now: a state could outlive an exception. C: zeroize on every refusal: an oversized request, a caller's slip, would end the object | model contract "Generator", "Exceptions"; spec FR-025, FR-058 |
| SEC-039 | Are FR-025's "no read-back path" and FR-033's refusal of reduced variants stated for the model's interfaces, naming the research interfaces? | Add: FR-025 and FR-033 add that `Generator` exposes no state, key or counter through any public method or attribute and refuses reduced variants, while `pi`, `pi_inv`, `pi_trace` and `ResearchDuplex`, named research-only in the docstring, are the only places that show state or run reduced variants. Python cannot make memory unreadable, so the rule concerns the interface | B: leave it to the model contract: the spec would not require it. C: none | spec FR-025, FR-033; model contract "Generator" |
| SEC-040 | Does FR-008 name a minimum set of assets for a keyed design, or leave the whole list to the design document? | Add: FR-008 names at least the key; the capacity part at every moment; the whole state within a request, whose earlier blocks Π⁻¹ recovers until the forget call; every output block delivered to the caller; the integrity of the limit counters; and the caller's nonce-uniqueness record. The design document may add more | B: the design document's list alone: a review could not tell an omission. C: none | spec FR-008; design document threat model |

## Design rationale

[checklists/design-rationale.md](./checklists/design-rationale.md): 90 items. RAT-033, RAT-036,
RAT-038, RAT-040, RAT-044, RAT-045, RAT-048, RAT-050 to RAT-052, RAT-054, RAT-062, RAT-063, RAT-068,
RAT-071, RAT-080, RAT-085 and RAT-086 are under Conflicts, RAT-037 and RAT-039 under "Re-run of
2026-11-01", RAT-041 to RAT-043, RAT-046 and RAT-047 under "Second re-run of 2026-11-01", RAT-049,
RAT-053 and RAT-055 to RAT-059 under "Third re-run of 2026-11-01", RAT-060, RAT-061 and RAT-064 to
RAT-066 under "Fourth re-run of 2026-11-02", RAT-067 under "Fifth re-run of 2026-11-02", RAT-069 and
RAT-070 under "Sixth re-run of 2026-11-02", RAT-072 to RAT-077 under "Seventh re-run of 2026-11-02",
RAT-078, RAT-079, RAT-081 and RAT-082 under "Eighth re-run of 2026-11-02", RAT-083 and RAT-084 under
"Ninth re-run of 2026-11-02" and RAT-087 to RAT-090 under "Tenth re-run of 2026-11-03"; the other 34
follow.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| RAT-001 | Is the set of components whose role the rationale must justify enumerated, or left to the design document? | Add: FR-034 lists them: the two lanes per node, the coupling function, the vein accumulator, the colour schedule, the local linear layer between boxes, the food nodes and their placement, rotation amounts, round constants, the padding rule, domain separators, forget, the absorption and output trees, and every component the chosen candidate adds. Each gets its role and its reason | B: leave the list to the design document: an omitted component could not be noticed. C: security.md §8's items only | spec FR-034 |
| RAT-002 | Does the spec require the converse of FR-034's walk: every component tied to an attack class, a bound or a functional need? | Add: every component of RAT-001 names at least one attack class it resists, a bound it supports or a functional need (bijectivity, locality, hardware cost). One with none is labelled "kept for the mapping only", with its cost stated, or removed | B: the walk through attack classes only, as now: a decorative component could pass. C: remove every component without such a role, decided before the evidence shows its cost | spec FR-034, FR-030 |
| RAT-003 | Must a minimum set of attack classes name a resisting design feature in this feature, or may any class be marked open for feature 005? | Add: a named resisting feature, not "open", for the classes this feature's evidence addresses: differential and linear (trail bounds), slide (round constants), invariant subspace (invariant checks, constants), rotational and rotational-XOR (symmetry checks, R4's rotational pairs), the inverse direction (R_b, Π⁻¹ in the bias rule) and the mode's generic attacks (the theorem). Other classes (algebraic, cube, integral, guess-and-determine and the rest) may be open for feature 005, each with its reason | B: any class may be open: the rationale could leave everything to feature 005. C: every class needs a resisting feature: forces arguments the evidence cannot yet support | spec FR-034 |
| RAT-004 | Are the colour-schedule rules stated in the spec, so that the bijectivity argument rests on a stated rule? | Add: FR-019 adds R11's rules: within one phase no update reads a lane that another update of that phase writes, every round runs every phase in a fixed order, and an odd n uses a three-colour schedule from the same generics or is unsupported with the reason | B: leave them in R11: the argument would rest on a plan detail. C: none | spec FR-019, Edge Cases "Odd grid sizes" |
| RAT-005 | Is FR-021's "keep it biased" tied to a measurable criterion? | Clarify: a lane is biased if any of its bits fires under the bias rule at a round count measured, and confined if an update maps it into a subrange. The rationale's full-range argument is that every update `dst ← dst ⊙ F(srcs)` is a bijection of dst for fixed srcs (R11), so no lane loses values; the bias rule checks the rest | B: S14's measure (the share of values in a range): specific to `phyco-v2`'s veins. C: none | spec FR-021 |
| RAT-006 | Must the design document answer security.md §8's eight network questions for the duplex as it is, including questions 4 and 6? | Clarify: FR-034 adds that each question is answered for the duplex as designed, and a question that does not fit gets the reason: for question 6, the output is the rate lanes themselves; for question 4, the cycle structure of Π takes the place of absorbing states, covered by the generic bound (research choice R13.2) | B: answer only the questions that fit: a skipped question looks like an omission. C: none | spec FR-034 |
| RAT-007 | Is "exhaustive where the width allows" given a threshold, and is the method stated for the 64-bit ARX-box at w = 32? | Clarify: FR-022 sets the threshold at box widths up to 16 bits (w ≤ 8), where full difference and linear tables are computed. Above that, at w = 16 and 32, the box's trail bounds come from the validated ARX search with the exact models of addition (R12), within the 8 h per instance of R7, and the bound for Π follows by counting active boxes | B: a time threshold (exhaustive when it fits 8 h): depends on the engine of the day. C: none | spec FR-022, FR-038; research R12 |
| RAT-008 | Are the properties of the local linear layer between ARX-boxes that the active-box count relies on required to be stated and justified? | Add: the rationale states the layer's differential and linear branch numbers, or the rule by which a difference must activate boxes in later rounds, and derives the active-box count from it; the validated active-box counting of R12 checks it | B: the active-box count alone: it would have no stated reason. C: none | spec FR-022; research R6, R12 |
| RAT-009 | Does the spec require an algebraic-degree argument for the Boolean family, or do low-degree attacks enter only through R_dist? | Stricter (a source of R_dist added): for every candidate, essential for the Boolean family, the rationale gives upper bounds on the algebraic degree of r rounds of Π and Π⁻¹ and the round counts below which a degree-based distinguisher (zero-sum, cube) costs less than 2¹²⁸ by those bounds. Such a distinguisher is a distinguisher of the round rule and raises R_dist like any other | B: leave degree to measured evidence: a low-degree attack would count only if a test happened to find it. C: leave algebraic attacks to feature 005: a weakness found then can only lead to a new model ID | spec FR-034, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule" (R_dist's sources); research R13 |
| RAT-010 | Must the rationale state the alternatives considered and the D4 result with its inputs, not only the design chosen? | Add: FR-034 requires a chapter on alternatives: the other family's representative and its score, every candidate not selected with its log entries and reason, the D4 record and the selection record, cited by record ID | B: the chosen design only: User Story 5's review could not see what was rejected. C: none | spec FR-034, FR-041 |
| RAT-011 | Is it specified what "reproduces no published primitive's constants" is checked against, so that the check can fail? | Add: R15's script holds a committed list of the polynomials and constant sets it refuses, each with its source: at least those of the reference designs of R12 and of every design the rationale cites. The check fails if the chosen polynomial is listed, or the constant table contains a listed sequence, also under word rotation or complement | B: search published lists online each time: a download per source, and not repeatable. C: none | spec re-decided feature-002 FR-007; research R15 |
| RAT-012 | Is the moment the constant rule is fixed stated, and is every remaining freedom in it, such as the seed, bound to a rule? | Stricter: the rule (polynomial, seed, reading order, distinct streams per call type or not) is fixed by one stated rule before the first candidate is scored, and each candidate's instance of it before its first tool run, recorded in the log's `parameters`. The polynomial is the first primitive polynomial of the stated degree, in a stated order, that is not on RAT-011's list, and the seed is a fixed public value | B: fixed at the freeze: constants could be tuned after results. C: none | spec FR-023, FR-044; research R15; data-model §3 |
| RAT-013 | Is the rotation-amount search fixed before it runs and its full ranking recorded? | Stricter: before the search runs, the candidate's log entry records its space of amounts, ranking measure, rounds, width and tie order; the full ranking is a committed record, and the amounts chosen are its first entry | B: record the chosen amounts only: a choice made after the fact could not be seen. C: none | spec FR-024; research R15; data-model §3 |
| RAT-014 | At which width are the rotation amounts chosen, and must the scaling rule keep the property they were chosen for? | Add: chosen at the D4 width (16, or else 8; R6), where both bounds are computed, with the scaling rule to every other width stated before the search. R_bound at w = 32 is computed with the scaled amounts, as it must be anyway, and the rationale reports whether the ranking held after scaling, without choosing again | B: choose at w = 32: closer to production, but the search may not fit 8 h per instance. C: choose per width: the variants would no longer be one design (ANA-029) | spec FR-024, FR-032; research R6, R14, R15 |
| RAT-015 | Is "differ in every round and at every food node" defined: distinct values only, or also not rotations or complements of one another? | Stricter: within one call of Π no two constants are equal, equal up to a word rotation by k = 1…w − 1, complements, or rotations of complements; the constant script checks this beside R13's symmetry checks | B: distinct values only, as written: rotational attacks could use related constants. C: none | spec FR-023, SC-006; research R13, R15 |
| RAT-016 | Are the number of food nodes and their placement rule required per size, with the reason for them? | Add: the design document states per supported size the number of food nodes, their positions by the rule, and the reason, for example the number of rounds until every lane depends on a constant injected in round 1, from R13's dependency propagation | B: the rule only, as written. C: a constant at every node: local by construction, at the cost of a ROM per node | spec FR-018, FR-023; research R13 |
| RAT-017 | Are padding constants and domain separators among the constants whose rule and reason the rationale must state? | Clarify: FR-034 covers every constant of Π and the mode: round constants, rotation amounts, padding constants and domain separators, the last two in the mode chapter; FR-054 already gives each domain separator a constructed-state vector | B: round constants and rotations only: the mode's constants would have no stated reason. C: none | spec FR-014, FR-023, FR-034 |
| RAT-018 | Is "the resulting margin" defined, and is Δ = 2's sufficiency argued, given that FR-038's bounds are single-trail bounds? | Add: the margin per claimable size is three numbers, R − R_dist, R − R_bound and R / max(R_f, R_b), with the term that set R. The rationale argues Δ = 2 against the single-trail limits of FR-038 as a pre-registered allowance, not a proof, and states the gap from clustering and hulls as a residual risk for feature 005 (FR-062) | B: one number, R − R_dist: hides which term set R. C: none | spec FR-034, FR-062 |
| RAT-019 | Is "round" defined in the spec, so that R, R_f, R_b, R_bound and r/R count one unit? | Clarify: a round is one pass over every colour phase in order, with that round's constants (R11); R, R_dist, R_bound, R_f, R_b and the r/R of FR-026 count rounds, and a phase is a pipeline stage (HW-003) | B: a round is one phase: every count and the D4 score per round change meaning. C: none | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule", FR-026, FR-029 |
| RAT-020 | Is it stated that R may differ between claimable sizes, and must the rationale explain its growth with n? | Clarify: FR-028 states that R is computed per claimable size and may differ, and the rationale explains its growth with n by the grid's diameter and the R_f and R_b found per size | B: one R for every claimable size, the largest of the three: stricter and simpler to state, but slower at 4×4. C: none | spec FR-028, FR-034 |
| RAT-021 | Is the round count of each research variant required to be stated with its reason? | Add: FR-032 requires each research variant's R with its reason: the round rule's value where its inputs exist at that variant, or a reduced count chosen for a stated test and marked as reduced; the variant ID carries R | B: leave research round counts free: a reduced-round result could be read as full-round. C: none | spec FR-032; data-model §10 |
| RAT-022 | Is the warm-up justified from the dependency evidence, so that full dependence before the first output is shown and not assumed? | Add: FR-029 adds that the calls of Π and rounds between key loading and the first output are stated, and that the dependency propagation through instantiate as a whole shows every state bit depending on every key and nonce bit before the first output, with the margin over R_f stated | B: argue it from R ≥ 2·R_f alone: true for one call, but it skips the absorption of the later inputs. C: none | spec FR-029; research R13 |
| RAT-023 | Is the rationale revised and reviewed again when R rises after the owner design review but before the freeze? | Add: FR-043 adds that any change after the owner design review (R raised, a new finding, a corrected document) makes a new design-document version, the rationale is revised, the review is repeated for the changed parts and recorded in its own row, and the review package is rebuilt | B: freeze with the old rationale and a note: the frozen rationale would not match the frozen R. C: none | spec FR-043, Edge Cases "A distinguisher on more rounds turns up before the freeze", Review & Approval |
| RAT-024 | Is Π⁻¹ written out in the design document at the same level of detail as Π, with its role stated and outside the production interface? | Add: FR-019 requires Π⁻¹ step by step, as Π is (reverse phase order, constants in reverse, each operation's width, signedness and update order), checked against the inverse generated from the operation list. Its roles are stated (R_b, inverse-direction attacks, the exposure within a request), and FR-033 states it is research-only | B: generate Π⁻¹ from the operation list only: the second transcription and feature 006's translators would have no text for it. C: none | spec FR-019, FR-027, FR-033 |
| RAT-025 | Are the inverse tests' inputs defined in the spec by count and source, or only in R13? | Clarify: SC-004 and FR-037 cite R13's counts: the 16 vector inputs and 2¹⁰ development test states, at every supported size and variant, counts that may only rise | B: leave them to R13: SC-004's "test inputs" stays open. C: none | spec FR-037, SC-004 |
| RAT-026 | What does an inverse or bijectivity failure mean for a candidate that is bijective by construction? | Clarify: a failure is first investigated. A recorded tool defect (PRO-029) gets R7's one re-run, inside the budget; otherwise the candidate is rejected as the edge case says, logged with the failure, and counted towards the stop rule. A list not made of R11's two forms is refused before any test | B: reject on any failure, defect or not: one interpreter bug would decide a candidate. C: none | spec Edge Cases "A candidate fails an inverse or bijectivity test"; research R7, R11 |
| RAT-027 | Is "rest on the mapping" defined in a way a reviewer can apply? | Clarify: every security statement in the rationale cites a bound record, an analysis, the mode theorem or a structural proof; the mapping chapter contains no security statement and none cites it; a statement whose only support is the mapping is a finding | B: a reviewer's judgement: not repeatable. C: none | spec FR-030 |
| RAT-028 | Is a check named that would find a security argument resting on the mapping? | Add: two checks. An item of the owner design review, "no security statement rests on the mapping", and a rule of FR-069's test that flags security wording (secure, strength, resist, unpredictable, chaos, Lyapunov, ergodic) inside the mapping chapter | B: the review item only. C: the test only: a pattern cannot judge an argument's support | spec FR-030, FR-069, User Story 5 |
| RAT-029 | Is the positive side of the mapping required: what it does contribute? | Add: FR-030 adds that the mapping chapter states what the mapping contributes (names, the structure of lanes and nodes, each component's place in the metaphor) and what it does not (any security property), answering security.md §8 both ways | B: the negative side only, as now. C: none | spec FR-030 |
| RAT-030 | Are only chaos and Lyapunov-exponent arguments forbidden, or related ones too? | Stricter (wording): FR-030's list reads "chaos, Lyapunov exponents, ergodicity, emergent complexity, biological unpredictability, or battery results offered as evidence of mixing or security", and RAT-028's test flags them | B: chaos and Lyapunov only, as written. C: none | spec FR-030, FR-069 |
| RAT-031 | Are FR-030's list of failed mechanisms and FR-051's lessons table consistent, with the reason S1, S3, S4 and S8–S10 are left out of FR-030? | Clarify: FR-030 adds the reason. FR-030 covers mechanisms of the step function; the others are answered elsewhere: S1 and S3 (the seed bound) by the key and the security target, S4 (no key, nonce or reseed) by the mode, S8 (seed words shared between pressures) by key loading and R4's rotational pairs, S9 and S10 (bijection and cycles) by bijectivity by construction and the generic bound. FR-051's table maps all fifteen | B: add those entries to FR-030 as well: one list in both places, with the mapping chapter covering things that are not mechanisms. C: none | spec FR-030, FR-051 |
| RAT-032 | Is "show how each failed mechanism is replaced" given acceptance criteria? | Add: for each of S2, S5–S7 and S11–S15, the design document names the rule of Π that replaces it and the evidence record (FR-037, FR-039) showing the failure does not recur, for example S13's merging against bijectivity by construction and the exhaustive check, and S12 and S14 against the full-range rule and the bias rule. The final report's lessons table cites the same records | B: a narrative through the mapping: not checkable. C: none | spec FR-030, FR-051 |
| RAT-034 | Is it specified how the rationale marks the kind of each statement, so that a reviewer can tell an argument from an observation? | Add: FR-062 adds that each statement carries one of seven labels (fact, argument, observation, hypothesis, unsuccessful attack, untested, residual risk), by section heading where a section holds one kind and inline otherwise, the label fixed by its support | B: by section only: mixed sections blur. C: inline only: noisy in a long document | spec FR-062 |
| RAT-035 | Is the review status of each AI-drafted argument in the rationale required to be visible? | Add: the provenance log records each AI-drafted section of the rationale with its review status, and the owner design review updates it; the frozen text carries no status, as FR-034 requires | B: status markers inside the rationale: its hash would change at every review. C: per-file status only, as now: a file with one reviewed section looks reviewed | spec FR-064, FR-067; data-model §14 (a `section` field) |

## Analysis

[checklists/analysis.md](./checklists/analysis.md): 88 items. ANA-001, ANA-036, ANA-048, ANA-054,
ANA-059, ANA-060, ANA-062, ANA-068, ANA-069, ANA-073 and ANA-082 are under Conflicts, ANA-033 to
ANA-035 under "Re-run of 2026-11-01", ANA-037 to ANA-045 under "Second re-run of 2026-11-01",
ANA-046, ANA-047, ANA-049 to ANA-053 and ANA-055 to ANA-058 under "Third re-run of 2026-11-01",
ANA-061 and ANA-063 to ANA-067 under "Fourth re-run of 2026-11-02", ANA-070 and ANA-071 under "Fifth
re-run of 2026-11-02", ANA-072 under "Sixth re-run of 2026-11-02", ANA-074 to ANA-076 under "Seventh
re-run of 2026-11-02", ANA-077 to ANA-079 under "Eighth re-run of 2026-11-02", ANA-080 and ANA-081
under "Ninth re-run of 2026-11-02" and ANA-083 to ANA-088 under "Tenth re-run of 2026-11-03"; the
other 31 follow.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| ANA-002 | Is a known result named for every tool whose verdict counts, not only for those FR-039 lists? | Add: one known result per counted tool, each with a validation record (data-model §7). The symmetry check re-finds S7's rotation structure of `phyco-v2`'s constants. The dependency propagation re-finds the hand-derived matrices of a stated small operation list. Exhaustive bijectivity re-finds a known permutation (2³² ones) and a stated map with collisions (fewer). The avalanche test re-finds a planted gap, a stated variant that leaves one lane unmixed. The cost model re-finds R5's hand-counted examples | B: FR-039's list only: the other tools' verdicts would count unvalidated. C: R4's cross-check alone: it covers two of the five tools | spec FR-039, SC-007; research R12, R13; quickstart V1 |
| ANA-003 | Is the acceptance criterion for re-finding a published result defined per validation target? | Clarify: per row of R12's table, the exact published value over the stated rounds. For trail searches that is the weight of the optimal trail, not the trail itself, since several trails can share it; for the S-box tables, every entry; for PRESENT, the stated minimum of active S-boxes over 5 rounds. Any difference is a failed validation | B: the trail itself: may fail on an equally good trail. C: none | research R12; data-model §7 |
| ANA-004 | Is it stated why each validation target is of the same kind as its use, or which further check covers the difference? | Add: R12 gains a "kind" column, plus cross-checks where kinds differ. The ARX search must reproduce the exhaustive box tables at w = 4 and 8 (boxes of 8 and 16 bits) before it counts at w = 16 and 32; the active-S-box counter must reproduce exhaustive trail optima of a χ-like candidate at w = 4. A tool used outside its validated kind writes `not evaluated` | B: the reference targets alone: a search validated on SPECK32's 16-bit words would count on 64-bit boxes unchecked. C: none | research R12; spec FR-039 |
| ANA-005 | Is "older than" in SC-007 defined so that it can be checked after the fact? | Clarify: both. The validation record's `created_utc` is earlier, and every counted record cites the validation record's ID and SHA-256, which could not be cited before that record existed | B: timestamps only: easy to read, easy to get wrong. C: chain order only: validation records are not in a chained log | spec SC-007; data-model §5, §6 (a `validation` field) |
| ANA-006 | Is it specified when a validated tool must be validated again? | Add: after any change to its code (its SHA-256 differs, REP-005), a dependency upgrade, or a use at a word width, operation set or kind of model outside its validated kind; each counted result cites the validation record of the tool's exact hash | B: once per tool, as now: a later edit could run unvalidated. C: before every run: would double the cost of 8 h searches | research R12; spec FR-039; cli contract "Guards" (tool validation) |
| ANA-007 | Is the path after a failed validation specified: fix, attempts, and results computed before the failure? | Add: the defect is recorded with its cause and fixed, and the tool is validated again, on a second known result as well as the first, so a fix cannot be tuned to one value; attempts are not limited, but each is recorded. Results of the failed version never count; results already counted from an earlier validated version stand, since they cite its hash | B: one fix, then the tool is withdrawn: a second bug ends the tool. C: none | spec Edge Cases "A tool fails its validation"; research R12; data-model §7 |
| ANA-008 | What happens if a validation target's published value cannot be obtained? | Clarify: the tool stays unvalidated and its results `not evaluated`; the owner may provide the value with its source (R19). If no trail route is validated, no candidate can establish R_bound, none is eligible, and the feature recommends stopping (stop rule) | B: validate on values recomputed from a primitive's definition, such as PRESENT's S-box tables: weaker, since the tool and the check would share one reading of the definition. C: none | spec FR-039, Edge Cases; research R12, R19 |
| ANA-009 | Do the Plan approval row, the plan's table of six fixed values and research.md agree on which values became protocol at plan approval? | Align: the plan's "Values this plan fixes" gains rows for every value research.md marks protocol, so one list names them all: R6's D4 rule (when, where, score, ties; nothing changes after the first score), R12's fixed tool set and R13's counts (only rise; symmetries only added). The plan approval covered research.md, so this records those values, it does not change them; the approval row itself stays as approved | B: limit protocol to the six rows (looser: R6, R12 and R13 would lose their stricter-only protection). C: none | plan "Values this plan fixes"; research.md opening (a pointer to the table) |
| ANA-010 | Does the eligibility rule say what "passes every structural test" means for the frequency and avalanche measurements, and require R_dist, R_f and R_b at every claimable size? | Clarify: those measurements "pass" when they are conclusive (their controls did not fire and no tool defect is left unresolved), whatever R_dist they give. R_dist, R_f, R_b and R_bound must be established at every claimable size, and inconclusive bias evidence at a size makes the candidate ineligible (R4) | B: treat a firing as a failed test: a firing raises R instead, so B contradicts the round rule. C: none | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Eligibility and selection", FR-037 |
| ANA-011 | Is it stated when a solver-computed bound counts as proven for R_bound? | Clarify: only when the solver proves the result: an optimal or infeasible status with the proven dual bound used, or a search that exhausts its space within its cap; the best solution found is never a bound. A search that times out gives no bound, and its record says `inconclusive` (R7) | B: count the proven dual bound at a time-out as a weaker bound (looser: R7 says a capped search gives no bound). C: none | research R7, R12; spec FR-038 |
| ANA-012 | May a bound established at one claimable size be carried to a larger size by a stated argument, or must it be computed at every size? | Settle: carried only by a proof in the design document that holds for every claimable n, for example that every r-round trail at n×n activates at least as many boxes as at 4×4, checked by computing it at 8×8; otherwise each size needs its own computed bound, as "at that size" reads | B: compute at every size: the strictest reading, and the 16×16 models may not fit 8 h per instance. C: extrapolate without proof (not allowed) | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule", FR-038; research R12 |
| ANA-013 | Is FR-029's "computed exactly" consistent with R13's structural propagation, and is full structural dependence stated as necessary but not sufficient? | Clarify: "computed exactly" means R13's exact structural propagation, which over-approximates actual dependence; full structural dependence is stated as necessary and not sufficient for full diffusion, and R4's avalanche test, with its cross-check, observes it | B: exact functional dependence: out of reach at b ≥ 1,024 bits. C: none | spec FR-029; research R13 |
| ANA-014 | Is the case of more than 128 measured round counts at one size covered? | Settle: the avalanche threshold stays 0.005/(128·S_A) whatever the number of round counts, so the rule never fires less. If more than 128 are measured, which needs R_cur above 128, the stated chance of a false firing becomes 0.005·m/128 per size for m round counts, reported beside the result | B: divide by m above 128: keeps 0.005 per size but fires less, which lowers R (looser). C: none | research R4 |
| ANA-015 | Is the invariant check's verdict defined for a form that holds on most but not all test states? | Stricter: a form that holds on more test states than its chance rate allows (for a w-bit sum or XOR, 2⁻ʷ), by a binomial test at the bias rule's level, 0.005 divided by the number of forms and round counts tested, fails the check as a probabilistic invariant and raises R_dist at that round count | B: forms that hold on every test state only, as now: one holding on 90% would pass. C: none | research R13; spec FR-037 |
| ANA-016 | Is it stated how a firing is reported, given the family-wise rate of chance firings? | Clarify: as a distinguisher at its round count that raises R, with the family-wise chance rate stated beside it (about 21% at R = 32, 23% with the avalanche test), never measured again to remove it; the final report repeats the rate, as the plan's Risks say | B: re-measure a firing on new inputs before it counts: fewer false raises, but running again until passing (Principle IV). C: none | research R4; spec FR-028, Bias rule |
| ANA-017 | Is the selection bias FR-041 requires the final report to state given a form? | Add: the report states the candidates logged and evaluated per family, the evaluations (measurements and searches) behind the frozen one, the bias rule's family-wise chance rates over them, and that every result of the frozen candidate is on development inputs chosen among up to six candidates, so feature 007's held-out evaluation is the unbiased test | B: one sentence that selection bias exists: not measurable. C: none | spec FR-041 |
| ANA-018 | Is r*, the largest common round count, justified as a measure of per-round strength at the production width? | Add: R6's rationale states that r* compares candidates over the same rounds and is not a per-round rate, since trail weight per round is often lower in the first rounds; the D4 record states r*, and the final report names the risk that a family better at r* is worse at R. The score is unchanged | B: change the score, for example to drop the first round (looser or at best neutral, and the D4 rule may not change, R6). C: none | research R6; data-model §8 |
| ANA-019 | Is the outcome "ineligible within budget" reproducible by others, given wall-clock hours on one machine and an engine that may get faster? | Add: each record states the hours projected and used, the measured speed and the engine's code hash (REP-005). Others reproduce the counts and verdicts of finished runs exactly, and an inconclusive outcome as "did not finish within the recorded budget on the machine of R1 with that engine", a fact about the run, not a property of the candidate; the final report says so | B: budgets in counted operations: machine-independent, but a change to R7 that is not clearly stricter. C: none | research R7; data-model §3 |
| ANA-020 | Is it specified how a budget row is shared between the three claimable sizes, so that 4×4 and 8×8 cannot use up the hours 16×16 needs? | Stricter: a run starts only if its projection plus the projections (probes only) of the same kind of run at every larger claimable size fit the hours left in its row; otherwise the engine is made faster first (R7), and counts never change. This reserves the 16×16 hours without fixing a split | B: a fixed split, for example a quarter, a quarter and a half: simple, but the statistics row's cost grows about sixteenfold from 4×4 to 16×16, so a fixed split could starve 16×16. C: first come, first served, as now | research R7; cli contract "Guards" (budget) |
| ANA-021 | Is a budget or cap stated for tool validation runs, the D4 reduced-width searches and the FUT-SEC-8 tool check? | Add: validation runs charge no candidate and have their own cap of 8 h per instance, as a search instance does. The D4 reduced-width searches are trail searches on their candidate and count against its trail row. The FUT-SEC-8 vector check and sweep fall under the demonstration's own budget row | B: no budget outside the candidate rows, as now: a validation run could grow without limit. C: charge validation to the first candidate that uses the tool: penalises whichever comes first | research R7; spec FR-039 |
| ANA-022 | Is "evaluated" defined for the stop rule's count of three candidates per family? | Clarify: from the candidate's first log entry, `proposed`, whatever happens after; a fourth `proposed` entry of a family is refused (data-model §3), so a design cannot be tried and dropped without counting | B: from the first score or first heavy run (looser: a design could be explored and dropped without counting). C: none | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Stop rule", FR-041; data-model §3 |
| ANA-023 | Is the limit of one re-run per kind of evidence stated per candidate and size, and what does a second tool defect in the same kind mean? | Settle (the strictest reading of R7's "once per kind of evidence"): one re-run per kind per candidate, across all sizes. A second recorded defect in that kind leaves the evidence inconclusive, which makes the candidate ineligible; the tool is fixed and validated again before any other candidate uses it | B: one re-run per kind, candidate and size: up to three per kind. C: none | research R4, R7 |
| ANA-024 | Is "agree with the random baseline" quantified for the FUT-SEC-8 match counts, and is the response to a disagreement specified? | Add: per target, the one-word false-match count is compared with a Poisson distribution of mean (2³² − 1)/2³², two-sided at level 0.01, and with the three targets pooled; any false match after two or three words is reported as a deviation. A disagreement is a finding (FR-060), analysed as in ANA-025, and never a reason to sweep again | B: report the counts beside the baseline, as now: "agree" is left to the reader. C: none | spec User Story 1, Pre-registered Design Protocol "FUT-SEC-8 demonstration"; data-model §4 |
| ANA-025 | Is the assumption behind the FUT-SEC-8 baseline stated, with what a deviation caused by `phyco-v2`'s known biases would mean? | Add: the record states the assumption (each candidate's output words uniform and independent of the target's) and that `phyco-v2`'s biases (S12, S14) could make false matches more frequent; a count above the baseline is an observation about `phyco-v2`, it does not change the success criterion, and it is reported as such | B: drop the baseline comparison: loses a check that the tool counts correctly. C: none | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "FUT-SEC-8 demonstration"; data-model §4 |
| ANA-026 | Is the null model stated for every comparison with a baseline? | Clarify: under a random permutation, p = 1/2 for each frequency bit, each avalanche difference bit and each rotational difference bit; for an invariant form, its chance rate on uniform states (2⁻ʷ for a w-bit sum or XOR). The uniform controls test these null models directly | B: leave them implicit: the controls test them, but nothing names them. C: none | research R4, R13 |
| ANA-027 | Are the avalanche test's uniform controls specified as fully as the frequency test's, and is a control run again after a re-run? | Add: the controls' derivation is stated, for example control states from `phycoweb-004/control/state/…` taken in pairs (2i, 2i + 1), beside R4's counts and threshold. A re-run after a tool defect always runs its control again first, with the same counts | B: reuse the first control unless the defect touched it: one run fewer, but the reason must be argued each time. C: none | research R4; data-model §5 |
| ANA-028 | Is it stated what counts as detection power for the bias tool before it is used on a candidate? | Add: the validation record shows that the tool re-finds S14's bias on `phyco-v2` and that, on 100 sets of synthetic binomial counts with a planted bias at R4's 90%-power values (0.0030, 0.0031 and 0.0033 at the three sizes), its firing rate lies within a stated binomial interval around 90%. Synthetic counts cost nothing to draw | B: S14 alone: S14's bias is far larger than R4's sensitivity, so it says little about the smallest bias detected. C: none | spec FR-039; research R4, R12; data-model §7 |
| ANA-029 | Is "from the same generics" defined: which parts of Π may change with w and n, and which may not? | Clarify: the operation list's structure (its operations, their forms and order, and the colour schedule's rule) is the same at every width and size. Only w, n and values derived from them by stated rules change: rotation amounts by the scaling rule, constants by the constant rule, the colour count by n's parity, food-node positions by their rule, c and the key and nonce lengths. Anything else makes a different design (PRO-013) | B: leave it to the design document: two variants could differ in structure and still claim one design. C: none | spec FR-024, FR-032; research R11, R14 |
| ANA-030 | Is it stated which verdicts may rest on reduced variants and which must come from the production width at each claimable size? | Clarify: the D4 score (by its rule) and exhaustive bijectivity (2×2, w = 4) may rest on reduced variants; R_bound, R_f, R_b and the bias evidence come from the production width at each claimable size. A reduced-variant result transfers as evidence about the design, by the same generics, never as a value of the round rule | B: carry reduced-width bounds to w = 32 by a scaling argument: unproven, and the round rule says "at the production width". C: none | spec FR-032, FR-038, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Round rule" |
| ANA-031 | Is the set of reduced variants used as evidence fixed before their results, and must the reason for each be logged? | Stricter: the reduced variants a candidate uses as evidence (widths, sizes, round counts, capacities) are logged in its `parameters` before its first tool run; one added later is logged with its reason before its first run, and every result on it is reported, used or not | B: logged at first use: a variant could be added after a result was seen. C: none | spec FR-032, FR-041; data-model §3 |
| ANA-032 | Are the reduced variants' bounds and statuses kept apart from claimable sizes in the size profile? | Clarify: data-model §10 already holds `status` (`claimable` or `research`), c and c/2, and the checks at s = c/2; FR-009 and FR-032 add that a research row's bound is labelled "research variant, no claim" and is never compared with s = 128 or reported as a strength | B: research variants in a separate file: harder to compare sizes. C: none | spec FR-009, FR-032; data-model §10 |

## Protocol

[checklists/protocol.md](./checklists/protocol.md): 80 items. PRO-001, PRO-026, PRO-041, PRO-044,
PRO-046, PRO-058, PRO-059, PRO-068, PRO-071, PRO-076, PRO-078 and PRO-079 are under Conflicts,
PRO-036 to PRO-040 under "Re-run of 2026-11-01", PRO-042, PRO-043, PRO-045 and PRO-047 to PRO-049
under "Second re-run of 2026-11-01", PRO-050 to PRO-057 under "Third re-run of 2026-11-01", PRO-060
under "Fourth re-run of 2026-11-02", PRO-061 and PRO-062 under "Fifth re-run of 2026-11-02", PRO-063
to PRO-066 under "Sixth re-run of 2026-11-02", PRO-067, PRO-069 and PRO-070 under "Seventh re-run of
2026-11-02", PRO-072 under "Eighth re-run of 2026-11-02", PRO-073 under "Ninth re-run of 2026-11-02"
and PRO-074, PRO-075, PRO-077 and PRO-080 under "Tenth re-run of 2026-11-03"; the other 33 follow.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| PRO-002 | Is the scope of the development-only rule stated consistently, and does it cover the work after the freeze? | Clarify: FR-040 holds for the whole feature, the freeze and everything after it included (the re-check, the L1 gate, the final report): no held-out key is created or used in feature 004 at any time; the strategy note's "before the freeze" is the weaker phrasing and is not used | B: "before the freeze" only (looser: after it, held-out keys could appear before feature 007's protocol exists). C: none | spec FR-040 |
| PRO-003 | Is R3's "a measurement with N inputs uses i = 0…N − 1" required for every use of development inputs? | Stricter: for every use: vectors (keys and nonces 0 and 1, R8), constructed states from development keys, the D4 scoring, tool validation and every test. A use of N inputs takes i = 0…N − 1 of the kind it names, declared up front, and the loader refuses any index outside it; a constructed input takes its index from its rule (PRO-001) | B: measurements only, as R3 says: other uses could pick indices. C: none | research R3, R8; data-model §2 |
| PRO-004 | Is each namespace's use restricted, and is the namespace each evidence script may load fixed per kind of evidence? | Stricter: `control` serves only uniform controls and `dev` is never a control; each evidence kind's namespace is fixed in code, not chosen on the command line, and the loader refuses `control` for any kind but a control | B: the namespace as an option, recorded: a run could use the other namespace and still record it truthfully. C: none | research R3; data-model §2; cli contract "Guards" |
| PRO-005 | Must every tool of this feature that reads feature 002's or 003's seed sets refuse their held-out sets (exit 2)? | Add: FR-040 adds that every such tool loads only feature 002's `dev` set, with `allow=("dev",)` as `fut_sec8.py` does, and exits 2 on any held-out set, as security.md §11.3 requires of development tools; a test checks every such call | B: rely on each call passing the allow-list, untested. C: none | spec FR-040; cli contract "Guards"; `tests/test_protocol_004.py` |
| PRO-006 | How are runs that are not evidence classed and recorded, and may their partial outputs be looked at? | Add: a record kind `projection` for R7's 60 s projections and any timing probe, with its inputs (the first inputs of the run it projects); its outputs other than timing are discarded unread, and the record says so. Its time counts against the row of the run it projects | B: probes unrecorded: a probe's outputs could be seen before the run. C: probes on inputs outside every measurement's range: nothing seen early, but the speed measured is of other inputs | research R4, R7; data-model §1; spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Development inputs" |
| PRO-007 | May statistical batteries be run on candidates in this feature, and if so, may their results influence the choice? | Settle (stricter): no battery (SP 800-22, PractRand, TestU01 or any other) runs on a candidate or on `phyco-v3` in this feature. The frequency and avalanche tests of the bias rule are the only statistics; the statistical programme belongs to feature 007 under its own protocol | B: batteries allowed on development keys, logged and never used in the choice: their results could still shape decisions informally. C: battery failures as distinguishers for R_dist: stricter for R, but no validated mapping from battery p-values to rounds | spec FR-040, FR-049 |
| PRO-008 | Is "before its freeze" resolved to one freeze, and which one must held-out keys postdate? | Settle (stricter): the later of the two, `phyco-v3`'s design freeze and feature 007's protocol freeze, which in practice is feature 007's. No held-out key can then influence the design or the statistics' protocol | B: the design freeze: keys could exist while feature 007's protocol is still open. C: none | spec FR-040; carried to feature 007 |
| PRO-009 | Is "cannot be computed before its freeze" defined well enough to be carried as a checkable requirement? | Clarify: held-out keys are derived from data that does not exist until that freeze, for example SHA-256 over feature 007's protocol freeze record with a public string; feature 007 states the derivation, and a test re-derives it. Varying the record to steer the keys would change the frozen protocol, visibly in its history | B: add a public value published after the freeze, such as a randomness beacon: stops any steering, but needs an outside source and its own approval. C: none | spec FR-040; carried to feature 007 |
| PRO-010 | Is it specified where the requirement is carried to feature 007, so that its transfer can be checked at the final result review? | Add: two places: an open item of the final report quoting FR-040's requirement in PRO-009's checkable form, and security.md §22 row 5a.4's text | B: the final report only: security.md's roadmap would not show it. C: none | spec FR-040, FR-052; security.md §22 row 5a.4 |
| PRO-011 | Does FR-041 require every variant to be logged, or only every candidate design? | Align: FR-041 adds that every variant ID used, every round count measured and every fix to an operation list is logged under its candidate, as data-model §3 already says; the stop rule counts candidates, and the log covers variants | B: candidates in FR-041 and variants in data-model §3 only: the spec would not require it. C: none | spec FR-041 |
| PRO-012 | Must a design enter the log before any tool runs on it? | Stricter: a design's `proposed` entry, with its operation list's SHA-256, comes before any tool run; every tool refuses an operation list whose hash has no `proposed` or `correction` entry (exit 2) | B: logged at first evidence, as SC-011's "that produced evidence" reads (looser: a design could be tried and dropped unseen). C: none | spec FR-041, Stop rule; cli contract "Guards"; data-model §3 |
| PRO-013 | Is "a distinct design of Π" given a criterion that decides whether a change makes a new candidate or a variant? | Settle: a new candidate if the change touches the operation list's structure: the nonlinear component, the round structure (its operations, their forms or order), the colour schedule, or the rule that generates the constants or the rotation amounts. A variant or fix if it changes only values the candidate's logged rules derive (w, n, R, c), or corrects a value to what those rules give. When in doubt, a new candidate | B: whatever the log entry says: several designs could share one entry to stay within three per family. C: any change is a new candidate: a typo fix would use one up | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Stop rule"; data-model §3 |
| PRO-014 | Is a record required for every run started, aborted runs and runs hit by a tool defect included, with the log citing each one? | Stricter: every run started on a candidate or variant writes a record, even if aborted (`inconclusive` or `blocked`, with the reason); a run later found hit by a tool defect keeps its record, with the defect noted; the log cites every record, used or not, as security.md §11.3's "report everything" asks | B: records for finished runs only: an aborted run that looked bad could vanish. C: none | spec FR-036, FR-041; data-model §1, §3 |
| PRO-015 | Is User Story 5's scenario 2 consistent with FR-041, and does it test the converse? | Align: scenario 2 reads "every candidate design is in the log, whether or not it produced evidence, and every record a log entry cites exists with the cited SHA-256", and SC-011 says "every candidate design" | B: keep "that produced evidence": a design dropped before any evidence stays outside the test. C: none | spec User Story 5 scenario 2, SC-011 |
| PRO-016 | Is the mapping from the selected candidate's ID and variant IDs to `phyco-v3` and its variant IDs required to be recorded? | Add: the `selected` log entry and the freeze record hold the map from `cand-…/n…-w…-R…-c…` to `phyco-v3/…`, with the operation list's SHA-256; the model's tests check that `phyco-v3` reproduces the candidate's outputs through the map | B: rename the candidate's records: rewrites evidence, and records are never overwritten. C: none | data-model §3, §13; spec User Story 5 scenario 2 |
| PRO-017 | Are superseded operation lists, and those of candidates not selected, kept unchanged with their SHA-256 in a committed inventory? | Add: before an operation list changes, its version is copied unchanged to `research/004/candidates/<id>/superseded/<sha256>.py`, and a committed inventory lists every hash and path, so every `oplist_sha256` in the log stays checkable; the re-check verifies the inventory | B: rely on git history: a version never committed would be lost, and only the owner commits. C: none | spec FR-041; data-model §3; plan "Project Structure" |
| PRO-018 | Is a recovery path specified for a broken chain in either hash-chained log? | Add: only with the owner's recorded go-ahead. The broken file is kept unchanged with its SHA-256; a repair script writes a `repair` entry with the break's position, its cause and what was restored; entries after the break count only where the records they cite corroborate them; the final report states the repair | B: no repair: the log ends and the feature stops, so one disk error would end it. C: none | research R16; data-model §3, §14; cli contract (a repair command) |
| PRO-019 | Is it specified where and when the owner's tie-breaking choice is recorded, with the evidence the owner saw? | Add: in its own row of the Review & Approval table ("D4 tie" or "Selection tie"), with the date, the choice and the SHA-256 of the record the owner saw (`d4/tie.json` or `selection.json`), and in that record; the choice is made only after the record exists and never changes | B: in the log only: the owner's choice would sit in a file the agent writes. C: none | spec Review & Approval, Pre-registered Design Protocol "Nonlinearity choice rule", "Eligibility and selection"; data-model §8, §9 |
| PRO-020 | Are the reasons a candidate may be dropped limited to those the rules name? | Stricter: `rejected` and `ineligible` only for the rules' reasons: a failed inverse or bijectivity test (RAT-026), a failed eligibility item (FR-018 to FR-031, FR-037, R_bound not established, inconclusive evidence) or a spent budget. A candidate may be withdrawn before its first tool run for any recorded reason and still counts towards the stop rule; the log writer refuses any other decision | B: any recorded reason, as data-model §3 allows: a choice outside the rules could decide the freeze. C: none | spec FR-041, Edge Cases; data-model §3 |
| PRO-021 | Is "stricter" given a direction for each protocol rule that has none? | Add: FR-044 gains a table of directions, each the one that makes the conclusion more conservative. Eligibility: items only added. Selection and the D4 rule: no change. Tool validation: known results and re-validation triggers only added. The FUT-SEC-8 demonstration: no change. Round rule: Δ only rises, sources of R_dist only added. Stop rule: budgets and the candidate limit only fall | B: one general sentence ("more conservative"): every case argued when it arises. C: none | spec FR-044, Pre-registered Design Protocol (a "Stricter means" line per rule) |
| PRO-022 | Is the bias rule's stricter direction stated, given that a lower threshold fires less and that adding a set lowers 0.005/S? | Settle: a change to the bias rule is stricter only if it can add firings and never remove one. Every firing recorded under the rule then in force stands, and a larger N, N_a or F, or a new set, is tested under its new S or S_A in addition. Stricter thus means that R can only rise | B: recompute every verdict at the new S (looser: an earlier firing could disappear and R fall). C: freeze the bias rule's inputs entirely: simplest, but a weakness seen in a new set could not be measured | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Bias rule", FR-044; plan "Values this plan fixes"; research R4 |
| PRO-023 | Does FR-044 require the owner's approval for a stricter change, as security.md §11.3 does? | Stricter: FR-044 adds "with the owner's recorded approval"; the agent may propose a stricter change but not make it | B: the agent may make one and record it: quicker, but a "stricter" change can still steer a result (PRO-022). C: none | spec FR-044 |
| PRO-024 | Is one place named where every protocol change is recorded, with its date, reason, approver and the results it precedes? | Add: a protocol change log, `research/004/protocol-changes.jsonl`, chained and locked like the candidate log: per entry the date, the rule, old and new text, the reason, the direction (PRO-021), the approver and the last record ID that existed before it, so "before any result it affects" can be checked; the spec's Clarifications point to it | B: the spec's Clarifications: readable, but the spec's hash changes with every entry (REP-006). C: the candidate log: mixes candidates with rules | spec FR-044; data-model (a new section); research R16 |
