| PRO-025 | Is it specified how a stricter change made after some candidates have results applies? | Add: to every candidate, those already evaluated included; each one's verdicts are recomputed under the new rule from saved outputs where possible (PRO-026), and the results known at the time of the change are recorded in the change log (PRO-024) | B: later candidates only: two candidates judged by different rules. C: none | spec FR-044, FR-041, Eligibility and selection |
| PRO-027 | Does FR-044 say whether settling a case the protocol leaves undefined is an allowed change? | Settle: FR-044 adds that settling an undefined case is allowed only before any result it affects exists, with the owner's approval, recorded in the change log, and never looser than any reading of the existing text. The plan's rules for an exact D4 tie, for no scored family and for a representative found ineligible first were settled that way, at plan approval | B: forbid settlements: an undefined case would block the feature when it arises. C: allow them at any time with a reason: a case could be settled after its result is known | spec FR-044 |
| PRO-028 | Do the plan's "no change after the first score" and the Plan approval row's "may only become stricter" agree on what may change between the two moments? | Align: both hold. The parts the D4 score and its tie-breaks use (R5's LUT table, L, the carry chains per round, and R6's rule) have no stricter direction, so in effect they do not change from plan approval; "no change after the first score" is the later of two locks. FR-026's reported figures (cost per output bit, implied throughput) decide nothing, so a correction to them, such as HW-003's, is recorded in the change log | B: allow any change until the first score (looser than FR-044). C: none | plan "Values this plan fixes"; research R5, R6 |
| PRO-029 | Is "tool defect" defined by a criterion that must be recorded before a re-run? | Add: a tool defect is one of: a failed validation or re-validation; a disagreement between avalanche counts and dependency matrices (R4); a disagreement between two evaluators of one operation list (scalar, batch, C, model); a crash or an undocumented exit status; or a bug in the tool's code, shown by the faulty line and a test that fails before the fix and passes after. It is recorded with that evidence before any re-run; a disliked result is none of these | B: the analyst's recorded judgement: a disliked result could be called a defect. C: none | spec Edge Cases; research R4, R7; data-model §1 `exclusions_and_reruns` |
| PRO-030 | Does the freeze record pin the logs' states, the selected operation list, `research/004/ir.py` and the evidence behind R by hash? | Add: the freeze record adds the last `seq` and line SHA-256 of the candidate log, the provenance log and the protocol change log; the selected operation list and `research/004/ir.py` with its tools; every evidence record behind R by SHA-256 as well as by ID; and the verdict scripts (PRO-033) | B: record IDs only, as now: a record could change while its ID stays. C: none | spec FR-043; data-model §13; cli contract `design_freeze.py` |
| PRO-031 | Is the order of the freeze steps specified, and is it stated what counts as dirty? | Clarify: (1) the owner commits the feature files; (2) `design_freeze.py create` runs with `git_dirty` false and writes the record as `draft`; (3) the owner's approval is written into it; (4) the owner commits the record; (5) `validate` exits 0. Dirty means a tracked file modified or an untracked file not ignored; ignored files (the locks, local-only outputs) do not count, and local-only outputs are in the record by SHA-256 anyway | B: count ignored files as dirty: the locks and local-only outputs would always make the tree dirty. C: none | spec FR-043; data-model §13; cli contract `design_freeze.py`; research R16 |
| PRO-032 | Which frozen file is authoritative if the frozen design document and the frozen model disagree, and does a correction need a new model ID? | Settle: the frozen model and vectors are the expected values (FR-056), and the design document is the specification. A disagreement found after the freeze is a finding; the frozen files stay unchanged, and the correction is a new model ID with its own design-document version, model, vectors and freeze (FR-028), even where the intended design is the same. If it bears on any claim, it is recorded at once (FR-051) | B: correct the model under the same ID: edits a frozen file, which FR-043 forbids. C: none | spec FR-028, FR-043, FR-056, Edge Cases |
| PRO-033 | Are the scripts that compute verdicts and the development-input module required to stay unchanged after the freeze, or be hashed in its record? | Add: both. The freeze record hashes `evidence_v3.py`, `round_rule.py`, `d4_choice.py`, `recheck_004.py`, `protocol/devinputs_v3.py`, `protocol/evidence.py` and `research/004/ir.py` with its tools, and after the freeze they do not change; a fix is a new file, so FR-065's re-check runs the code that produced the verdicts | B: hash only and allow changes: the re-check would have to recover old versions from git. C: none | spec FR-043, FR-065; data-model §13 |
| PRO-034 | Is the outcome of a declined owner design review specified, with how each is logged? | Add: a decline states its reasons, and ends in one of: a revision of the design document or rationale (same candidate, new version, review repeated); a fix that leaves the design unchanged, logged under the candidate; a further candidate within the stop rule, which the rules must then select; or a stop. Each is logged with the reasons. The review cannot put another eligible candidate in the rules' place (FR-041), and throughput is reported, never a reason to decline | B: let the owner pick another eligible candidate: a choice outside the rules. C: none | spec FR-041, FR-043, Review & Approval |
| PRO-035 | Is it stated what may follow a stop, and must a later feature count this feature's candidates in its selection bias? | Add: after a stop, a later feature may evaluate further candidates only under its own pre-registered protocol and new model IDs, and it counts this feature's candidates, results and stop in its selection bias (FR-041); the stop is reported with what was reached (security.md §21, option C) | B: a stop ends this design line for good: strict, and it closes options the owner may want later. C: none | spec Pre-registered Design Protocol "Stop rule", FR-044, FR-052 |

## Hardware readiness

[checklists/hardware.md](./checklists/hardware.md): 95 items. HW-003, HW-009, HW-010, HW-018,
HW-033, HW-043, HW-044, HW-071, HW-073 and HW-083 are under Conflicts, HW-038 to HW-042 under
"Re-run of 2026-11-01", HW-045 to HW-057 under "Second re-run of 2026-11-01", HW-058 to HW-070 under
"Third re-run of 2026-11-01", HW-072 under "Fourth re-run of 2026-11-02", HW-074 under "Fifth re-run
of 2026-11-02", HW-075 to HW-078 under "Sixth re-run of 2026-11-02", HW-079 to HW-082, HW-084 and
HW-085 under "Seventh re-run of 2026-11-02", HW-086 to HW-090 under "Eighth re-run of 2026-11-02",
HW-091 and HW-092 under "Ninth re-run of 2026-11-02" and HW-093 to HW-095 under "Tenth re-run of
2026-11-03"; the other 32 follow.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| HW-001 | Is FR-025's "at most one or two serial 32-bit carry chains" resolved to one number per stage, with a timing argument, and do a subtraction or a counter increment count? | Clarify: "at most two serial w-bit carry chains per pipeline stage", R5's reading. A subtraction counts as one chain, as an addition does. A public counter held as 32-bit words counts where it sits on a stage's path, and not when it is registered on a path of its own. The design document gives the stage's estimated delay against the 5 ns of 200 MHz on the XC7A100T −1, with its source, as an estimate that feature 008's post-route timing checks | B: one chain per stage: stricter for eligibility, and it may fail candidates with two additions in one phase. C: none | spec FR-025, re-decided feature-002 FR-002; research R5 |
| HW-002 | Is a limit or an estimate stated for the LUT levels on a stage's path besides its carry chains? | Add: R5's count adds the LUT levels on each stage's longest path (⌈(k − 1)/5⌉ for a k-input bitwise function, one more per dependent operation), reported per candidate beside the chain count, and the design document states each stage's estimated depth against the 200 MHz target; reported, not an eligibility rule | B: a limit, for example four LUT levels per stage: stricter, but chosen without evidence. C: none | research R5; spec FR-025, FR-026 |
| HW-004 | Is the 2w-bit ARX-box built from w-bit operations only, and how do FR-025's "32-bit" chains read at widths 4, 8 and 16? | Clarify: FR-022 adds that the box uses only w-bit operations on the node's two lanes, never a 2w-bit addition, so every datapath word stays at w bits (re-decided FR-002); FR-025 reads "w-bit carry chains", so the same count applies at every width | B: allow 2w-bit additions inside the box: 64-bit chains at w = 32, against re-decided FR-002 and the 200 MHz target. C: none | spec FR-022, FR-025 |
| HW-005 | Is the output interface's width stated per size, and is the implied throughput the lesser of r/R and what the interface carries? | Add: the design document states the output interface's width per size, for example 32 bits per cycle at every size, as the vector files list words, or a stated wider bus. FR-026's implied throughput is the lesser of r/(P·R) bits per cycle (HW-003) and the interface's bits per cycle, and both are reported | B: report r/R only: at 16×16 and R = 32 that is 252 bits per cycle with P = 2, about eight times what a 32-bit interface carries. C: none | spec FR-026; research R5; data-model §10 |
| HW-006 | Must FR-026's implied throughput include the forget call after every request and the cost of instantiate, for a stated request size? | Add: the implied throughput is stated for stated request sizes (the 2¹⁹-bit cap, and 4,096 words as in the long-run digest), counting each request's output calls plus its forget call, with instantiate's calls spread over a stated number of requests per instantiation; the rate before forget calls is reported beside it as an upper bound | B: before forget calls only, as R5 quotes: overstates the rate, most of all for small requests. C: none | spec FR-026; research R5; data-model §10 |
| HW-007 | Does the spec require an estimate of which claimable sizes are expected to fit the XC7A100T-1? | Add: from the cost model, the design document estimates LUTs and flip-flops per claimable size (LUTs per node and round × n² for the stages built, plus a stated allowance for the trees and ROMs, and 2w·n² state flip-flops plus pipeline registers) against the device's 63,400 LUTs and 126,800 flip-flops, and names the largest size expected to fit, as an estimate feature 008 checks | B: leave fitting to feature 008: a size could be claimable and never fit the board. C: none | spec FR-026; research R5 |
| HW-008 | Are the absorption and output trees specified per size, with their latency in FR-025 and FR-026 and the timing justification required as a stated argument? | Add: the design document specifies per supported size each tree's fan-in per stage, depth, registered stages and latency in cycles. The latency enters FR-025's reset-to-first-output latency and FR-026's throughput, and the justification Principle III asks for is an argument in the design document (a register at every stage, a fan-in bounded by a stated number), referenced from the plan's Complexity Tracking | B: leave the trees to feature 006: the latency and throughput stated here would omit them. C: none | spec FR-018, FR-025, FR-026; plan "Complexity Tracking" |
| HW-011 | Is it stated which input lengths are public, and is the reseed input's length public even when its content is secret? | Clarify: FR-020 adds that the lengths of the personalization string, the reseed input and every request are public, as are the call type and the variant, and a cycle count may depend on them and on nothing else; a reseed input's length is public even when its content is secret, which the threat model states | B: pad every input to its maximum, so no count depends on any length: constant counts per call type, at the cost of absorbing the maximum every time. C: none | spec FR-013, FR-016, FR-020 |
| HW-012 | Is "constant time" defined for each implementation, so that no artifact calls the model constant time? | Clarify: in hardware, a cycle count fixed by public values (HW-013); in feature 006's C, no branch or memory index that depends on secret data; in the Python model, branch-free on data as a discipline only, never called constant time, since Python integers are not. The claim test flags "constant time" beside the model | B: one definition, by operation count, for all three: the model would seem to inherit a timing property it lacks. C: none | spec FR-020, FR-069; model contract "Module rules" |
| HW-013 | Is the cycle count of every call stated as a function of public values only? | Add: the design document gives, per call (instantiate, generate of n bits with its forget, reseed of m bytes, a refused request, zeroization, the start-up self-test), a formula in cycles from the variant and the public lengths only, counting cycles with ready high (HW-031); feature 008 compares measured counts with them (FUT-HW-5) | B: state that counts are constant, without formulas: feature 008 would have nothing to compare with. C: none | spec FR-020, FR-025, FR-059 |
| HW-014 | Is "the whole state" to be zeroized enumerated, with every trigger and its latency? | Add: FR-025 lists the registers: the lanes, every pipeline register, the registers of both trees, the key-loading buffer, the held key (HW-010), the round, phase and limit counters (to their reset values) and the output register. Triggers: reset, an explicit zeroize, a failed self-test, an error path (SEC-038) and a per-key limit reached, each with its latency in cycles, output-valid low throughout | B: "the whole state" left to the design document: a register could be missed. C: none | spec FR-025, FR-059 |
| HW-015 | Is the hardware counterpart of the model's "zeroize is final" specified? | Add: FR-025 adds that in hardware a reset or zeroization returns the device to a state that accepts a new key, since a device cannot end itself as an object can; the per-key limits and counters then restart, so across resets they are usage rules for the caller, as across objects in the model, and the design document lists them with the other usage rules | B: a device that zeroization ends until it is configured again: matches the model, but awkward on the board. C: none | spec FR-007, FR-025; model contract "Generator" (a note) |
| HW-016 | Is it specified that no output, and no output-valid signal, can follow a zeroization or a reset until an instantiate has completed? | Add: after reset or zeroization, output-valid stays low and the output register holds zeros until an instantiate completes; a generate request before then is refused with an error signal, as the model raises `CallSequenceError` | B: leave it to feature 006: the model's refusal would have no stated hardware counterpart. C: none | spec FR-025, FR-058 |
| HW-017 | Does "no read-back path" cover the FPGA's configuration readback and any debug core, or only the RTL's ports? | Add: FR-025 covers the RTL's ports and, on the board, the configuration readback and any debug core (an integrated logic analyser, JTAG user registers). Feature 008 builds the production bitstream with readback security set and no debug core, and records it; this feature states the requirement and carries it | B: RTL ports only: the board's readback would expose key and state. C: none | spec FR-025; carried to feature 008 |
| HW-019 | Is the start-up self-test's scope defined, and is its expected output given for each claimable size? | Add: FR-059 adds that the self-test runs instantiate with key and nonce 0 from the frozen vectors, then a generate long enough that every node, lane, constant ROM entry of the calls run, both trees and the counters take part, then a reseed if the design has one. It compares with the expected output stated per claimable size from the frozen vectors, then zeroizes; on failure it zeroizes and blocks every call | B: one call of Π on a fixed input: misses the mode's calls and the trees. C: none | spec FR-018, FR-059 |
| HW-020 | Is T4 given a disposition for `phyco-v3` beyond constant time, with glitch behaviour specified or deferred and its risk stated, and does instantiate end with a forget call? | Add: FR-008 adopts T4: constant time by construction here; power, EM, fault injection and the behaviour on reset and clock glitches deferred to feature 008, with the risk stated (a state read by a probe or fault in mid-request exposes that request back to the last forget call). The mode ends instantiate with a forget call, in the form the theorem covers (R10), so no compromise reaches the key (SEC-017) | B: exclude T4 with a reason: the board feature would inherit no stated risk. C: none | spec FR-008, FR-013, FR-025; research R9, R10 |
| HW-021 | Is an odd n the same core under FUT-GRID-1, and how does the generic n select the schedule? | Clarify: FR-018 adds that the colour schedule is a function of n's parity, computed at elaboration from one stated rule (two colours for even n, three for odd n), so an odd n is the same core with another generic value; without such a rule, odd sizes are unsupported with the reason (R14) | B: even sizes only, 3×3 listed as unsupported. C: none | spec FR-018, Edge Cases "Odd grid sizes"; research R14 |
| HW-022 | Is it stated how the constant tables for every n and w reach the RTL? | Add: the frozen `phycoweb/constants_v3.py` holds one table per supported (n, w), and the constant script also writes each as JSON beside the vectors, frozen with them; feature 006 turns them into packages and the RTL selects one by its generics, while the LFSR stays in the script. A size without a table cannot be built until one is generated by the same script | B: evaluate the LFSR at elaboration: covers every n, but it is a second implementation of the rule, to be checked against the script's tables. C: none | spec FR-018, FR-023; research R15; data-model §13 |
| HW-023 | Is a "channel" defined, so that FR-018's "at most four channels per node" can be judged on each operation list? | Clarify: a channel is one w-bit word passed from a node to a neighbour in one phase; a node receives at most four channels per phase, for example one from each neighbour or two from each of two, counted from the operation list, the linear layer between boxes included | B: per direction, whatever the width: a node could take every lane of every neighbour. C: none | spec FR-018; research R11 |
| HW-024 | Is "joins neighbouring nodes of the layout" defined for the folded torus, with the longest link for odd n as well as even? | Clarify: FR-018 states the folding rule and its bound: logical neighbours, wrap-around included, sit at most two positions apart in each dimension of the layout, for odd n as well as even, with at most one node between a link's ends; the design document gives the rule and the longest link per size | B: a plain torus with its long wrap links registered like a tree: needs a Principle III justification of its own. C: none | spec FR-018 |
| HW-025 | Are the number of food nodes per size and the ROM depth at each stated, so that the constant storage can be estimated? | Add: per supported size, the number of food nodes, the ROM depth at each (R entries per call type, times the number of distinct constant streams) and the word width, with the total bits reported; the cost model still does not score the ROMs (R5) | B: leave ROM sizes to feature 006. C: none | spec FR-018, FR-023; research R5, R15 |
| HW-026 | Is the key-loading interface generic in the capacity c, and is the choice stated against FUT-GRID-2? | Add: FR-025 states that the key-loading interface is generic in c: a c-bit key and a c/2-bit nonce (R14), 256 and 128 bits at every claimable size. Keyless variants have none, and FUT-GRID-2's "defined for every supported size" is met through the keyed variant each size has (SEC-011) | B: the 256-bit key only: key loading undefined at 2×2, against FUT-GRID-2. C: none | spec FR-025; research R14 |
| HW-027 | Do FR-027 and FR-055 agree on what the design document writes for a sign-free operation? | Clarify: FR-027 adds that every state word, counter and intermediate is declared `unsigned` and every operation of FR-055 is written as an unsigned operation; a signed type, resize or comparison appears only where FR-055 justifies it, with vectors at its sign boundary | B: let the translator choose, since the bits agree: a later resize or comparison could then extend a sign. C: none | spec FR-027, FR-055 |
| HW-028 | Must every shift and rotation amount lie in 1 to w − 1 at every width, after the scaling rule as well? | Stricter: FR-024 adds that every amount, after scaling, lies in 1 to w − 1 at every supported width, and the constant script and the operation list refuse 0 and w; where scaling would give 0 or w at w = 4, the rule names the allowed amount to use instead | B: allow 0 as the identity, written without shifts: correct, but the design's structure then changes at w = 4. C: none | spec FR-024, FR-027, FR-055; research R14, R15 |
| HW-029 | Are the bit numbering, the index direction of a word and the direction of each rotation stated? | Clarify: FR-027 adds that bit 0 is the least significant, words are `unsigned(w − 1 downto 0)`, rotl(x, k) moves bit i to bit (i + k) mod w, and the lane and node order are stated; FR-015's serialization gives the byte and bit order of output | B: follow `numeric_std` habits without stating them: a translator writing `0 to w − 1` would reverse every bit. C: none | spec FR-015, FR-027 |
| HW-030 | Does FR-027's list cover Principle I's truncation and rounding rule? | Add: FR-027 adds the truncation rules: the last output block of a request whose length is not a multiple of r keeps its first bits in FR-015's serialization order and discards the rest, and the padding rule fixes partial input blocks (FR-014). Nothing rounds, since nothing divides | B: requests in whole rate blocks only: no truncation, but callers must buffer. C: none | spec FR-014, FR-015, FR-027; model contract "Generator" (output) |
| HW-031 | Is the flow control of the key-loading, absorption and output interfaces specified? | Add: valid and ready on each interface; while the consumer stalls, the core holds its state and output and resumes the same sequence. A call's cycle count then depends on stalls, which the consumer sets in public, and never on secret data, and HW-013's formulas count cycles with ready high | B: valid only, the consumer always ready: simpler, but a slow consumer loses output. C: none | spec FR-025; carried to features 006 and 008 |
| HW-032 | Is the latency "from reset to the first output" stated in cycles from a defined event, including the self-test and its zeroization? | Clarify: two figures, both in cycles and fixed by public values: from reset released to self-test done and state zeroized (ready for a key), and from the last key word accepted to the first output valid, with the trees' latency included | B: one figure from reset with the key ready: it then depends on how fast the host supplies the key. C: none | spec FR-025, FR-059 |
| HW-034 | Are FUT-GRID-3's output-taps clause and FUT-GRID-4's disruption clause given a disposition? | Add: the table gains them. FUT-GRID-3's "output taps MUST NOT depend only on a local neighbourhood" is met by specification, since the output is the rate part after a full call of Π (FR-015) and every output bit depends on every input bit after R_f rounds (FR-029). FUT-GRID-4's "disruption behaviour MUST be re-established at each size" is not applicable, since D5 removes the disruption (FR-031) | B: leave both to the existing rows: the clauses stay unaccounted for in SC-019's coverage. C: none | spec Security Policy traceability (FUT-GRID-3, FUT-GRID-4), Input coverage |
| HW-035 | Is FUT-GRID-5's "validate an intermediate size before 16×16" carried to features 006 and 008? | Add: the final report and FR-056's mapping carry it: the RTL and the board validate 4×4 before 8×8 and 16×16, with R7's order of sizes as the pattern | B: leave it to those features: the order would be decided again there. C: none | spec FR-036, FR-052, FR-056; security.md §22 rows 5a.3, 5a.5 |
| HW-036 | Is each FUT-HW item given a design-level acceptance criterion, so that "Designed for" and "Specified" can be checked? | Add: per row. FUT-HW-1: every operation's width, signedness and shift kind stated (FR-027), a sign-free datapath (FR-055), and 0 DSP blocks as no multiplication in the operation set, with an instruction to feature 006 that synthesis must not map additions to DSP slices. FUT-HW-2: chain and level counts per stage (HW-001, HW-002) and the size estimate (HW-007). FUT-HW-3: the implied throughput (HW-005, HW-006). FUT-HW-4: vectors at stage granularity (HW-037). FUT-HW-5: the cycle formulas (HW-013), the port list with no read-back (HW-017) and the zeroization list (HW-014) | B: keep "Designed for" and "Specified" without criteria: nothing to check. C: none | spec Security Policy traceability (FUT-HW-1 to FUT-HW-5) |
| HW-037 | Is the trace granularity of the vectors matched to the pipeline stage, so that an RTL mismatch can be located to one stage? | Add: R8 and data-model §11 trace the state after every phase of every round for the first four inputs, as `pi_trace` already returns, instead of after every round; vector lengths only grow, so R8 allows it | B: per round, as now: a mismatch is located only to a round. C: none | research R8; data-model §11; spec FR-036 |

## Reproducibility

[checklists/reproducibility.md](./checklists/reproducibility.md): 101 items. REP-003, REP-015,
REP-029, REP-035 to REP-037, REP-049, REP-056, REP-058, REP-070, REP-074, REP-082, REP-093, REP-097
and REP-100 are under Conflicts, REP-028 and REP-030 to REP-034 under "Re-run of 2026-11-01",
REP-038 to REP-048 and REP-050 to REP-055 under "Second re-run of 2026-11-01", REP-057 and REP-059
to REP-069 under "Third re-run of 2026-11-01", REP-071 to REP-073 under "Fourth re-run of
2026-11-02", REP-075 under "Fifth re-run of 2026-11-02", REP-076 to REP-081 under "Sixth re-run of
2026-11-02", REP-083 to REP-091 under "Seventh re-run of 2026-11-02", REP-092 and REP-094 to REP-096
under "Eighth re-run of 2026-11-02", REP-098 under "Ninth re-run of 2026-11-02" and REP-099 and
REP-101 under "Tenth re-run of 2026-11-03"; the other 25 follow.

| ID | Question | Recommended (A) | Other options | Changes |
|----|----------|-----------------|---------------|---------|
| REP-001 | Is "reproducible" defined per kind of record, with the fields excluded from a comparison and the environment named? | Add: FR-053 defines it. Deterministic kinds (vectors, constants, dependency matrices, counts, digests) reproduce byte for byte; searches and solver runs reproduce their values and verdicts. The comparison excludes `record_id`, `created_utc`, timings, `platform`, `versions` where the environment differs, `git_head` and `git_dirty`, and the environment is either of R1's two, as recorded | B: byte-identical everything: impossible for timestamps. C: none | spec FR-053, User Story 4 Independent Test; data-model §1 |
| REP-002 | Is User Story 4's "re-run every evidence script with its recorded command" scoped against the cost of the heavy runs? | Align: User Story 4's independent test becomes FR-065's re-check, which recomputes every verdict from saved outputs, as the routine test; full re-runs are listed per kind with their cost, light kinds always and heavy kinds on request, one at a time | B: re-run everything: up to about 16.5 days of runs (R7). C: none | spec User Story 4 Independent Test, FR-065; research R16 |
| REP-004 | Is a recorded command required to be runnable as written? | Add: FR-061 adds that the command names the interpreter by its full path (the Windows Python, or `~/.venvs/phycoweb/bin/python` in WSL), records the working directory, the repository root, and uses paths relative to it; a test re-runs one light command of each kind exactly as recorded | B: the script and its arguments only: a reader must guess the interpreter. C: none | spec FR-053, FR-061; data-model §1 |
| REP-005 | Does every evidence record hold the SHA-256 of the code it ran, and is a self-written tool's "version" defined as that hash? | Add: data-model §1 gains `code_sha256`: the SHA-256 of every code file the run used (the script, the `protocol/` modules, the candidate's operation list, `research/004/ir.py` and its tools, a C source and its binary). A self-written tool's version (FR-039) is its hash, and FR-053's "model hash" is the operation list's before selection and `model_v3.py`'s after. With `git_dirty` often true, the hashes, not `git_head`, identify the code | B: `git_head` only: an uncommitted change would be invisible. C: none | spec FR-039, FR-053, FR-061; data-model §1, §4 |
| REP-006 | Is it stated how a record's `spec_sha256` is resolved to a version of the spec, which may be uncommitted when a run starts? | Add: the first time a record cites a spec hash, `protocol/evidence.py` stores a copy of that spec as `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/spec-versions/<sha256>.md`, committed with the records, so every `spec_sha256` names content that is kept, whether or not a commit holds it | B: refuse any run while the spec has uncommitted changes: strict, but only the owner commits, so runs would wait on each commit. C: drop `spec_sha256`: loses the link to the rules in force | data-model §1; research R16 |
| REP-007 | Is each environment stated in full, so that a third party can rebuild the two environments? | Add: one environment record per platform, `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/environment-windows.json` and `environment-wsl.json`, written at setup and on any change: Windows build, WSL distribution and version, Python, gcc, make and the full `pip freeze`; each evidence record cites its environment record's SHA-256 | B: versions per record, as now: a package outside the list would go unrecorded. C: none | research R1, R18; data-model §1 |
| REP-008 | Is a check required, before an evidence script runs, that the interpreter and packages match R1 and `requirements.txt`? | Add: `protocol/evidence.py` checks at start that the interpreter and installed packages match R1 and the pins for that platform, and exits 2 on any mismatch; a changed environment needs R1 updated first, with the owner's approval | B: record the mismatch in `limitations` and run anyway: a record could rest on an environment no one approved. C: none | research R1, R18; cli contract "Guards shared by every script" |
| REP-009 | Are FR-035's "supported Python versions" listed? | Clarify: FR-035 lists them. The model supports Python 3.10 and 3.14, the two tested; 3.8, 3.9 and 3.11 to 3.13 are "expected, not tested", since the model uses only the standard library and passes the 3.8 grammar check. The analysis tools need 3.10 or later and are tested on 3.10.11 and 3.14.4, and a comment in `requirements.txt` says that its markers admit 3.11 to 3.13 untested | B: call 3.8 and later supported, as the constitution asks: untested versions would be called supported. C: test 3.11 to 3.13 as well: needs more environments and downloads | spec FR-035; research R17, R18 |
| REP-010 | Are Hypothesis tests required to run derandomized or with a recorded seed, with the example database disabled? | Add: FR-058 adds that Hypothesis runs under a settings profile registered by the test package with `derandomize=True` and `database=None`, so every run tries the same examples and a failure replays by running again | B: random with a recorded seed: more examples over many runs, but a run's examples depend on its seed. C: none | spec FR-058; research R17 |
| REP-011 | Are the solver options that decide a bound fixed and recorded, and is a floating-point bound rounded in the conservative direction? | Add: R12 fixes and records the options: a MIP gap of 0, the proven dual bound used and never the best solution, the feasibility and integrality tolerances, the thread count and the 8 h limit. A floating-point bound becomes data-model §6's rational by rounding in the direction that weakens it, a weight rounded down, never up | B: use the solver's value as given: a tolerance could make a bound look slightly stronger than proven. C: an exact rational solver: a download | research R12; data-model §6; spec FR-038 |
| REP-012 | Is it stated where each downloaded file's SHA-256 and size are recorded, so that a reader can tell which copy a value came from? | Add: a committed downloads manifest, `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/downloads.json`, one entry per file of R19 with its name, source, size, SHA-256, download date and local path; a validation record's `source` cites the manifest entry with the paper's table and value | B: add the hashes to R19's table: research.md changes after approval just to hold records. C: in each validation record only: a paper used twice carries its hash twice | research R19; data-model §7; plan "Phases and gates" (setup) |
| REP-013 | Is every local-only output required to be deterministic, with the cost of its regenerating command recorded? | Add: R16 adds that a local-only output must come from a deterministic command, so its committed SHA-256 can be checked by regenerating it, and the record states that command's cost (heavy or not, hours projected). An output that is not deterministic, such as a solver log with timings, is never local-only: it is committed, compressed, or its verdict rests on a committed deterministic summary | B: allow non-deterministic local-only outputs: no one could check their hashes. C: none | research R16; spec Edge Cases "Large evidence records" |
| REP-014 | Is it stated which verdicts the re-check recomputes from committed per-round summaries alone, and which need a local-only output first? | Add: every verdict is recomputable on a fresh clone from committed files, so the committed per-round summaries must suffice, for example R_f from the per-round "every output bit depends on every input bit" flag. Local-only outputs serve only to re-derive a committed summary, which `recheck_004.py` also does when they are present, and it lists both checks per kind | B: regenerate local-only outputs on a fresh clone: heavy runs for a re-check. C: none | spec FR-065, SC-017; research R16; cli contract `recheck_004.py` |
| REP-016 | Is it stated that the model reads no clock, random source, environment variable or platform property? | Add: the model contract's module rules add it, and the import test bans `time`, `random`, `os`, `sys`, `platform` and `secrets` in the model, as it bans numpy; the generators read the clock and platform only for the sidecar record of REP-015, never for content | B: rely on the vectors agreeing across platforms: shows agreement, not the rule. C: none | spec FR-035; model contract "Module rules"; research R17 |
| REP-017 | Is a canonical order required for every list in a record or vector file, and are settings that could change an output recorded? | Add: the data-model introduction requires every list sorted by a stated key (fired bits by round count, direction, set and bit; matches by candidate value; IDs lexicographically; symmetries in a stated enumeration order), so nothing depends on set iteration, hash randomization, thread completion or worker count. Records hold `PYTHONHASHSEED` if set and the worker and thread counts, and a test runs one light kind with two worker counts and compares the outputs | B: sort only where a tool happens to produce unordered output: a new tool could miss it. C: none | spec FR-053; data-model introduction, §1, §4, §5 |
| REP-018 | Is agreement required between the numpy batch interpreter, any C evaluator, the scalar interpreter and the model, on stated reference outputs at every width? | Add: before any evidence run on a candidate, the scalar and batch interpreters agree on reference outputs for R8's 16 Π inputs at every variant the candidate uses, computed by the scalar interpreter and committed with the candidate. A C evaluator must agree on the same outputs before its first run, not on the `phyco-v3` vectors, which exist only after selection; after selection the model agrees with all of them on the committed vectors | B: check only after selection: evidence could rest on an evaluator that disagrees with the scalar one. C: none | research R4, R7, R11; spec FR-035 |
| REP-019 | Is the bias rule's "computed exactly" defined, given that scipy's binomial tails are floating-point and may differ between versions near 0.005/S? | Add: R4 defines it as integer critical counts derived once per (N, S) by exact integer arithmetic over the binomial distribution (sums of binomial coefficients, compared with the threshold as a rational) and recorded in a committed table. The verdict compares each count with its critical values, so scipy's tails decide nothing; scipy still computes the p-values reported | B: scipy's tails at a stated version: the two environments' versions may disagree near the threshold. C: a normal approximation: not exact | research R4; plan "Technical Context"; data-model §5 |
| REP-020 | Is the byte string hashed for the long-run digest defined, so that another implementation can reproduce it? | Clarify: R8 and data-model §11 define it as the SHA-256 of the first 2²⁰ output words in FR-015's canonical serialization, 4·2²⁰ raw bytes, not of the `%08X` text lines, so feature 006's C stream and the hardware export can reproduce it from their own output | B: the `%08X` lines with LF endings: ties the digest to a text format and its line endings. C: none | research R8; data-model §11; spec FR-015 |
| REP-021 | Is the textual format of every vector file and record fully specified, so that another implementation can write byte-identical files? | Add: the data-model introduction specifies hex in upper case, zero-padded to the word's width; lanes in the design document's order; the header's grammar (`# key=value` fields in a fixed order, separated by single spaces); JSON with sorted keys, two-space indentation, `ensure_ascii=False`, rationals as "p/q" strings, floats only in reported statistics and written with `repr`; a final newline; LF only | B: equal values only: two implementations could write different bytes with the same content, and their hashes would differ. C: none | data-model introduction, §11; spec FR-036 |
| REP-022 | Does SC-009's "on both Windows and Linux" cover the constants and the constructed-state vectors? | Align: SC-009 covers every generated file; the quickstart's M1 and M3 run in WSL as well, like M2, and SC-006 and SC-016 cite SC-009's two platforms | B: the vectors only: the constants and the constructed states, from the same model, stay unchecked on Linux. C: none | spec SC-006, SC-009, SC-016; quickstart M1, M3 |
| REP-023 | Is it stated per kind of file whether its SHA-256 is taken over the raw bytes or over LF-normalized content, with the choice recorded beside each hash? | Settle: two kinds, each recorded beside its hash as `raw` or `lf-normalized`. Every new file is hashed over its raw bytes and is pinned to LF or `binary`, so its raw and normalized content agree on every checkout. An existing `text=auto` file is hashed over its LF-normalized content (REP-025). The CRLF-pinned freeze records of features 002 and 003 keep their recorded hashes over CRLF bytes | B: LF-normalized content everywhere: robust, but no hash then matches `sha256sum` of the file. C: raw bytes everywhere: an existing `text=auto` file's hash would differ between a Windows and a Linux checkout | spec FR-053; research R16, R18; data-model §1, §13 |
| REP-024 | Does R18's list of pins cover every kind of new file whose hash is recorded, raw tool output and gzip-compressed records included? | Add: `.gitattributes` gains `reports/004-crypto-architecture-v3/** text eol=lf`, which covers solver logs, CSV and text outputs, followed by `*.gz binary`, so that compressed records are never treated as text; later lines take precedence, and `*.bin binary` already exists | B: pin each new kind as it appears: one could be missed until REP-026's check finds it. C: none | `.gitattributes`; research R18; spec FR-053 |
| REP-025 | Does FR-053's pin rule cover an existing file that a new manifest or record hashes, such as `security.md` and `README.md`? | Add: a new record or the review package hashes an existing `text=auto` file over its LF-normalized content and says so (REP-023), so the hash is the same on every checkout, and no existing file's pin changes | B: pin `security.md` and `README.md` to LF: their working-tree bytes on this Windows checkout change from CRLF to LF at the next checkout. C: hash their raw bytes: the hash then differs between platforms | spec FR-053, FR-063; data-model §16; research R18 |
| REP-026 | Is a repeatable check required that every file whose hash is recorded has an explicit line-ending rule in `.gitattributes`? | Add: a test lists every path whose hash a committed record, manifest or freeze record holds and checks with `git check-attr text eol` that each has an explicit rule or `binary`, or that its record marks the hash `lf-normalized`; it replaces the one-off check noted in `.gitattributes` on 2026-10-31 | B: repeat the one-off check before each freeze: a missing pin could slip in between. C: none | spec FR-053; research R18 |
| REP-027 | Is the encoding of every new text file stated beside its line ending? | Add: FR-053 adds UTF-8 without a byte-order mark for every new text file; scripts write with `encoding="utf-8"` and `newline="\n"`; REP-026's check also looks for a byte-order mark; and no hashed file is written by Windows PowerShell 5.1, whose `Out-File -Encoding utf8` adds one | B: an encoding for JSON only, as now. C: none | spec FR-053; data-model introduction; research R18 |

## Rows that change the protocol

PRO-047 places each round's protocol changes here: one list per round, naming every row whose option
altered the text of a pre-registered rule, or would alter it in a round not yet answered, whatever
its kind, under each rule it alters. The protocol change log's first entries, written when it is
created, are these rows, one entry per row, in round order (data-model §18); a round answered after
the log exists adds its entries the usual way, proposed by the agent and approved by the owner (spec
FR-044).

A row is listed under every rule whose text its Changes cell names or its
applied change altered (PRO-058): the rule in the spec's Pre-registered Design
Protocol, the research section that holds its values, a requirement the rule
takes as its content, as each heading below names them, or the plan's table "Values this plan
fixes". Every Stricter or Settle row is listed, since its kind says that it changes a rule; REP-023
is the one that names none of these texts, and it is listed under the hashes of research R16, which
it settles. This reading is the inclusive one: a listed row that changes no rule costs one log
entry, while a row left out would be a protocol change outside the log. Each list names its round's
two commits by hash, the commit before the round's first applied row and the commit that completed
its alignment, so that a round's alignment commits fall inside its pair, the first two rounds' pairs
being the commits that hold their rows with their other edits, named as such (PRO-061). Before the
tasks approval the agent makes the comparison with `research/004/place_check.py`, committed for it,
standard library only, which reads the named commits with `git show` and writes
`research/004/place-check.json`, over each round's pair and over every span from the plan-approval
commit, 65bcbf2, outside the pairs, up to its `compared_to`, each rule's text compared in each place
its heading names, delimited as spec FR-044 states; a round with no alignment commit takes as its
second commit the commit of its last applied row, named as such. The results written beside each
list restate that file. A place changed by an edit that no row made, in a round's commits or between
two rounds, is recorded beside a list with its commit, for the owner to confirm at the tasks
approval as an entry of its own or as no change of rule, with the reason, in the Review & Approval
row "Place check", which names the file's SHA-256 (PRO-063): an edit inside a round's pair is
recorded beside that round's list, and its entry follows the round's row entries; an edit between
two rounds is recorded beside the list of the round before it, as ad2c195's is beside the re-run's,
and its entry follows that round's entries, so that the log keeps the order of the commits
(PRO-064). Before the log is created, its writer, `scripts/protocol_change.py create`, whose `check`
makes the same comparison, reads that row at HEAD and exits 2, writing nothing, while a place whose
text changed has no row of that round listed under its rule and no such confirmation, while its
result up to `compared_to` differs from the file the row names, or while a place changed after
`compared_to`, until the comparison is made again over the later commits and its file confirmed in
"Place check <k>", read with the highest k; the comparison's result is recorded with the first
entries (data-model §18).

PRO-067 gives two headings the places of the texts their directions govern, each delimited as spec
FR-044 states: "Records and their hashes" research R1 and R16, data-model's introduction and §1,
§13, §14, §16 and §17, the cli contract and the notes under the Review & Approval table, and
"Protocol changes and their directions" spec FR-044, data-model §18 and the cli contract's
`protocol_change.py`, so that a change made in those texts alone is a change of that rule. The
comparison reads them over every pair and span, the eighth list is written by them, and beside each
earlier list stand, added on 2026-11-02, the rows of its round whose Changes cells name one of those
places or whose applied change altered one and that the list does not yet list under that heading:
for the first two rounds by the Changes cells alone, since each applied its rows in one commit with
other edits, whose message names no row; from the second re-run on also by the messages of the
round's commits, which name, row by row, the places each row's change altered; and from the sixth
re-run on also by the rows' tags in those places' text.

Each heading takes the stricter direction that spec FR-044's list states for it, and each listed
row's rules take `stricter` in that direction or `settled`, with its readings, a rule whose
direction is "no change" taking `settled` whatever the row's kind (data-model §18; PRO-066). The
directions, heading by heading: Development inputs, no change; FUT-SEC-8 demonstration, no change;
Bias rule, only a change that can add firings and never remove one; Round rule, Δ only rises and
sources of R_dist are only added; Nonlinearity choice rule (D4), no change; Tool validation, known
results and re-validation triggers only added; Structural checks, counts only rise, and symmetries,
constant relations, forms and sources of R_dist only added; Eligibility and selection, eligibility
items only added and selection no change; Stop rule, budgets and the candidate limit only fall;
Constant rule, fixed with its refused list before the first candidate is scored and no change after,
the refused list only growing; Security-target parameters and output limits, where s, the key and
nonce lengths and c only rise, ε only falls, N only rises, and u, every output limit and the
per-request cap only fall; Values this plan fixes, as the plan's table states for each; Vector
lengths, lengths only grow; Records and their hashes, what a record holds, which files are hashed
and kept, and the writers' and guards' checks and refusals only added, no file's hash kind changing
once a record holds it; and Protocol changes and their directions, an entry's fields, the comparison
and the guard's refusals only added, no rule's direction loosened.

The first three lists were written on 2026-11-01, after their rounds were answered, by their rows'
Changes cells, the reading before PRO-058, so they await the owner's confirmation, asked for at the
tasks approval. A later round's list is written with its rows, before the answer that approves it,
and if the answer chooses another option for a row, the list follows the option chosen before the
change log takes it. The fourth list, the third re-run's, was written with its rows on 2026-11-01,
and the owner's answer to that round, "all recommended" on 2026-11-02, approved it as written
(FR-044). The fifth list, the fourth re-run's, was written with its rows on 2026-11-02, and the
owner's answer to that round, given in advance on 2026-11-02, approved it as written (FR-044). The
sixth list, the fifth re-run's, was written with its rows on 2026-11-02, and the owner's answer to
that round, given in advance on 2026-11-02, approved it as written (FR-044). The seventh list, the
sixth re-run's, was written with its rows on 2026-11-02, and the owner's answer to that round, given
in advance on 2026-11-02, approved it as written (FR-044). The eighth list, the seventh re-run's,
was written with its rows on 2026-11-02, under the places that PRO-067's recommendation gives two of
its headings, and the owner's answer to that round, given in advance on 2026-11-02, approved it as
written (FR-044). The ninth list, the eighth re-run's, was written with its rows on 2026-11-02,
under the same places, and the owner's answer to that round, given in advance on 2026-11-02,
approved it as written (FR-044). The tenth list, the ninth re-run's, was written with its rows on
2026-11-03, under the same places, and the owner's answer to that round, given in advance on
2026-11-02, approved it as written (FR-044). The eleventh list, the tenth re-run's, was written with
its rows on 2026-11-03, under the same places, and the owner's answer to that
round, given in advance on 2026-11-02, approved it as written (FR-044). Each
listed row enters the log with its rules' texts before and after its round, as
data-model §18 states (PRO-051). The log is created, and no evidence run starts, only after the
owner has confirmed every list; a row the owner takes out of a list is recorded here, beside that
list, with the owner's reason, that it changed no rule.

Commits 7f42e03 and b9d9740, the eleventh report's remediation and a follow-up to it, on the
owner's instruction of 2026-11-03 to make and commit the edits for its issues, changed research

tool's binary among them, and made each run exit 2 unless the SHA-256 that the binary prints with
`--source-sha256` equals its source's entry. It also settled the old words "a binary is rebuilt
from committed source, its hash reported only" as a reproduction that runs the committed binary
and rebuilds none, which was looser than their other reading, a reproduction that rebuilds the
binary and runs the rebuild, for a binary stale against its make file or a repository header, so
the last sentence of that commit's message, that each change is stricter under every reading, did
not hold for it. Commit b9d9740 keeps both readings: a reproduction runs the committed binary,
rebuilds each C tool's binary from that commit's source in a second worktree, reports the
rebuild's SHA-256 only and, where that differs from the committed binary's, runs the rebuild too,
the outcome `match` only when both runs match. Each commit is recorded here for the owner to
confirm at the tasks approval as an entry of its own or as no change of rule, with the reason; the
agent's reading is that the two together are stricter under both readings of the old text, and
that they set no reading aside.

## After the answer

The options chosen for the 29 rows of the re-run were written into the documents their Changes cells
name, among them spec.md, plan.md, research.md, data-model.md, the two contracts, quickstart.md and
security.md (version 2.12), each change with its reason. Changes to the protocol are recorded before
any result exists, as FR-044 requires; none exists yet. The six checklists were then re-read over
the complete documents and the repository, and that second re-run found 71 more items.

under which option A holds together. SEC-085 gives each line its own `claim_level`, from the models

option A's "every input interface's ready low" would refuse the zeroize that option A requires.

code, restating option A's "in every state". SEC-091 reads `phyco-v3`'s bound as the least of its
